### 19. Handoff: SchwingNet, CTSpinoPelvic1K and the imaging programme

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/handoff-schwingnet-ctspinopelvic1k-and-the-imaging-programme-rbcdmf`; account: second account; link id: RBCDMF1YwL93MmeJTkv3oa; docs, handoff; date: 2026-10-08 (Byline "Oct 8, 2026 . @[the owner]"; the body repeats "Where things stand on 2026-10-08" and "every result found on d...); confidentiality: mixed.
- Question: What must a new student or a fresh Claude project know about SchwingNet, CTSpinoPelvic1K, OpenSpineToolkit, Beethoven and the data platform: where every result lives, how it is regenerated, which numbers are leaked or superseded, what each manuscript still needs, and the rules that bind the work?
- Summary: The shareable summary of the handoff written into spinesurg-ct-nnunet/HANDOFF/ on 8 October 2026. It names the one question (naming a vertebral level from what the surgeon has in the room, without counting from C2 or a registered scan), the four projects and where each lives, and the five pieces of the SchwingNet model with what each trained on: a YOLO11m-pose body detector on 12,709 films, a YOLO11m vertebra and sacrum detector on 3,094 real frames, the allspine_v9 ResNet-18 level encoder with 27 classes, a BiGRU recurrent head, and five C-ArmLSpine fold models. It flags a defect to decide on (the fold banks still hold VinDr and FluoroCap crops against the directive), that real C-arm data is barely used, and the results catalog's headline numbers (97.4% of bodies named on a whole lateral frame, 97.0% from the count alone, 80.5% held-out-device recall, a comparator at 97.6% that cannot be exceeded). It lists the regeneration recipes, the manuscripts and their states (paper 1 held pending technology transfer, NeurIPS 3474 accepted as a poster, CNS 2026 accepted), the hold-out rule of 2026-09-23 with checks CHK-1 to CHK-9, the two historical leaks (NHANES II twice, CTSpinoPelvic1K twice) that make allspine v2, v6, v7, anchor_drr and vindrps record only, the ten rules, a student's first week, and what context was exported. The results catalog wins over any other file or memory note.
- Key claims:
  - (measured) 97.4% of bodies are named on a whole lateral frame and 74.8% with one body in view; the count alone names 97.0% of anchored bodies and the coupling adds 1.2 points.
  - (measured) Held-out-device detection recall is 80.5% and the same-device plateau about 84%; renders raise film naming 6 to 22 points at 5 to 25 persons.
  - (measured) There are 0 verified real-frame transitional test persons against 35 needed.
  - (inferred) The LevelCheck comparator at 97.6% cannot be exceeded at any reachable n; the open gate scored names only because of a path bug.
  - (measured) The 99.02% and 99% selective-accuracy numbers come from the leaked allspine_v2 bank and are record only.
  - (measured) NHANES II entered the banks twice, so 48 of 54 validation persons of the older banks were also in training; CTSpinoPelvic1K entered under two names, putting 301 of 801 persons on both sides.
  - (measured) The C-ArmLSpine fold models behind the fine-tuned numbers still train on 2,632 VinDr crops and 2,100 FluoroCap crops per fold bank, contradicting the directive that FluoroCap is out of training.
  - (measured) Of COBRA2026's 692 fetched cases only 145 Elekta cases are in any frame bank and 0 of 225 Varian; nothing after 2026-09-27 changed the banks or models.
  - (measured) 92 of 97 transitional validation and test persons sit in the current encoder's training lineage, so no transitional number on it is held out.
  - (measured) The one-labeller COBRA transitional screen is not trusted: precision 10 of 24 on L6 and 0 of 52 on T13; the registry holds L6 146 and T13 14.
  - (measured) The power argument: 35 error-free at-risk persons for the 90% bound, 22 carriers for 85% sensitivity, 19 for paper 2's H1; every planning value assumed 95 to 99% that no measurement supports.
  - (measured) CTSpinoPelvic1K: 802 records; T12 against L1 AUC 0.990; v11 sacral slope 36.6 against Veilleux 36.5.

### 20. Vertebra Detector Field Report

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/vertebra-detector-field-report-1jljc6`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: 1jLJC6zVJpcSw1VCCFbJPR; page, field-report; date: undated (No header date, run stamp or 'as of' line; the only years named are the NHANES II film years ('NHANES II film, 1976-8...); confidentiality: schwingnet-method.
- Question: How well does a single-class vertebra detector, trained on a union of partially-labelled public spine-radiograph datasets, locate every vertebral body and its four corners when measured per source rather than pooled?
- Summary: A stage-1 field report on a class-agnostic vertebra detector (YOLO11m-pose, one class, four corner keypoints, 1024 px, 111 epochs on one H200) trained on three credential-free sources (NHANES II film, Mendeley 716 standing DR, a partial BUU-LSPINE download): 958 images, 6073 instances, 727 patients with patient-disjoint splits. It reports box mAP@50 0.980 and corner mAP@50 0.922 on validation, AP@50 0.957 and PCK@0.2 91.7% on a 558-instance held-out test, and 6.8 ms inference per image, broken out per source and per acquisition. The single-class design is justified by the confounding of level identity with dataset of origin; level naming is deferred to a second head not yet started. The page states plainly that the ignore-region loss mask is written but not wired in, that every test image comes from a source the model trained on, that no cross-site or intraoperative data is present, and that VinDr-SpineXR, IEEE DataPort, CSXA, the full BUU, CM Spine and AASCE are blocked on credentials or transfer limits. Two parsing bugs caught by validators (BUU step-2 edge pairing, Roboflow duplicate hashes) are recorded, and the reproduce commands are given.
- Key claims:
  - (measured) The detector reaches box mAP@50 0.980 and corner mAP@50 0.922 on validation, AP@50 0.957 and PCK@0.2 91.7% on the held-out test, at 6.8 ms per image.
  - (inferred) Training per-level classes on a union of partially-labelled datasets confounds level identity with dataset of origin, so the detector carries one class and leaves level to a later head.
  - (measured) The headline numbers are in-distribution: the ignore-region loss mask is written but the trained model used cropping only, and the union-with-ignore ablation has not been run.
  - (measured) Every test image comes from a source the model also trained on; nothing is cross-site.
  - (measured) Corner mAP@50-95 reaches 0.901, which the report values over the box number because it says the corners land tightly.
  - (measured) Two parsing bugs were caught by validators: BUU edge lines pair step-2 rather than consecutively, and Roboflow exports duplicated 107 instances across splits.
  - (inferred) Horizontal flip is disabled because on a lateral radiograph it swaps the anterior/posterior meaning of the corner slots and the keypoint head fails to converge.
  - (inferred) The 0.861 (BUU) versus 0.970 (NHANES) gap is not yet a measurement because BUU contributes only 15 test instances.
  - (inferred) Recall is not reported globally because on partially-labelled sources a missed vertebra may be one the dataset never annotated; detections are scored only inside the trusted-background region.
  - (unverified) The intended external hold-outs (VinDr-SpineXR, IEEE DataPort's 236 films) are blocked on credentials, so no genuinely external test set exists yet.
  - (measured) CSXA's 4,963 images are behind a SciDB browser session and four candidate API endpoints all returned errors; its absence is the biggest gap in the corpus.
  - (inferred) The 0.957 AP@50 demonstrates the pipeline is correct end to end but is not evidence of cross-site generalization.
- Numbers (42 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 111 epochs: Training length of the stage-1 detector run
  - 0.980 box mAP@50: Validation box mAP@50 at the end of training
  - 0.922 corner mAP@50: Validation corner (keypoint) mAP@50
  - 0.957 AP@50, n = 558 instances: Held-out test AP@50, all groups
  - 91.7 % PCK@0.2, n = 558 instances: Held-out test corner accuracy at 0.2 of body height
  - 0.768 PCK@0.1, n = 558 instances: Held-out test corner accuracy at 0.1 of body height, all groups
  - 6.8 ms per image: Inference time per image
  - 3217 vertebra instances: NHANES II film corpus contribution, lateral, annotated levels
  - 2666 vertebra instances: Mendeley 716 standing DR corpus contribution, lateral, level unknown
  - 190 vertebra instances: BUU-LSPINE standing DR corpus contribution, AP + lateral, partial download
- Datasets: NHANES II film (1976-80), lateral, per-level masks, Mendeley 716 standing DR (Roboflow export, no level names), BUU-LSPINE standing DR (partial download, AP + lateral), VinDr-SpineXR (not obtained; PhysioNet credentialed), IEEE DataPort 236 lateral full-spine films (not obtained; subscription login), CSXA (not obtained; 4,963 images behind SciDB), CM Spine (not obtained; only public intraoperative source), AASCE via SpineWeb (not obtained; thoracic and AP full-spine); tools: YOLO11m-pose (ultralytics), AdamW, src/ignore.py (ignore-region loss mask), src/build_dataset, src/export_yolo, src/train (fliplr=0.0, mosaic=0.0), src/run_eval, results.csv and metrics.json (page generated from them), Roboflow export, CVAT (planned annotation programme), rclone (suggested for BUU), NVIDIA H200; models: YOLO11m-pose, one class, four corner keypoints, 20.9 M parameters (runs/pose/runs/detect_v1/weights/best.pt).
- Decisions:
  - The detector carries one class; anatomical level is left to a second head sharing the backbone, trained later and measured separately.: Per-level classes on a union of partially-labelled datasets confound level identity with the dataset it came from.
  - Horizontal flip augmentation is disabled.: On a lateral radiograph a flip is anatomically impossible and swaps the anterior/posterior meaning of the corner slots, so the keypoint head fails to converge.
  - Recall is not reported globally; detections are scored only inside the trusted-background region.: On partially-labelled sources a missed vertebra may be one the dataset never annotated.
  - PCK is normalised by each vertebra's own body height, never by image diagonal.: Image diagonal would mean something different on a cervical crop than on a full-spine film.
  - Results are reported per source and per acquisition, never pooled.: A pooled number would hide the generalization behaviour worth measuring.
  - Splits are patient-disjoint.: BUU pairs an AP and a lateral per patient and NHANES participants have both a cervical and a lumbar film.
  - No level names were invented for the Mendeley 716 export.: The Roboflow export carries no level names.
  - The level head is not started until detection is stable.: The spec is explicit that detection must be stable first.
- Open questions: The union-with-ignore ablation, the experiment that justifies the method, has not been run. / Whether the BUU versus NHANES gap (0.861 vs 0.970) holds once BUU has more than 15 test instances. / Cross-site generalization is unmeasured; every test image comes from a training source. / Access to VinDr-SpineXR (PhysioNet credentialing), IEEE DataPort, CSXA (SciDB), the full BUU, CM Spine and AASCE. / The level head, per-acquisition augmentation for C-arm, and the annotation programme (CVAT, honeypots, adjudication) are not started. / The reproduce command says --epochs 120 while the run reports 111 epochs.
- Stale markers: Not yet in this run (ignore-region mask) / the union-with-ignore ablation ... has not been run / Level head - not started / Partial download (BUU, 77 of ~7,200 files) / Blocked on credentials, not on work / four candidate API endpoints all returned errors (CSXA) / the ultralytics loss patch is not wired in
- Related: Counting From The Sacrum (same-topic); SchwingNet Figures (same-topic); Spine X-ray Data Handoff (same-topic); State of the literature: datasets held, missing, size and power (same-topic); SchwingNet Data Registry (same-topic)
- Coverage: Three embedded prediction images (lumbar lateral Mendeley, lumbar AP BUU, lumbar lateral same patient BUU) could not be read; only their captions and alt text were. The training-curve chart survives only as axis labels. No instruction-like text was found.

### 21. Naming Vertebrae Without an Anchor

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/naming-vertebrae-without-an-anchor-3dfzmh`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: 3DFZMHt4oAnrDVHwxdZnyf; page, report; date: 2026-09-17 (Byline under the title: "Detroit Medical Center / Wayne State University . September 17, 2026"; the page also carries...); confidentiality: schwingnet-method.
- Question: Can a registration-free classifier name vertebral levels on a limited-field radiograph that holds no anatomic anchor, and does a short sweep of overlapping exposures recover what a single exposure cannot?
- Summary: A research page, styled as a manuscript in preparation for Radiology: Artificial Intelligence, that tests a feasibility report's claim that level naming in the anchor-free isolated stack (its State C) is mathematically underdetermined. A ResNet-18 crop encoder with a bidirectional GRU head over 2-9 vertebrae, trained on banks v2 through v6 of CT renders and real films, named ordinary spines at 97.2% and anomalous spines at 87.2% in the label-defined isolated stack on real film, and 91.8% ordinary versus 66.0% anomalous on interior windows whose pixels hold neither end of the stack, against a 0% placement-blind baseline. The predicted failure mechanism is real but bounded (83% of errors are one-level shifts) and the report's geometric substitutes did not transfer to real film (the normal-L5 Wigh band held 5.1% of ordinary films). A sweep of overlapping C-arm frames linked from a landmark and decoded as one chain raised two-vertebra frames from 69.6% to 95.1% and thoracic frames from 13.4% to 97.1-99.3% on renders, with two bodies of overlap as the optimum. The page records its retractions (an earlier decoder claim was a tautology of the code; the 20-film 87% was six points optimistic, v6 gives 81.3% on 4,026 windows and 49.9% on anomalous interior windows), its weak spots (L6 under 50%, never seen on a photograph), the data-quality interventions, every network tested, a prior-art table and the reproduction commands.
- Key claims:
  - (measured) The anchor-free isolated stack is not underdetermined: on real film it carries level information with a measured, anomaly-dependent cost.
  - (measured) On interior windows whose pixels exclude both ends of the stack, ordinary lumbar naming is 91.8% against a 0% placement-blind baseline.
  - (measured) The predicted failure mechanism (an integer offset between five- and six-lumbar readings) is real but bounded: most errors are one-level shifts.
  - (inferred) L6 is named correctly less than half the time because the model has never seen a photograph of a six-lumbar spine; it is a data problem, not a loss-function problem.
  - (measured) The report's geometric detectors do not transfer to real film; only sacral slope separates six-lumbar spines.
  - (measured) A sweep of overlapping frames from a landmark, linked into one chain, recovers the single-exposure limitation without a preoperative CT.
  - (measured) Two bodies of overlap between exposures is the optimum; the protocol, not the network, buys the last four points.
  - (measured) Decoding the chain as one sequence against four alphabets with the landmark declared is the lever, worth 9.7 points on identical features.
  - (measured) Training on 76,042 decoder-pseudolabelled crops (bank v5) cut anomalous accuracy while leaving ordinary accuracy unchanged.
  - (measured) The 20-film 87% figure was optimistic; v6, with all 144 anomalous BUU studies in the test partition, gives 81.3%, and on interior windows an anomalous body is named at a coin toss.
  - (measured) The earlier claim that the decoder chose a variant template only on films not reaching the junction was a tautology of the page's own code and is retracted.
  - (inferred) No published method chains consecutive frames from a landmark to name what lies between them.
- Numbers (80 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 87.2 %, n = 572 windows, interval [84.3, 89.7]: exact naming, label-defined isolated-stack state, real film, anomalous spines, bank v2
  - 97.2 %, n = 14,492 windows, interval [96.9, 97.4]: exact naming, isolated stack, real film, ordinary spines
  - 66.8 %, interval [62.9, 70.4]: anomalous isolated-stack naming after pseudolabel training (bank v5)
  - 91.8 %, n = 455 films, interval [89.4, 94.1]: ordinary lumbar naming on interior windows whose pixels exclude both ends of the stack, v2, film-clustered interval
  - 66.0 %, n = 12 films, interval [39.6, 87.2]: anomalous lumbar naming on interior windows, v2
  - 0 %: sacrum-anchored counting rule on interior 3-body windows, the floor
  - 83.0 % of errors: isolated-stack errors that are exactly one level (52% too low, 31% too high)
  - 44.9 / 8.3 %: errored windows carrying one coherent offset; share of all isolated-stack windows
  - 5.1 % of films, n = 3,456 ordinary lateral films: ordinary BUU laterals whose lowest-body Wigh index falls in the report's normal-L5 band 1.20-1.45; median 2.08
  - 3.61 odds ratio, n = 280 VerSe subjects, interval [2.08, 6.27]: co-occurrence of lumbosacral and thoracolumbar anomalies, both ends graded
- Datasets: CTSpinoPelvic1K, VerSe, BUU-LSPINE (buu_full), NHANES (nhanes, nhanes_drr, NHANESPS pseudolabelled), CSXA, AASCE, Mendeley, VinDr-SpineXR (vindr), lateral-236 (LAT236PS), FluoroCap decoded panels (bank v7), physics_drr and physics_rare CT renders, verse_drr, verse_drr_obl2, ctsp_drr, ctsp_drr_obl2 renders, whole-spine laterals (reading queue); tools: ResNet-18 encoder, bidirectional GRU head, DINOv3 (frozen and fine-tuned), CORN loss, ordinal focal loss, logit adjustment, SDXL LoRA film-look filter with Canny ControlNet, fitted film-noise model (scatter haze, Poisson + read noise, MTF blur), Wilson 95% intervals and film-cluster bootstrap (2,000 resamples), tools/make_feasibility_figures.py, tools/pipeline_state.py, tools/eval_interior_windows.py, slurm/reproduce_feasibility.sh and slurm/reproduce_all.sh (sbatch), LEDGER.tsv with sha256 per input/output, explain.json (occlusion ablation), paper/rsnai/main.tex (LaTeX manuscript), docs/SWEEP_PROTOCOL.md, docs/FEASIBILITY_REPRODUCE.md; models: ResNet-18 + BiGRU level classifier, banks v2, v3, v4, v5, v6 and v7 (arms r18_ce, r18_corn, r18_ordfocal, r18_ce_8ep), dinov3_frozen and dinov3_ft arms, the vertebra body detector (trained on real radiographs), the orientation network, the decoder (pseudolabeller) with variant alphabets, the transition-role head, the four-class linear probe on the frozen encoder, comparators: LevelCheck / msLevelCheck, VDVM 2023, X23D, RadGS-Reg, SX-Stitch 2024 / Liu 2026, Radlink Panoramic Fluoro, Wang 2015, MVMS (Huang 2022), ResNet-Transformer, Cina 2021, VERIDAH 2026.
- Decisions:
  - v6 moves all 144 anomalous BUU studies to the test partition, duplicates anomalous CT renders x4 in training and changes nothing else, so the ordinary column is a control., the author (the owner): 124 of the 144 wrongly counted studies sat in the training partition of every bank so far, so the 87% was measured on a model trained to give the ordinary an...
  - v6 changes the labels, not the architecture., the author: every ResNet-18 arm sits within 0.6 points and nothing tested beats the recipe
  - All 144 BUU anomalous studies barred from training; the S1a line promoted to an L6 box on six-lumbar studies and L5 flagged sacralised on four-lumbar., the author: BUU's counted names are wrong on those studies by the dataset's own reading
  - A film with no anomaly ground truth is dropped from the split rather than assumed ordinary., the author: reading absence of evidence as absence of anomaly manufactures specificity
  - The pooled cross-cohort odds ratio is not reported., the author: VerSe base rates are not prevalences and the pooled figure is Simpson's paradox
  - The earlier claim that the decoder chose a variant template only on films not reaching the junction (D2) is retracted and replaced., the author: it was a tautology of the page's own pseudolabelling code
  - The bank builder refuses unfitted film-noise parameters; a guessed setting was discarded., the author: the guessed setting overshot (noise 0.049, SNR 20); the fitted knobs land inside the real films' band
  - The film-look diffusion filter is trained on the public digitised X-ray datasets only, with FluoroCap excluded by an assertion that aborts the build., the author
  - Renders whose vertebrae flip the encoder's answer on more than 20% of bodies are dropped., the author

### 23. FluoroCap Corpus Audit

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/fluorocap-corpus-audit-9hjzzs`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: 9HjZzs5DXZq1cwT94v1SNx; page, audit; date: 2026-09-14 (Header line 'Corpus audit . 14 September 2026' and the footer 'Generated from tools/fluorocap_qc.py against the decod...); confidentiality: mixed.
- Question: What does the mined FluoroCap corpus hold, what are its machine-assigned vertebra pseudolabels worth when the caption is held out, and what blocks a morphology-based vertebra detector?
- Summary: An audit of eleven thousand spine radiograph panels mined from journal figures and surgical video, of which about three thousand carry machine-assigned vertebra labels. It finds that image size is the binding constraint (half the corpus is smaller than the level classifier's 320-pixel crop window), that the transitional classes T13 and L6 have never been assigned to any real panel, and that caption-held-out containment is 56.8% on 389 panels while the level model alone is right 38.3% of the time. A cross-check through one code path shows the level model scoring 98.7% on its own validation split but 35.7% on CT renders, traced to a training bank built from cervical and lumbar films with zero thoracic crops, so re-weighting cannot help. Constraint decoding lifts caption-free same-figure region agreement from 52.1% to 91.5%, and single-crop morphology holds from L1 to L4 on six-lumbar spines but never recognises the L6 itself. No panel has been read by a human, so every accuracy figure is agreement between automatic sources; the page ends with an ordered list: read 400 panels, finish the all-spine bank and retrain, oversample T13 and L6, raise resolution, fold in the self-trained detector.
- Key claims:
  - (measured) Only 29% of known panels produced a vertebra box, and much of the missing 71% is detector miss rather than non-radiograph panels, since the modality classifier scores 93% held out.
  - (measured) Image size, not contrast or sharpness, is the binding constraint: half the corpus is smaller than the classifier's 320-pixel crop window.
  - (measured) T13 and L6 have never been assigned to a single real panel, so the transitional-anatomy claim has no real-image evidence behind it.
  - (measured) Caption-held-out containment is 56.8% (n = 389) and only that row is a test; rows where the caption constrained the search agree 100% and prove nothing.
  - (measured) The level model alone is right 38.3% of the time on caption-held-out panels; every outside evidence term beats it, so the corpus must not be used to retrain the level model without re-weighting.
  - (measured) The collapse outside the lumbar spine is absence of training data, not imbalance: the bank holds no thoracic films and T2 through T11, T13 and L6 have zero training crops, so re-weighting would do nothing.
  - (measured) The model scores 98.7% on its own validation split but 35.7% on CT renders through the same code path.
  - (inferred) At L1 the gap between own films (85.4%) and renders (43.0%) is domain, not coverage.
  - (measured) Constraint decoding recovers structure the classifier lacks: caption-free same-figure region agreement rises from 52.1% to 91.5%.
  - (measured) Single-crop morphology holds from L1 to L4 on six-lumbar spines but the L6 is never recognised and L5 collapses, which is the safety case for the rare classes.
  - (measured) No panel has been read by a human; every accuracy figure is agreement between two automatic sources and bounds error from one side only.
  - (measured) Hardware robustness is not a measured property: the zero-shot implant detector has 33% recall and no labelled implant set exists on real panels.
- Numbers (80 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 11,349 panel images: panel images on disk in the corpus
  - 3,294 panels: panels decoded
  - 2,528 panels: panels with at least one vertebra box
  - 56.8 %, n = 389: caption-held-out containment of decoded level set and caption level set
  - 0 training crops: crops of T10, T11, T13 or L6 in the level model's training bank
  - 10,870 panels: journal figure panels
  - 479 frames: surgical video frames (YouTube route)
  - 2,815 captions: captions naming a vertebral level
  - 1,707 panels: panels with three or more boxes
  - 11,929 labels: vertebra labels assigned
- Datasets: FluoroCap corpus (mined panels), Europe PMC full text, PMC 18-million image set, Open-i, YouTube surgical video, Wikimedia Commons, Openverse, CT render banks (anchor DRRs), CTSpinoPelvic1K, VerSeFusion, all-spine bank (building); tools: tools/fluorocap_qc.py, constraint decoder, vision-language read, printed-level OCR, figure-mate vote, caption hardware-constraint parser, cone-beam renderer, reader pages, decode and panel manifests; models: level model (27-class, 320 px crop window), recurrent head, four-way region head, modality classifier, self-trained vertebra detector, zero-shot implant detector.
- Decisions:
  - The corpus must not be used to retrain the level model without re-weighting. (2026-09-14): Every outside evidence term beats the level model alone, so the model would be learning largely from its own errors.
  - Re-weighting (logit adjustment, class-balanced sampling), which the audit first recommended, is dropped in favour of an all-spine bank merging the films with every CT render source, now building. (2026-09-14): Thoracic levels, T13 and L6 have zero training crops; you cannot re-weight a class with no examples.
  - Two cone-beam renderer defects were fixed: padding with soft tissue instead of air, and a name collision on the source-image distance. (2026-09-14): Frames came out uniformly white and the render shard died mid-write.

### 24. Vertebral Levels Without CT

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/vertebral-levels-without-ct-a4rydj`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: A4rydJAxQurBkPKrvfAUQs; page, report; date: 2026-09-18 (Dateline under the lead: "18 September 2026 . every figure measured on held-out data"; the page also says a literatur...); confidentiality: mixed.
- Question: Can vertebrae be named from C-arm spot films without a CT by matching them onto a map built from a short sweep of overlapping exposures, and what still stands between that and a clinical result?
- Summary: An internal summary of a level-identification method that never counts: a short sweep of overlapping fluoroscopic exposures from a landmark (the sacrum or C2) builds a map of the patient's own spine, and later spot films are matched onto it. On twelve held-out whole-spine studies a thoracic two-vertebra film is named 4.3% alone and 91.3% matched (three vertebrae: 22.2% to 96.3%); lumbar real films go from 78.7% to 93.6%; per vertebra T11 to L5 the matched accuracy is 91.7 to 96.8%. All figures were re-measured after an audit found the earlier evaluation overlapped the training set, which had inflated the baseline rather than the sweep. Two bodies of overlap is the working point (3.0 exposures, 98.3%). Two things do not work yet: the automatic vertebra finder's duplicated boxes (17.9%), traced to a linking rule, made the sweep worse than not sweeping; and transitional spines are unmeasured with eight held-out cases. The page sets the literature straight (LevelCheck's 99.998% is a convergence rate; the only comparable figure is 88.3% on cadaveric long films), adopts a disc-based rule for sacral fusion, shows that lowering the detector threshold is the wrong lever, and asks the group to work three Hugging Face review sites (plain radiographs, published figures, a surgeon reader study) before a retrospective collection at DMC and a prospective study. Marked not for distribution outside the group.
- Key claims:
  - (measured) A thoracic spot film with two vertebrae and no landmark is named 4.3% of the time alone and 91.3% once matched onto the sweep; three vertebrae 22.2% to 96.3%.
  - (measured) The thoracic effect rests on twelve studies and the exact figure is provisional.
  - (measured) Lumbar real radiographs swept up from the sacrum go from 78.7% to 93.6% at two vertebrae in view.
  - (measured) Per vertebra from T11 to L5, three in the frame and matched, accuracy is 91.7 to 96.8% with no level falling away; 95% intervals are 5 to 10 points wide.
  - (measured) An earlier version of the page scored patients the model had trained on; the leak inflated the lone-frame baseline, so the re-measured advantage of sweeping is larger on smaller samples.
  - (measured) Two bodies of overlap is the working point: 3.0 exposures, chain intact 96.7%, 98.3% at two vertebrae; four bodies needs 6.9 exposures and loses accuracy.
  - (measured) Field width matters much less than the exposure count it implies.
  - (measured) With boxes from the group's own detector the sweep chain survives on 20% of films instead of 100% and sweeping becomes worse than not sweeping.
  - (measured) The detector misses only 2.2% of vertebrae on real film but 17.9% come out duplicated, traced to a linking rule in the group's own code, not the detector.
  - (unverified) Fixing the linking rule took naming from 67% to 92% with automatic detection.
  - (measured) Transitional spine performance is unmeasured: eight held-out transitional spines, one with six lumbar vertebrae named wrongly.
  - (measured) Real six-lumbar clinical images (83) were the only intervention that moved the six-lumbar number: 67.4% to 73.5% on 105 held-out patients.
- Numbers (71 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 4.3 % named correctly, n = 12 studies: Thoracic two-vertebra spot film judged on its own pixels, whole-spine studies swept from C2
  - 91.3 % named correctly, n = 12 studies: Thoracic two-vertebra spot film matched onto the sweep
  - 22.2 %, n = 12 studies: Thoracic three-vertebra film alone
  - 96.3 %, n = 12 studies: Thoracic three-vertebra film matched onto the sweep
  - 12 studies: Whole-spine studies surviving strict exclusion of everything trained on
  - 78.7 to 93.6 %, n = 25 films (chart says n=24): Lumbar two vertebrae, real radiographs, alone vs swept up from the sacrum
  - 0.0 to 100.0 %, n = 8 spines: Cervical two-vertebra frame, whole-spine, alone vs swept
  - 0.0 to 100.0 %, n = 6 spines: Upper thoracic two-vertebra frame, whole-spine, alone vs swept
  - 57.6 to 95.2 %, n = 71 spines: Lower thoracic two-vertebra frame, CT renders, alone vs swept
  - 76.9 to 93.9 %, n = 118 spines: Lumbar two-vertebra frame, CT renders, alone vs swept
- Datasets: Held-out whole-spine studies swept from C2 (12 studies; CT renders), Held-out lumbar real radiographs (25 films; the chart says n=24), Held-out CT spines rendered as radiographs (lower thoracic n=71, lumbar n=118), The rare-variant image set (845 six-lumbar spines, no landmark), The 376 transitional patients in cross-validation, CTSpinoPelvic1K (named only in the three review-site addresses), 3,000 lumbar and whole-spine radiographs on the plain-radiograph review site, 1,500 published-figure panels on the published-figures review site, The largest vertebra dataset and the main lumbar radiograph dataset (unnamed; cited for avoiding the sacral-fusion case); tools: The sweep-and-match method (map from overlapping exposures, image correlation at reduced resolution), The group's vertebra detector (confidence threshold 0.25), The linking rule between frames (tolerant version in testing), The evaluation tool that enforces the training exclusion, Hugging Face Spaces review sites (plain radiographs, published figures, surgeon reader study), Simulated C-arm reader-study interface with exposure counter, Cohen's kappa (Progress tab), LevelCheck (CT registration, literature comparator); models: The naming component (the group's own; six-lumbar class 67.4% to 73.5%), The vertebra detector (the group's own; 99.2% found), A planned sacrum detector of its own, LevelCheck (registration, literature), The registration-free network (literature, detection F1 >96%), 3-D labellers on CT (literature).
- Decisions:
  - Adopt the rule: look for the disc, not the fusion. A body still separated from the sacrum by a disc is a separate vertebra and counts; if the disc is gone it is part of the sacrum; a disc between S1 and S2 makes the top segment separate. (2026-09-18 (settled this week)), the group (the author): Every major dataset avoids the case, surgeons agree only a third of the time on the sacral endplate, and the disc is the one thing visible and decidable.
  - Keep two bodies of overlap between exposures as the working point., the author: Past two bodies the sweep needs more than twice the exposures and loses accuracy; the point survived the re-measurement.
  - Keep the detector threshold at 0.25; lowering it is ruled out as the wrong lever. (2026-09-18 (settled this week)), the group: Misses stay at 2.4% at every setting while spurious boxes double and the map survives on 8% instead of 40% of films.
  - Give the sacrum a detector of its own and define its target as the S1 body between its endplates; a partially lumbarised S1 gets its own box., the author: Two of the three remaining misses are the sacrum, and losing it costs the anchor the whole map is built from.
  - No figure will be quoted for transitional spines from the eight held-out cases, and no rate from the reader study., the author: Eight spines cannot distinguish 60% from 95%; the ten transitional misnamed frames are a census, not a sample.
  - Annotation order: finish the plain radiographs first, then the published figures; a twelve-case quiz (nine needed) gates entry and is taken once., the author: The first set is easier to learn on; shared misconceptions between readers would be written into the training set.
  - The reader study shows a deliberately wrong suggestion on 33 of 77 films and cuts each film's travel short of the sacrum., the author: To measure how often a confident wrong suggestion talks a correct reader out of a correct answer.
  - Solve the detector problem before the C-arm packaging question., the author: No use putting a system on a C-arm while automatic vertebra-finding makes it worse than doing nothing.
  - Everything is re-measured with the training exclusion enforced by the tool, replacing the earlier version of the page., the author: An audit found the evaluation and training set overlapped through a patient-grouped split the evaluation scripts could not see.
  - Not for distribution outside the group., the author
- Open questions: Transitional-spine accuracy: the 376-patient cross-validation is running, encoder folds landed, sequence folds running, no result yet. / Whether the tolerant linking rule settles the duplicated-box blocker beyond the one dataset where it recovered eight points. / Whether the sacrum detector closes the 99.2% gap. / Whether an embedded module on the C-arm carries the models (inferred from size, not measured). / Re-implementing the published methods for a head-to-head benchmark on one held-out set. / The retrospective collection at DMC enriched for transitional anatomy, read against cross-sectional imaging. / The prospective study in theatre: naming, exposures and reading time against the surgeon's own determination. / Whether a surgeon is better with the system than without, and whether a wrong suggestion makes them worse (the reader study).

### 25. Spine X-ray Data Handoff

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/spine-x-ray-data-handoff-bddbdd`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: BdBDdkFn4HQVV3fmbJcqfP; page, handoff; date: 2026-09-03 (Body says 'Everything here was checked against the grid on 3 September 2026, not recalled' and the footer 'Grid state...); confidentiality: schwingnet-other.
- Question: Which of nine public spine radiograph sources did the spine-detector pipeline actually obtain, why did the rest fail to download, and how does the collaborator taking the handoff get each one, ordered by how fast it unblocks?
- Summary: A handoff for the spine-detector pipeline's collaborator covering nine public spine radiograph sources. The manifest holds 6,073 vertebra instances from three ingested sources (NHANES II annotated subset, Mendeley 716 spondylolisthesis, and 77 BUU-LSPINE files), of which only 125 are AP against 5,948 lateral, so AP is the gap. The two biggest wins need no permission: a 400-pair BUU-LSPINE subset already sits on the owner's PC un-ingested, and the 2,000-pair subset is a single Google Drive zip that sidesteps the gdown folder cap that stopped the first download at 77 files. It corrects an earlier report: VinDr-SpineXR is PhysioNet restricted-access (register, sign the DUA, same day), not credentialed. CSXA (4,963 annotated cervical laterals) has zero images on disk only because Science Data Bank's download is a JavaScript UI behind a login; CM Spine's Drive link refused gdown; the BUU full release, AASCE/SpineWeb Dataset 16 and the IEEE DataPort lateral set need forms or a subscription. It closes with rules: everything lands on the grid, patient-disjoint splits, never invent levels, three sources not to use, and acknowledge every source.
- Key claims:
  - (measured) The manifest holds 6,073 vertebra instances from three sources, 125 AP and 5,948 lateral; AP is the gap.
  - (measured) A complete 400-pair BUU-LSPINE subset with annotations is already on the owner's PC and was never ingested.
  - (inferred) The first BUU download got 77 files because gdown's folder mode stops at roughly 50 files per folder; the 2,000-pair subset is a single zip that avoids the cap.
  - (reported) VinDr-SpineXR is restricted-access, not credentialed, on PhysioNet; the earlier report was wrong.
  - (measured) CSXA images are absent only because Science Data Bank's download is a JavaScript UI behind a login; eight guessed API endpoints all failed.
  - (measured) Every one of the 125 AP instances the model has seen came from the 50 BUU AP files.
  - (inferred) With the 400 and 2,000 BUU subsets, AP instances go from 125 to roughly 12,000.
  - (measured) 508 of the Mendeley 716 images are BUU images; only the 208 Honduran are independent and must stay out of training.
  - (inferred) AASCE levels are implicit by index (top vertebra assumed T1), so a 13-rib or L6 spine shifts every label; spot-check 50 images.
  - (unverified) The IEEE DataPort lateral set is the only public lateral source where pelvic incidence is computable and should be the external hold-out.
  - (measured) NHANES corners are derived from masks (minimum-area rectangle), not drawn by a radiologist, and the manifest records this.
  - (measured) Grid state on 3 Sep 2026: buu_v1 77 files, cm_spine 0, csxa 6 files (no images), mendeley_716 2 files, nhanes_annotated 2 files.
- Numbers (55 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 9 sources: public spine radiograph sources covered
  - 6,073 vertebra instances, n = 3 sources: instances in the manifest
  - 125 AP instances, n = 6,073: AP share of the manifest
  - 5,948 lateral instances, n = 6,073: lateral share of the manifest
  - 800 images, n = 400 patients: BUU-LSPINE 400-pair subset on the owner's PC, never ingested
  - 0 images: CSXA images on disk
  - 2,400 AP images: AP images from the two BUU subsets together
  - 7 cases: L6 (LSTV) cases in the BUU 400 subset's sheet

- Datasets: BUU-LSPINE (400-pair subset, 2,000-pair subset, full 3,600-patient release, 77-file partial buu_v1), VinDr-SpineXR (PhysioNet), CSXA Cervical Spine X-ray Atlas V3.0 (Science Data Bank), CM Spine intraoperative (zengyuyuyu/Neighbor), AASCE / SpineWeb Dataset 16, IEEE DataPort lateral full-spine, NHANES II annotated subset (Zenodo 10223910), NHANES II full unlabeled set (CDC), Mendeley 716 spondylolisthesis (5jdfdgp762), Not to use: Kaggle vertebrae X-ray images, TrainingDataPro spine sets on HuggingFace, Kaggle Lumbar Coordinate Pretraining (RSNA 2024 MRI); tools: gdown, rclone, wget, scp / ssh, unzip, pip, src/converters/buu.py (BUU converter), src/build_dataset.py, Google Drive, PhysioNet, Science Data Bank, SpineWeb, IEEE DataPort, Keypoint R-CNN JSON (Mendeley annotation format); models: the spine-detector model (Stage-1 supervision, level head).
- Decisions:
  - Ingest the BUU 400-pair subset and the 2,000-pair subset before touching anything that needs a form. (2026-09-03): The two biggest wins need no permission from anyone; together they give 2,400 AP images against 125 AP instances seen.
  - VinDr-SpineXR stays on the grid: never on HuggingFace, never through an API, never in a shared Drive; each person signs their own DUA.: The DUA forbids redistribution and use on online platforms.
  - The IEEE DataPort lateral set is the evaluation hold-out: annotate it, never train on it.: It is the only genuinely external test set; until it exists every test image comes from a source the model trained on.
  - Keep the 208 Honduran Mendeley images out of training entirely.: They are the only genuine cross-site lateral lumbar test set; 508 of the 716 are BUU images.

  - Split by patient ID, never by image.: BUU pairs AP and lateral per patient; Mendeley contains BUU patients; NHANES participants have two films.
  - Do not invent levels: where a source gives positions rather than names (BUU, AASCE), record level_source = inferred_from_position.: That distinction is the project.
  - Ruled out: Kaggle 'vertebrae X-ray images', TrainingDataPro spine sets on HuggingFace, Kaggle 'Lumbar Coordinate Pretraining'.: Scraped and murky; commercial scrape; the last is RSNA 2024 MRI routinely miscited as X-ray.

  - Ask the WSU library about an institutional IEEE DataPort subscription before anyone pays.: The dataset requires an IEEE DataPort subscription.

### 26. FluoroCap Reality Check

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/fluorocap-reality-check-czudvb`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: CZuDVb97j8AQbaapGAdYLs; page, audit; date: 2026-09-15 (Header line 'FluoroCap . validation . 15 September 2026'; 'All counts are snapshots from 15 Sep 2026, roughly 06:55-0...); confidentiality: mixed.
- Question: Can the FluoroCap corpus and the level model, as they exist on 15 September 2026, support a 99% level-identification claim on real fluoroscopy, and what would it take to get there?
- Summary: A validation page dated 15 September 2026 that measures what the FluoroCap corpus and the level model actually prove, ranks the blockers to a 99% claim by the strength of the evidence, condenses six literature sweeps (datasets, methods, synthetic-to-real, edge cases, acquisition, resolution) and lays out a from-scratch target, data plan, edge-case coverage table, evaluation protocol and 90-day and 12-month schedule. It finds exactly one held-out number (98.7% exact on 8,422 radiograph crops with human labels), no human-verified fluoroscopy panel (0 of 17,486 manifest rows), every render figure overlapping training, no real image with an L6 or T13 label, the level model as the weakest decoder term (37.9% on 169 real panels) and the v2 weights destroyed. The literature holds no synthetic-trained classifier reaching 95-99% on real fluoroscopy, discrete labels lose 9-28 points at the domain boundary, and the one real-film LSTV classifier reaches 76.4%. The verdict is that the approach is sound as a research direction and unmeasured as a claim, and that the binding constraint is labelled real intraoperative images, not pixels; the plan's Tier 0 is a registration-labelled PACS pull plus raw 3D C-arm projections, with the mined figures kept as an external test set only. The page was produced by fifteen agents in 41 minutes, with 48 literature claims checked against their sources (36 confirmed, 12 corrected, none refuted).
- Key claims:
  - (inferred) The thesis that local shape identifies a vertebral level is sound as a research direction but unmeasured as a claim.
  - (measured) The only held-out accuracy the files prove is 98.7% single-crop on 8,422 cervical and lumbar radiograph crops with human labels; nothing applies to fluoroscopy.
  - (measured) No real image in the corpus carries a human-verified level; every manifest row is caption-derived with readers pending.
  - (measured) Every render evaluation overlaps training, so no honest render number and no learning curve exist.
  - (measured) The level model alone is the weakest decider on real panels.
  - (measured) The all-spine v2 model exists only as a validation JSON; its weights were destroyed by the chain's link step and a retrain was pending.
  - (reported) The literature contains no synthetic-trained classifier reaching 95-99% on real fluoroscopy; discrete-label precedents drop 9-28 points at the domain boundary.
  - (inferred) Caption labels carry 5-10% counting-convention noise concentrated in the transitional stratum, making a 99% target against them uninterpretable.
  - (inferred) Transitional anatomy has no real positive and thin synthetic support, which caps accuracy at about 94% before any other error.
  - (measured) Hardware supervision is entirely synthetic while 57% of the included test panels are instrumented.
  - (measured) The thoracic spine has zero real training images and is the highest-risk region; an overall 99% would hide a thoracic figure below 60.
  - (inferred) The mined figures are a test set, not a training set: captions, burned-in text, compound-figure noise and duplicates make them unfit for level training.
- Numbers (80 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 98.7 %, n = 8,422 crops from 1,653 held-out patients: exact single-crop level accuracy of the films-only model on held-out cervical and lumbar radiograph crops at 0.17 mm/px with human labels
  - 99.8 %, n = 8,422 crops: within-one-level rate of the same films-only model (run head 99.4%)
  - 99.4 / 93.6 %, n = CSXA 7,440 / NHANES 982 crops: the 98.7% split by source
  - 0 human-verified panels, n = 17,486 manifest rows: fluoroscopy panels read by a human
  - 37.9 %, n = 169 caption-free panels: level model as the sole decider on real panels
  - 73.1 / 79.5 %, n = 93 / 44 panels: VLM and caption deciders on caption-free panels, beside the model's 37.9%
  - 35.7 %, n = first 300 ctsp_drr renders: films-only model on CT renders, the only strictly out-of-training render score
  - 80.2 / 78.5 %, n = 12,906 / 16,392 crops: all-spine v1 / v2 own validation with mixed labels; nhanes_drr is the NHANES films a second time
  - 92.7 %, n = first 600 ctsp_drr renders: all-spine v1 'diag' score, which overlaps training
  - 77.8 / 43.4 / 43.7 / 43.1 / 37.1 %, n = 2,657 renders (ctsp_drr 801, verse_drr 374, physics_rare 1,482): v1 per stratum: normal / Castellvi I-II / III-IV / L6 spines / T13 spines, overlapping training

### 27. Spinal Border Variants

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/spinal-border-variants-fye5ef`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: FyE5Ef7bZjrUJkgE5wEnok; page, analysis; date: undated (No date, stamp or 'as of' line anywhere in the page; the only version marker is the data folder named in the footer,...); confidentiality: schwingnet-other.
- Question: Across the 802 densified CTSpinoPelvic1K cases, does a variant at the thoracolumbar border (rib 12, lumbar ribs) predict a variant at the lumbosacral border (LSTV), and which segmentation-derived measures carry that phenotype?
- Summary: A results page over CTSpinoPelvic1K's 802 densified cases that measures the thoracolumbar and lumbosacral borders separately from the segmentation and tests whether a variant at one end predicts one at the other: it does, with an odds ratio of 19.6, 44 cases carrying both where independence predicts 9 (of 796 with complete measurements: 667 typical at both ends, 60 thoracolumbar only, 25 lumbosacral only). Three findings are kept: rib 12 length as a fraction of rib 11 is bimodal (modes near 0.68 and 0.32, 67 cases below 0.30); labelled LSTV cases sit at four (35) or six (26) free lumbar vertebrae instead of the cohort's five (734); and the thoracolumbar measure rib12_len_left_mm separates the lumbosacral LSTV label with d about 0.55. A table of Cohen's d ranks has_l6_label (4.7), n_non_rib_bearing (0.8), rib ratios (0.3 to 0.5), sacral size (-0.3) and Castellvi proxies (under 0.2). Caveats: the sacral foramina counts from the detector are unusable as anatomy (3 left and 7 right on one symmetric sacrum) and were excluded from the co-occurrence test; the Castellvi axes are proxies not grades; the 33 of 802 LSTV labels are an imperfect reference. A rib renumbering done alongside cut ribs on the wrong vertebra from 109 to 19 by giving lumbar ribs their own class (74 and 75), and seven cases are listed as needing a person.
- Key claims:
  - (measured) A thoracolumbar variant strongly predicts a lumbosacral variant: odds ratio 19.6, 44 cases with both where independence predicts 9.
  - (inferred) This is the 'one population, not two' claim made countable, but it is an association in segmentation-derived measures, not a causal or clinical result.
  - (inferred) Every measure is derived from the segmentation rather than from the vertebra numbering, because counting up from the sacrum begs the question when the sacrum is what changed.
  - (measured) Rib 12 length as a fraction of rib 11 is bimodal, a main population near 0.68 and a hypoplastic one near 0.32, which is what a discrete developmental variant should look like.
  - (measured) Labelled LSTV cases avoid five free lumbar vertebrae, sitting at four (35) and six (26) while the cohort piles at five (734); the 13 lumbar-rib cases cluster at four.
  - (measured) A thoracolumbar measure, rib12_len_left_mm, separates the lumbosacral LSTV label with d above 0.5, the co-occurrence showing a second way.
  - (measured) The sacral foramina counts from the detector are unusable as anatomy: small canals close in a bone mask and morphological closing invents others.
  - (inferred) The Castellvi lateral span and gap axes are proxies, not grades, so their flat effect sizes may reflect the proxy rather than the anatomy.
  - (measured) The LSTV labels are the reference and are imperfect, so effect sizes inherit their errors; 33 of 802 carry a non-normal label.
  - (measured) TotalSegmentator numbered the rib cages from the bottom, so a 13th rib on L1 took the id the T12 rib needed; a lumbar-rib class of its own freed the sequence and cut ribs on the wrong vertebra from 109 to 19.
- Numbers (30 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 802 densified CT cases: The CTSpinoPelvic1K cohort measured
  - 796 cases with complete measurements, n = 802: Cases entering the co-occurrence test
  - 19.6 odds ratio, n = 796: Association between a thoracolumbar variant and a lumbosacral variant
  - 44 cases with both variants, n = 796: Observed co-occurrence against 9 expected under independence
  - 9 cases expected under independence, n = 796: Expected co-occurrence if the two borders varied independently
  - 667 cases typical at both ends, n = 796: The co-occurrence table's typical cell
  - 60 cases thoracolumbar variant only, n = 796: The co-occurrence table
  - 25 cases lumbosacral variant only, n = 796: The co-occurrence table
  - 0.68 rib 12 / rib 11 length ratio (main mode): Main population of the bimodal rib 12 length fraction
  - 0.32 rib 12 / rib 11 length ratio (hypoplastic mode): Hypoplastic population of the bimodal rib 12 length fraction
- Datasets: CTSpinoPelvic1K (802 densified cases), data/v5_final (the measurements), morphometrics/ (figure and per-case values); tools: TotalSegmentator (rib cage numbering), the foramina detector (bone mask with morphological closing), Cohen's d standardised differences, the co-occurrence (odds ratio) test; models: TotalSegmentator.
- Decisions:
  - Every measure is derived from the segmentation rather than from the vertebra numbering.: counting up from the sacrum begs the question when the sacrum is what changed
  - The sacral foramina counts are kept as a relative signal with the reference box set to the cohort mode rather than a textbook 4, and are deliberately excluded from the co-occurrence test.: the detector does not deliver the anatomy: small canals close in a bone mask and morphological closing invents others
  - A flat effect-size bar is reported as a finding, not hidden as a gap.: it says the measure does not carry the phenotype in this cohort
  - Lumbar ribs get their own classes, rib_left_lumbar and rib_right_lumbar (74 / 75), after which each rib is named for the vertebra it articulates with.: TotalSegmentator numbered the cages from the bottom, so a 13th rib on L1 took the id the T12 rib needed
  - Case 0008's original labelling is preserved beside the +1 shift.: if its L5 is sacralized, that shift is wrong
- Open questions: 0008: labelled SACRALIZATION with the two readers disagreeing and shifted +1; if its L5 is sacralized the shift is wrong. / 0389 and 0720: no thoracic vertebra labelled, so no rib 12; fixable by rib order only if their L1 really is L1. / 0344: left ribs 6 and 7 reaching L3 and L5, a segmentation defect to repair. / 0179 and 0412: cages that disagree with themselves, no single renumbering fits. / 1153: right cage fragmented into speckle, to reannotate. / Whether the Castellvi proxies' flat effect sizes reflect the proxy or the anatomy.
- Stale markers: Measurements from data/v5_final / Open - these need a person (seven cases) / foramina counts are detector output and undercount true anatomy

### 28. FluoroCap Pipeline Map

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/fluorocap-pipeline-map-h9rmbs`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: H9RmBsLLyDrRda2d7jCQJk; page, atlas; date: 2026-09-14 (Header chip 'Pipeline map . 2026-09-14' and footer 'Generated with the repository at commit of 2026-09-14 (private)'.); confidentiality: schwingnet-method.
- Question: How do the pieces of FluoroCap fit together across six lanes (sources, ingest, evidence, decision, learning loops, serving), what does each stage wait for, and why is each safe to rerun?
- Summary: A map of the FluoroCap pipeline in six lanes: images come in from every reachable captioned source (PMC, aggregators, OpenAlex PDFs, Commons, YouTube with 905 queries, Reddit, Internet Archive with 799 items, patents, sitemaps and Common Crawl, archives), are made into documented panels with a 110-field manifest and DINOv2 duplicate groups, get independent lines of evidence (a YOLO11 pose corner detector, a four-model level ensemble with BiGRU run labeller and flip TTA, EasyOCR printed labels, OCR-guided recovery with SAM, Qwen2.5-VL-7B blind reads, OWLv2 hardware, anatomical anchors rendered as DRRs from CT, a 4-way orientation net), are decoded by decoder v2 into one contiguous run per panel scored by floored model probability plus caption, OCR, hardware, anchors, VLM and figure/video mates, go to reader pages with two readers and adjudication, and are frozen with SHA256SUMS before any claim. Learning loops are detector self-training (Noisy Student), FluoroGen (SDXL + ControlNet + LoRA restyling DRRs into C-arm frames), anchor detector training on 3,000+ rendered views, level-model arms in Slurm arrays, and nnU-Net v2 CT segmentation folds. Serving is a FastAPI level service in Docker with private weights. A Mermaid diagram and a table give the synchronisation: every stage is a Slurm job or a login-node orchestrator polling for a file or job name, outputs are JSON lines named by run tag, and the filesystem is the contract. An environments table names spineps, spinedet, fluorogen, totalseg, a Singularity image and WSL on the workstation.
- Key claims:
  - (unverified) Nothing in the pipeline shares memory or a database; the filesystem is the contract, with JSON-lines outputs named by run tag so any stage can be rerun.
  - (unverified) Decoder v2 is a pure deterministic function of its inputs running on CPU in seconds.
  - (unverified) The manifest regenerates byte-identical from the same inputs, which is what makes the release freeze idempotent.
  - (unverified) The corner detector was fine-tuned on zoomed windows because collimated frames broke the film-trained scale.
  - (inferred) A visible sacrum names L5 without counting, so anatomical anchors are rendered as DRRs from CT.
  - (unverified) Printed labels read by OCR are the strongest anchor and point to vertebrae the detector missed.
  - (unverified) The grid's outbound IP is captcha-throttled by PMC, so every network route runs from WSL on the workstation.
  - (unverified) A user-site onnxruntime built for a newer glibc shadows the fluorogen env unless PYTHONNOUSERSITE=1 is set.
  - (unverified) The same figure arrives by three routes, so splits are by duplicate group to avoid leakage.
  - (unverified) YouTube is the only source of real C-arm monitor frames with spoken level cues.
  - (unverified) The model never leaves the server: weights are pulled from a private repo with a read token and the website sends pixels only.
- Numbers (23 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 197k frames: frames from yt-dlp workers on WSL
  - 905 queries: YouTube search queries
  - 799 items: Internet Archive texts
  - 11 languages: OpenAlex PDFs and caption constraints parsed in 11 languages
  - 110 fields: manifest columns
  - A-E tiers: source tiers in the manifest
  - I1-I4 / E1-E8 inclusion / exclusion criteria: manifest criteria
  - 4 models: level ensemble size
  - 96 px: single-vertebra ResNet target size
  - 4 array tasks: OCR array and Qwen2.5-VL array
- Datasets: Europe PMC / PMC OA (BioC + JATS), Open-PMC-18M, Open-i, MultiCaRe, OpenAlex PDFs, Wikimedia Commons, Openverse, YouTube (yt-dlp), Reddit feeds, Internet Archive texts, Google Patents, Manufacturer sitemaps, Common Crawl, Wellcome, Europeana, Flickr, CTSpinoPelvic1K, VerSeFusion, TotalSegmentator labels; tools: Slurm (arrays, afterany chains, login-node orchestrators), rsync, yt-dlp, curl_cffi, WSL, requests, PyMuPDF, nibabel, scipy, PyTorch (torch cu124), ultralytics, torchvision, diffusers, transformers (<5), peft, accelerate, EasyOCR, Singularity (spinesurg-ct.sif), WandB trainer, FastAPI, Docker, Hugging Face (weights registry, private), Mermaid, SHA256SUMS / LOCK.json freeze, conda-style environments spineps, spinedet, fluorogen, totalseg; models: YOLO11 pose corner detector, single-vertebra ResNet level ensemble (4 models) with BiGRU run labeller and flip TTA, EasyOCR, SAM (point-prompt recovery), Qwen2.5-VL-7B, OWLv2 (zero-shot hardware), DINOv2 (duplicate groups), anchor detector YOLO11 (anchor_det_v1.pt), 4-way orientation net, 3-class panel classifier, decoder v2, detector self-training yolo_v6f / detect_v6f (Noisy Student), FluoroGen (SDXL + ControlNet + LoRA), nnU-Net v2 (CT segmentation folds), TotalSegmentator.
- Decisions:
  - Every stage is a Slurm job or a login-node orchestrator that polls for a file or job name; nothing shares memory or a database.: Outputs named by run tag let any stage rerun and every consumer find the newest evidence.
  - The unit of labelling is the panel, not the figure.: Figures are montages.
  - Splits are by duplicate group.: The same figure arrives by three routes; grouping prevents leakage.
  - The corner detector is fine-tuned on zoomed windows.: Collimated frames broke the film-trained scale.
  - No single line of evidence decides a level; decoder v2 scores every run over all of them with hard caption containment when it fits.: Independent lines of evidence per panel, none of which decides alone.
  - Manifest, model registry ids and analysis plan are frozen (SHA256SUMS, LOCK.json) before any claim.: Locked record before claims.
  - The weights registry releases by script only and refuses public destinations; the model never leaves the server.: Private weights served behind one endpoint; the website gets pixels, boxes and names only.
  - Every network route, GitHub and Hugging Face push and the sync run from WSL on the workstation, not the grid.: The grid's outbound IP is captcha-throttled by PMC; WSL is not.

### 29. Naming Vertebrae Without CT

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/naming-vertebrae-without-ct-hjy4vc`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: HJY4VC3VUVh7oNH4C7YEpR; page, report; date: 2026-09-18 (Dateline '18 September 2026 . every figure below is measured on spines the system was not trained on'.); confidentiality: schwingnet-method.
- Question: Can a short C-arm sweep plus morphology-based naming identify the vertebral level on a later limited-field spot film without a CT, and how does each of the six steps of that chain perform on held-out spines?
- Summary: A results page for registration-free vertebral level identification from the C-arm: a short sweep of overlapping exposures builds a map of the patient's own spine, later spot films are matched onto it by correlation and named from morphology, never from a count. The headline is that a three-vertebra spot film with no landmark is named correctly 93.9% of the time on the sweep against 85.7% alone (90.4% with the automatic detector rather than human boxes). The page walks the six-step chain (sweep with two bodies of overlap: chain intact 88.3% to 97.5%; a class-agnostic detector finding 97.6% of bodies with the sacrum given its own detector at 98.2% precision / 96.9% recall; box repair from local spacing cutting in-frame duplicates from 3.03% to 0.61%; majority rather than unanimity in frame linking cutting cross-frame duplicates from 21.8% to 3.0% and lifting chain naming from 67.4% to 91.8%; chain naming 100% on real film and 95.0% on CT renders; spot-film placement). It gives per-region two-vertebra figures (cervical and upper thoracic 0% alone to 100% swept), a widened cranial-anchor table (oblique renders fall to about 58%), a re-implemented comparison on 1,653 films and 8,422 vertebrae (counting paradigms abstain on 97-98%, this work 97.0% exact), and a literature comparator table (surgeons unassisted 69.6%, with CT overlay 97.6%). Transitional performance is declared unmeasured (eight held-out spines, one six-lumbar named wrongly), a cross-validation over 376 transitional patients is running, and the reading programme is argued for because only real six-lumbar examples ever moved that class (67.4% to 73.5% after 83 real images). Design decisions, a planned reader study of assisted naming, and plainly stated limits close it; it is marked not for distribution outside the group.
- Key claims:
  - (measured) A three-vertebra spot film with no landmark is named correctly far more often once matched onto the sweep than on its own pixels.
  - (measured) Two bodies of overlap is the right sweep protocol; more exposures make the chain worse.
  - (measured) The general detector's misses concentrate on the sacrum and are not recoverable by lowering the threshold.
  - (measured) A single-class sacrum detector trained on patient-split data recovers the anchor.
  - (measured) Box repair from local spacing, never from a count, removes most duplicate bodies and closes over half the gap to human boxes.
  - (reported) The published lumbar baseline's fixed-cardinality decode fails structurally on six-lumbar and fused spines.
  - (measured) Requiring a majority rather than unanimity when linking frames removed the project's largest error.
  - (measured) Once the chain is right, every vertebra in it is named correctly on held-out real film.
  - (measured) The further a frame sits from a landmark, the more the sweep is worth.
  - (measured) Transitional-spine performance is unmeasured, not poor.
  - (measured) Oblique projections are markedly harder, which matters because a C-arm is rarely square to the patient.
  - (measured) Counting paradigms score near zero on tight fields because they abstain, which is the clinical problem this work removes.
- Numbers (77 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 93.9 % named correctly: three-vertebra landmark-free spot film placed on the sweep, held-out real radiographs
  - 85.7 % named correctly: same spot film judged on its own pixels
  - 90.4 % named correctly: on the sweep with the automatic vertebra-finder instead of human boxes
  - 6 steps: length of the chain from exposure to named vertebra
  - 3 exposures: sweep covering most lumbar work; five or six reach mid-thoracic
  - 2 vertebral bodies of overlap: sweep protocol
  - 88.3 % chain intact: half a body of overlap, held-out spines
  - 96.7 % chain intact: one body of overlap
  - 97.5 % chain intact: two bodies of overlap
  - 82.5 % chain intact: four bodies of overlap, at more than twice the exposures
- Datasets: NHANES (real films, 39 spines in the cranial-anchor table; part of the 3,000-film review site), BUU (real films, 36 spines; the level-as-class detector family), VinDr (films in the plain-radiograph review site), whole-spine laterals (review site), CT studies rendered as radiographs (whole-spine and oblique renders), FluoroCap (1,500 published-figure panels with captions), the 376 transitional patients (cross-validation set), the 4,526-image sacrum-box training set; 5,110 images with a sacrum box; tools: C-arm sweep protocol, class-agnostic vertebra detector, sacrum-specific single-class detector, box repair from local spacing, image-correlation frame linker with a protocol search window, encoder with run-level sequence head, evaluation tool that enforces train/test exclusion, web reading tool / two review sites, transitional-likelihood queue score; models: the class-agnostic vertebra detector (this work), the sacrum detector (this work, single class, patient split), the morphology encoder and run-level naming head (this work), the published per-level detector of Klinwichit et al. (BUU family, level-as-class), the published lumbar baseline that decodes exactly five boxes, a published registration-free network on cadaveric long films (88.3%), CT-to-fluoroscopy registration (LevelCheck line).
- Decisions:
  - Name from morphology, never from a count.: Every failure mode in the published work traces to a count: a fixed cardinality, or a tally from a landmark that a transitional segment corrupts.
  - Repair boxes from local spacing measured in the frame, not from an expected number.: a six-lumbar spine is handled like any other
  - Require a majority, not unanimity, when linking frames.: Worth 24 points of chain naming, and provably free where the boxes are correct; with a human's boxes the two rules are identical to the second decimal
  - Delimit at the disc, not at the fusion.: on a third of these the bridge is joint-like in one place and solid in another, and expert readers disagree about which endplate is sacral (kappa 0.32-0.38)
  - Give the sacrum its own model.: It is not a four-cornered body and should not be asked of a model trained on four-cornered bodies.
  - Box repair fills at most two bodies into one gap and never extrapolates past the ends of a run.: a run of invented boxes is worse than an honest hole; inventing a sacrum would fabricate the landmark everything else depends on
  - The deployed configuration excludes pseudolabels on anomalous spines.: training on them cost twenty points of anomalous accuracy
  - No figure is quoted from the eight held-out transitional spines.: Eight spines cannot distinguish 60% from 95%
  - The planned reader study is on stored images, not a clinical trial, with transitional spines over-represented and each film read twice in randomised order.: it measures whether the aid helps a human, which is the claim, and it does not measure outcome
  - Not for distribution outside the group.
- Open questions: Does a surgeon assisted by this system reach the accuracy of a surgeon assisted by CT registration in cases with no CT? Nothing here answers it yet. / What is the rate at which a wrong suggestion overturns a correct read, and a correct suggestion rescues a wrong one? / What is transitional-spine performance? Unmeasured; the 376-patient cross-validation is running. / Can the published per-level baseline (Klinwichit et al.), training on this split, add a measured fourth comparator row? / Which registration-free comparators with public code or weights exist to re-implement? / Does the ensemble of general and sacrum detectors reconciled by chain geometry help? / Can a partially lumbarised S1 be labelled once readers draw them? / How much do oblique projections, where a C-arm is rarely square, degrade the deployed system?
- Stale markers: 18 September 2026 / A cross-validation over all 376 transitional patients is running / The published per-level detector of Klinwichit et al. is training on our split now / A broader search for registration-free comparators is running / until this week almost every one of those was simulated from a CT / Readers are now drawing them (S1 boxes) / Two review sites are live / A reader study of assisted naming ... the next thing to be built

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/fluorocap-access-setup-jlvkft`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: JLVkFTK3RUFrrLJs4bUEJn; page, runbook; date: 2026-09-14 (Header line 'Environment setup . 14 September 2026' and footer 'FluoroCap environment setup, written 14 September 2026'.); confidentiality: schwingnet-other.

It opens with the free fix already running: publishers' own sites hold larger renditions than PMC, their figure URLs are deterministic from the DOI for five publishers, and the measured gains on real corpus figures run from 4x (Springer, BMC, Nature) to 16x (Cureus); 18 of 21 test figures were replaced, the 3 failures being on SAGE. It then lays out the hand-over mechanism (a mode-600 file ~/.fluorocap/credentials.env filled in an editor, cookie jars, an Instagram session; tools/fluorocap_creds.py init and check report names and pass/fail only), the free API keys worth about five minutes each (NCBI, YouTube, Springer, Elsevier, Flickr, Reddit, Google CSE, PatentsView), Wayne State institutional access (campus IP first, proxy cookie second, with cautions about the 30 June 2026 library note and publisher licences), Instagram on a dedicated account at 8 to 20 seconds per post, Facebook as unreliable, and X/Twitter and TikTok as not worth buying. It ends with the order to do things in and the repository files that hold the same content.
- Key claims:
  - (measured) Publisher sites keep larger figure files than PMC, and for five of the corpus's largest publishers the figure URL is deterministic from the DOI and PMC file name.
  - (measured) Resolution gains by publisher: 16x Cureus, 9-20x MDPI, 12x Elsevier CDN, 6x Frontiers, 4x Springer/BMC/Nature.
  - (measured) Eighteen of twenty-one test figures were replaced by the refetch; the three not replaced are on SAGE, which blocks non-browser clients.
  - (measured) Secrets are handed over through one mode-600 file outside every repository, filled with an editor, and the check command prints only which keys are set and whether each service answers.
  - (reported) An NCBI API key raises the rate to ten requests a second from three, tripling the refetch that resolves 2,900 figures through NCBI.
  - (unverified) Video frames are the only source that is 1080 px native, and the current YouTube route scrapes search pages and misses most of what the API returns.
  - (measured) Google Patents cut the harvester off after forty requests, so the patent-drawing route needs a PatentsView key.
  - (unverified) The grid is inside the university's address space and publishers authenticate by IP, so the closed-journal refetch should be tried from the grid login node before asking for proxy cookies.
  - (reported) The library note of 30 June 2026 says DMC residents were dropped from WSU library access, so affiliation should be confirmed before exporting cookies.
  - (inferred) The closed-journal refetch is limited to about 600 figures with matched captions at one request every three seconds per host, capped per day, never full text or PDFs in bulk, because publisher licences forbid systematic downloading.
  - (unverified) Instagram serves posts at 1080 px, larger than the median journal panel, but serves almost nothing to an anonymous client, so the route runs on a logged-in dedicated account at one post per 8 to 20 seconds.
  - (unverified) X/Twitter's API at $100 a month gives only seven-day search, which cannot reach the historical posts, and TikTok needs URLs via yt-dlp rather than keys.
