### 31. Lift Before Label?

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/lift-before-label-jzjq8t`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: JzjQ8T76GJbZ36TtAWgPUZ; page, analysis; date: 2026-09-15 (Header 'FluoroCap . prior art . 15 September 2026' and the method note 'returned 173 sourced findings on 15 September...); confidentiality: schwingnet-method.
- Question: Should the FluoroCap identifier reconstruct a frame in 3-D before naming the vertebral level, can CT-to-fluoroscopy registration supply labelled training frames, and would paired synthetic CT editing widen the data?
- Summary: A prior-art synthesis from four literature sweeps (173 sourced findings) answering three questions for the FluoroCap level identifier. On lifting to 3-D before classification the answer is no: no paper reconstructs a radiograph to 3-D then classifies with a gain over the 2-D classifier, single-view lifting is a prior-driven completion that erases exactly the transitional features the label depends on, and what helps instead is 3-D knowledge inside the 2-D model (two-view fusion, physics and randomisation in the renderer, correspondence pretraining). On registration as a data source the answer is yes: registering the patient's CT to the frame labels the real frame, which beats its DRR twin as training data, and the page budgets hundreds to low thousands of registered frames from at least 10 to 20 patients. On paired synthetic editing the answer is partly: hardware insertion is mature, lesion synthesis is proven only for 3-D segmentation, and transitional-variant or corpectomy editing has never been published and is worth a week. It closes with an ordered plan, open questions, and a caveat that the adversarial verification pass did not run.
- Key claims:
  - (reported) No published work reconstructs a radiograph or C-arm frame to 3-D and then classifies with a gain over a 2-D classifier; a reconstruction stage should not be built.
  - (reported) The only lift-then-decide gain in X-ray-to-CT is chest X-ray to CTPA for pulmonary embolism, and its mechanism does not transfer here.
  - (reported) The closest work, a DRR-trained lumbar reconstruction network, scored almost nothing on real Cios Spin frames until adapted with 3,317 registered real crops and still needed three to four calibrated views.
  - (inferred) Single-view lifting is a prior-driven completion that completes an L6, an absent twelfth rib or a corpectomy defect toward the typical spine, the very feature that decides the label.
  - (reported) Putting 3-D knowledge into the 2-D model has evidence: two-view fusion, CT-derived correspondence pretraining and pose randomisation in the renderer.
  - (reported) Registration labels real intraoperative frames, which is how such images get labels; training on the real frame beats training on its DRR twin.
  - (inferred) No learning curve over the number of registered real frames exists for any spine task; budget hundreds to low thousands of frames over at least 10 to 20 patients.
  - (reported) Inserting hardware into CT before rendering is mature and validated on real frames.
  - (reported) Editing a labelled CT to create transitional variants or a corpectomy defect has not been published by anyone and is cheap to try with the project's label maps.
  - (reported) Recognition sim-to-real gaps are wider than geometric ones.
  - (reported) Whole-CT generation rendered to DRRs loses to both real frames and 2-D X-ray diffusion.
  - (reported) The level is recoverable from projections without volumetric processing; the discriminating cues are projected features.
- Numbers (54 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 173 sourced findings: findings returned by four literature sweeps
  - 0.691->0.803 AUC, n = 898 real paired patients: X-ray2CTPA pulmonary embolism gain, generator trained on >7,000 CTPAs
  - 8 vs 76 % surface score (one case): Jecklin 2024 DRR-trained lumbar reconstruction on a real Cios Spin frame vs its DRR twin
  - 3,317 registered real crops, n = 10 specimens: real crops needed to adapt Jecklin 2024's network; F1 0.84 vs 0.86 DRR ceiling
  - 99.5 vs 97.3 % identification: ProVLNet two-view fusion vs single view on calibrated biplanar DRRs
  - +15.5 / +35.0 % (AP / lateral): Huang 2022 two-view gain on real cadaver long films
  - +1.4 to +2.0 points: Dabboussi 2025 CT-derived multi-view correspondence pretraining on real fracture classification
  - 8.58->20.82 mm landmark error: Mo 2025: a co-trained registration loss doubled landmark error
  - 366 real fluoroscopy frames, n = six cadavers: DeepFluoro registered frames with forward-projected 3-D annotations (Grupp 2020)
  - 20 of 20 clinical radiographs: LevelCheck correct level at 200 multistarts; 398 of 398 in the later series
- Datasets: DeepFluoro, CTSpinoPelvic1K, VerSe (DRRs), VerSe19 (Genant-graded renders), NLST (DRRs), the FluoroCap corpus (figure mates), the 802 labelled CT cases, the retrospective pull (Protocol 1), 178 pelvic CTs (xvr pretraining); tools: DiffDRR, DiffPose, xvr, DeepDRR, SyntheX, pix2pix, CycleGAN, ADDA, LevelCheck, msLevelCheck, Gaussian splatting (IXGS), the physics renderer, the registration harness, the grid; models: the direct 2-D level identifier (FluoroCap), the crop encoder, X-ray2CTPA, Jecklin 2024 lumbar reconstruction network, ProVLNet, Btrfly-net, 2-D ResNet LSTV classifier (Kwak 2025), X2CT-GAN, XctDiff, TomoRay, X2BR, Spine-DART, DiffPose, FluoroSAM, FreeTumor, MAISI, Pelphix, PolyPose, Pri3D, FiT3D, DINOv2, CroCo, RealDRR.
- Decisions:
  - Do not build a reconstruction stage; keep the direct 2-D identifier and use both views when the C-arm gives both. (2026-09-15): No published lift-then-classify gain exists, and single-view lifting completes transitional anatomy toward the typical spine.
  - Build the registration route, label every real frame that registers, keep the DRR twin only as augmentation, and fine-tune the identifier on real frames. (2026-09-15): Registration labels real frames, and real frames beat their DRR twins as training data (4.89 vs 7.83 mm).
  - Hardware editing stays; transitional and corpectomy CT editing is worth a week; do not generate whole CTs. (2026-09-15): Hardware insertion is validated on real frames, transitional editing is unpublished but cheap with existing label maps, and 3-D generation rendered to DRRs u...
  - Do not build a single-view reconstruction stage, a 3-D CT generator, or an img2img translator. (2026-09-15): The evidence says each costs more than it returns.
- Open questions: How a discrete level label's sim-to-real gap scales with the number of registered real frames; no learning curve exists for any spine task. / Whether transitional-variant CT editing transfers at all; no prior work. / Whether MAISI or a vertebra-conditioned generator at higher resolution would change the 3-D-generation verdict. / Whether a pose-conditioned identifier helps on real frames. / Whether PMC10968904 holds classification-level evidence for 3-D reconstruction pretraining in natural images; it could not be opened.
- Stale markers: 15 September 2026 / The adversarial verification pass and the synthesis agent hit the session's subagent limit before running / the verification pass is scheduled to run when the limit resets, after which any correction is folded in here / Finish the registration harness (running) / it is being built: DiffDRR/DiffPose on the grid, synthetic then DeepFluoro / PMC10968904 could not be opened / Real registered frames from the retrospective pull (Protocol 1) still to come

### 32. Overnight: The Honest 99%

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/overnight-the-honest-99-kantj8`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: KanTj8B4omwWtpbz9wABZD; page, report; date: 2026-09-16 (Header 'FluoroCap . overnight, 15-16 September 2026'; the page reports an overnight run of 15 to 16 September.); confidentiality: schwingnet-method.
- Question: What accuracy can the FluoroCap vertebral-level model honestly promise, at what coverage, and what does the night's work say to do next?
- Summary: An overnight report from nine agents on the FluoroCap level-naming model. It reports accuracy as a pair, accuracy among answered and coverage: 99% right on the 35.7% of vertebrae it names exactly, 99% at 55.7% coverage for a region, and conformal prediction at a 1% error rate giving one region on 70.3% of vertebrae at 99.7%, all on held-out renders. The transitional flag does not improve with more positives (0.652 at ten, 0.713 at 120), and on 3,600 real radiographs it reads age and exposure, not anatomy, while a sex control transfers. Two defects were found: an abstention rule that abstained on the Castellvi IIIb positives it should protect, and label-map errors in VerSeFusion and record 1043. Two benches await the owner (179 panels of 865 vertebrae; 585 transitional figures), the corpus is tabled (20,700 panels, 12,106 figures), a PRISMA-ScR scoping review lost its hypothesis and found that level-determination countermeasures are never evaluated against an outcome, and the next steps are the verification bench, an ordinal loss, real-film calibration, a Castellvi I abstain class and a selective-prediction paper.
- Key claims:
  - (measured) Among the vertebrae it names exactly the model is right 99% of the time, naming 35.7% of them; for a region, 99% at 55.7% coverage.
  - (measured) Conformal prediction at a 1% error rate returns a single region on 70.3% of vertebrae at 99.7% accuracy, with a mean set of 2.56 names.
  - (measured) All headline numbers are on held-out renders; the real-film version needs a calibration set.
  - (reported) The best published whole-spine identification is 96.5%, on MRI, counting from C2; no published system reaches 99% on plain radiographs.
  - (measured) More examples will not move the transitional flag: the learning curve from 10 to 160 positives is flat past forty.
  - (measured) The domain gap is not the explanation for the flag's failure on real film: a sex control trained on the same renders transfers (0.951 to 0.829).
  - (measured) On real films the transitional flag retrieves known cases at chance and reads degeneration and exposure, not anatomy.
  - (measured) Framing beats data: only the tight lumbosacral junction view excludes chance (0.656); the wide field showing the twelfth rib is below chance (0.406).
  - (measured) The abstention rule abstained on the Castellvi IIIb positives because it required L5 or L6 in frame; it now asks for the lowest mobile lumbar segment by position.
  - (measured) The VerSeFusion manifest's eighteen T13 subjects are six L6 cases and twelve with no such label; label 25 means L6 in one scheme and T13 in another.
  - (reported) Choosing models on noisy validation labels can cost 24 points of apparent accuracy, so a random verification bench comes first.
  - (unverified) Castellvi type I is about two thirds of transitional anatomy, is separable in no held source, and contaminates every negative set.
- Numbers (71 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 35.7 % of vertebrae, n = 2,956 answered, interval 95% lower bound 98.7% on the accuracy: coverage at which exact-level naming is 99.0% accurate among answered
  - 55.7 % coverage: coverage at 99% accuracy when naming a region instead of an exact level
  - 70.3 % of vertebrae: single region returned by conformal prediction at a 1% error rate, at 99.7% accuracy
  - 96.5 % accuracy: best published whole-spine identification, on MRI, counting from C2
  - 47.4 / 74.9 % coverage (exact / region): coverage at 97.5% target accuracy
  - 58.0 / 95.7 % coverage (exact / region): coverage at 95.0% target accuracy
  - 72.8 / 100 % coverage (exact / region): coverage at 90.0% target accuracy
  - 78.8 / 93.4 % accuracy (exact / region): accuracy at full coverage
  - 2.56 names: mean conformal set size at a 1% error rate
  - 33.6 % of vertebrae: single confident name from conformal prediction, at 99.2% accuracy
- Datasets: FluoroCap corpus (PubMed Central figures, video frames, Reddit, Commons), VerSe, VerSeFusion, BUU films, the project's own 802 CTs, 3,600 real radiographs, Radiopaedia (not scraped), Eurorad (not scraped), public-domain atlases and Bookshelf (image hunt); tools: conformal prediction, risk-coverage curve (generalized area), PRISMA-ScR, Fisher exact test, the physics renderer (scatter term, photon budget, material model), ordinal loss; models: the vertebral level model (27 classes), the transitional flag model, the sex control model, the backbone and ordinal-loss arm (running).
- Decisions:
  - Report accuracy as a pair, accuracy among answered and coverage, with the whole risk-coverage curve, instead of one accuracy. (2026-09-16), the author (the agent): no published system reaches 99% on plain radiographs; a single accuracy would be untrue

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/protocol-1-eprotocol-draft-mkmh7p`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: MkmH7pD6fUhep8kcS228EY; page, protocol; date: 2026-09-15 (Header line: "Protocol 1 of 2 \| WSU eProtocol draft, 15 September 2026"); confidentiality: schwingnet-other.

Aim 2 is rewritten so that intraoperative fluoroscopy-CT pairs are a named data source and 3-D to 2-D registration of each intraoperative image to the same patient's CT supplies the reference level labels; five hypotheses (H1 to H5) cover CT level naming, registration labels, a registration-free model, fracture detection and registry outcomes. It gives the background figures (wrong-level surgery 0.12% of 34,544 cases, LSTV prevalence 15.3%, LevelCheck 398 of 398), the statistical methods (Wilson intervals, strata by anatomy, region and instrumentation), a sample-size argument (about 1,500 verified vertebrae per stratum, 6,000 in all, 400 fractured studies), the de-identification procedure (DICOM PS3.15 profile, OCR screening and 100% visual inspection of frames, 10% audit of text) and the confidentiality, consent and HIPAA waiver pages.   - (measured) L5 end-plate and pedicle width are contaminated because the body is cut at the canal wall; three replacement rules over all 802 records did not beat the shipping one, so L5 is withheld.
  - (measured) Licensing is enforced in code: src/licensing.py makes the exporter unable to select a forbidden source.
  - (inferred) Commercial-clean sources today are CSXA, the 208 Honduran images, and NHANES if counsel agrees; VinDr is research-only and is the entire lesion corpus.

  - Withhold L5 from the end-plate and pedicle-width panels and from level_atlas.csv rather than publish with a caveat (locked).: The body is cut at the canal wall where L5 transverse processes arise; three replacement rules did not beat the shipping one.
  - Licensing is researched first and enforced in code, src/licensing.py (in effect).: Nothing in a checkpoint records what it saw, so a README is not a control.

  - IRB correspondence purged from public history with git-filter-repo and both remotes force-pushed (done).: Eight named DMC/WSU staff with work addresses were on two public remotes.
  - For Cobb angles, train on synthetic and validate on real; synthetic cannot be the test set.: Spinal-AI2024 is synthetic; AASCE is the real benchmark.

### 35. Counting Against Two Anchors

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/counting-against-two-anchors-qkktgu`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: QkktguwS3JwgGWUbFdjvVj; page, spec; date: undated (No date, 'as of', chip or run stamp appears in the text; the only provenance line is 'Full derivation in docs/ANCHOR_...); confidentiality: schwingnet-method.
- Question: How can a segmentation network enumerate vertebrae correctly in transitional anatomy when it is trained almost entirely on normal anatomy?
- Summary: A training-design page for single-pass LSTV and TLTV labelling on CTSpinoPelvic1K (802 CT records with spine, pelvis, per-level ribs and femurs in one frame). It argues that predicting a level name is the wrong target, since a sixth lumbar vertebra occurs in roughly 2% of people and a model learns to call every lowest lumbar vertebra L5; instead two dense ordinal heads predict n_up (vertebrae above the sacrum) and n_down (below the lowest rib-bearing vertebra), the name is derived, and an anomaly is detected as disagreement \|n_up + n_down - K\|, a self-supervised constraint that holds on every normal case. Because a lumbar rib and a sacralisation shift that sum identically, it proposes four morphological anchors (costal facets, rib articulation, SI articulation, promontory) whose pattern of disagreement is a signature naming the variant, plus a thoracic-versus-lumbar morphology head that repairs the rib anchor when the twelfth rib is aplastic (T12 separates from L1 at AUC 0.990, 97.3% over 1485 vertebrae). It names the patch tension (a 112 x 112 x 320 column-spanning patch at 4.01 Mvox cannot span the 309 mm pelvis, and anisotropy blurs the 2-4 mm costotransverse joint), resolves it with a cascade, lists five experimental arms with arms 1 and 2 as the matched pair the argument turns on, and lists the metrics to report instead of Dice. It closes by recording that sacral foramina failed as an anchor because their count in normal-labelled cases (mode 3, not 4) overlaps the variants, dominated by field of view.
- Key claims:
  - (inferred) Predicting a level name directly is the wrong target: the model is rewarded for never predicting L6, a property of the target and not the backbone.
  - (inferred) Two ordinal counts from opposite anchors (sacrum and lowest rib-bearing vertebra) let the name be derived and the anomaly be detected as disagreement, learned from the normal cases.
  - (inferred) The sum n_up + n_down cannot distinguish a lumbar rib from a sacralisation, since both reduce it by one.
  - (inferred) Four morphological anchors are perturbed by different variant families, so the pattern of disagreement is a signature that names the variant and decomposes a case with both a lumbar rib and a sacralisation.
  - (measured) Thoracic and lumbar vertebrae separate by shape alone on this corpus at AUC 0.990 and 97.3% accuracy over 1485 vertebrae.
  - (measured) A tall narrow 112 x 112 x 320 patch at 1 mm spans the column in 4.01 Mvox, cheaper than nnU-Net's default 192 cubed at 7.08, but cannot span the pelvis's 309 mm p95 width.
  - (inferred) The consistency penalty \|n_up + n_down - K\| is a self-supervised constraint that applies to every case, including those with no transitional label.
  - (measured) Sacral foramina are not a usable anchor on this corpus: the count in normal-labelled cases has mode three and overlaps the labelled variants, dominated by field of view.
  - (inferred) The delta between arm 1 (semantic heads only) and arm 2 (plus ordinal heads and consistency loss) on the same folds is the result the design turns on.
  - (inferred) Count accuracy must be split by typical and transitional anatomy, since an overall figure hides a null result on the transitional cases.
- Numbers (18 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - roughly 2 % of people: prevalence of a sixth lumbar vertebra, the reason a name-predicting model never predicts one
  - 97 %: how often the 'lowest lumbar is L5' prior is right; also the share of normal cases the counting functions are learned from
  - ~800 cases each: normal cases from which each counting function (from the sacrum, from the ribs) is learned
  - sixteen lumbar-rib examples: the scarce positives the model need not memorise
  - 0.990 AUC, n = 1485 vertebrae: T12 versus L1 separation by shape alone with each patient's size divided out
  - 97.3 % accuracy, n = 1485 vertebrae: T12 versus L1 classification by shape alone on this corpus
  - about 265 mm: T11 to S1 endplate distance on this corpus, the receptive field n_up at L1 needs
  - 112 x 112 x 320 voxels at 1 mm: tall narrow patch that spans the column in 4.01 Mvox
  - 7.08 Mvox: nnU-Net's default 192 cubed patch, for comparison with 4.01 Mvox
  - 309 mm (p95): pelvis width the tall narrow patch cannot span
- Datasets: CTSpinoPelvic1K (802 CT records with released labels); tools: nnU-Net (ResEnc-L, 3d_cascade_fullres, default 192^3 patch), ordinal cross-entropy (rank-consistent ordinal regression), Dice + CE, BCE + Dice losses, ROC on the anchor-agreement scalar, Castellvi grading; models: ResEnc-L with a tall narrow patch, semantic heads only (arm 1), ResEnc-L plus two ordinal heads and the consistency loss (arm 2), 3d_cascade_fullres (arm 3), PrimusV2 on the winner (arm 4), the staged count-free pipeline (arm 5, the existing comparator), rib-bearing per-voxel head (already built).
- Decisions:
  - The target changes from a level name to two ordinal counts (n_up from the sacrum, n_down from the lowest rib-bearing vertebra) with the name derived.: A name-predicting model is rewarded for never predicting L6 (about 2% prevalence) and calls every lowest lumbar vertebra L5; the defect is in the target, not...
  - Anchors are chosen by morphology (costal facets, rib articulation, SI articulation, promontory) so that each variant family perturbs a different subset.: Every count anchor is perturbed by the variants it should detect, and a lumbar rib and a sacralisation shift n_up + n_down identically.
  - A third per-voxel head predicts thoracic versus lumbar type from morphology, repairing the rib anchor when the twelfth rib is aplastic; the vertebra names the rib.: With an aplastic twelfth rib n_down has no origin, and the costal facet is present in hundreds of training thoracic vertebrae (T12 vs L1 at AUC 0.990).
  - Ordinal regression is used rather than plain regression for the count heads.: The targets are small ordered integers; rank-consistent formulations respect that where an L2 loss does not.
  - The cascade (3d_cascade_fullres) is the principled resolution of the patch tension: a low-resolution stage locates sacral anchors against the whole pelvis and a full-resolution stage resolves the costal facet.: The ordinal heads need a column-spanning receptive field (265 mm) and the morphology classes need native detail (2-4 mm joint); no single patch serves both.
  - Arms 1 and 2 are run as a matched pair on the same folds; their delta is the result.: It isolates the contribution of the ordinal formulation from that of the architecture, the comparison the whole argument turns on.
  - Sacral foramina are ruled out as anchor C.: Across 802 records the foramina count in normal-labelled cases has mode three and overlaps the labelled sacralisations and lumbarisations completely, dominat...
  - Rib-free count accuracy is reported split by typical and transitional anatomy, with anchor-agreement calibration (an ROC on the scalar), stump-rib recall and the failure mode, rather than Dice.: Dice would hide a model 97% accurate overall and 0% on transitional cases.
- Open questions: Whether the ordinal heads plus consistency loss (arm 2) beat the semantic-only baseline (arm 1) on the same folds; the arms are listed, not run. / Whether the cascade in fact serves both the counting heads and the morphology classes, given that they want different patches. / Whether PrimusV2's positional embeddings help where long-range ordering is the entire task. / Stump-rib recall, the case where the rib anchor fails and morphology carries it alone. / Whether \|n_up + n_down - K\| separates transitional from typical anatomy on this corpus (the ROC is proposed, not reported). / Whether the SI-articulation anchor moves under Castellvi III-IV and lumbarisation ('may move').
- Stale markers: the rib-bearing head is 'already built' / the staged count-free pipeline is 'the comparator that already exists' / the five arms are proposed and not yet run / 'Counts quoted are measured on the released labels' of CTSpinoPelvic1K, which may change with a later release
- Related: Counting From The Sacrum (same-topic); Vertebral Count Anomaly Rule (same-topic); Rib Anchor Audit (same-topic); Naming Vertebrae Without an Anchor (same-topic); Spinal Border Variants (same-topic); Counting Levels Under Fluoroscopy (same-topic); Level Counting Visual Abstract (same-topic); LSTV Verification Bench (same-topic); Handoff: SchwingNet, CTSpinoPelvic1K and the imaging programme (same-topic); FluoroCap Reality Check (same-topic)

The anchor table's legend ('anchor holds / anchor moves / may move') implies colour coding not visible in the text, so which cells carried which colour is read from the arrows...

### 36. FluoroCap Adjudication Bench

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/fluorocap-adjudication-bench-rnsyir`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: RnsYiR7HZZsgW3UkY7jB5e; bench, bench; date: undated (No date appears in the page's text, metadata or script data. The batch file's own summary (not the artifact) says no...); confidentiality: mixed.
- Question: Are the vertebral levels the FluoroCap decode pipeline assigned to 120 stratified panels correct, as judged by a human reader who reads the film before seeing the caption?
- Summary: The page is a single-reader adjudication bench over 120 FluoroCap panels drawn stratified across the pipeline's deciding evidence (caption, model, mate, ocr, vlm, hardware, none, anchor) and spinal region (cervical, thoracic, lumbar, sacral), with or without a caption. For each panel it shows the image with invert, boost and zoom controls, what the pipeline decided (the decoded levels with per-level confidences, the decider, the box count, whether the order was reversed, whether the caption was forced, the region drawn and the id), and a caption the reader may reveal, recording whether it was revealed. The reader picks one of five verdicts (levels correct, off by one level, wrong by more than one, cannot tell, not a spine radiograph), may type the correct levels and a note, and the reads are saved to the artifact's shared record through the db capability when it connects, else to the browser's localStorage. The item data comes from decode_v2_v2.jsonl under the batch tag b1; 93 panels come from PMC figure panels (with captions) and 27 from video panels (without). No verdicts or results are present in the page.
- Key claims:
  - (measured) The 120 panels were drawn stratified across the pipeline's deciding evidence and the spinal region.
  - (measured) The bench records whether the reader revealed the caption before giving a verdict.
  - (inferred) For a panel with no caption naming a level, the reader's verdict is the only ground truth it will have.
  - (measured) Reads go to a shared record when the page's database connects, otherwise they stay in the one browser.
  - (measured) The pipeline's decisions shown come from the decode run decode_v2_v2.jsonl, each panel carrying its decider, decoded levels and confidences.
  - (measured) Panels the pipeline could not decide (decided_by null, stratum none) carry many more boxes than the decided ones.
- Numbers (24 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 120 panels, n = 120: Panels in the bench, drawn stratified
  - 120 image files: JPEG panels in the artifact's img/ folder: 93 named FLUO-* and 27 video panels
  - 31 strata: Distinct decider\|region\|caption strata in strata_counts (sum 120)
  - 11 panels: Largest stratum: caption-decided thoracic panels with caption
  - 10 panels: Caption-decided lumbar panels with caption
  - 6 panels: Caption-decided cervical, and model-decided thoracic, panels with caption
  - 4 panels: Standard stratum size for most decider-region cells (e.g. vlm, ocr, hardware, model, mate, none per region)

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/openspine-board-t3gfds`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: T3GfDSZpBYiLRC2166iu8o; page, board; date: 2026-09-11 (Date chip in the header: '11 SEP 2026 . 09:05 EDT'); confidentiality: mixed.

The dataset paper (10 pages, pushed to both remotes) got five fixes: canal depth was measuring the wrong chord on the wrong slice (473 to 12 records below 11 mm), 128 low pelvic incidences were a plate leaning backwards (39/39 vs 0/39, 140 plates rejected on geometry, table II n 563 with PI 52.7 SS 33.2 PT 19.7), L5 withheld for end-plate and pedicle width, recumbency's effect on sacral slope corrected against Hasegawa, Figure 5 reverted to one CT line, and a hardcoded rib base fixed (237 of 802 records moved). The radiograph corpus grows from 4,327 toward about 13,500: the CSXA converter yields 24,815 named cervical vertebrae on 4,963 films, and BUU ships unused LSTV truth (3,461 / 15 / 130, 4.0%) and slip grades (465 grade-3, 194 grade-4). Architecture: one backbone, four heads, level identity on a head; a licensing table checked against primary sources is enforced in src/licensing.py. Waiting on the owner: purging public IRB files, BUU commercial permission, and a competing group (Barr et al.) already on CSXA.

### 38. Rib Anchor Audit

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/rib-anchor-audit-t7wr2h`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: T7wr2H41C4dfFUduW6zwms; page, audit; date: undated (No date line, chip or stamp in the text; the only version marker is 'CTSpinoPelvic1K . v4 -> v5'.); confidentiality: mixed.
- Question: Did merging the reviewed ribs and thoracic spine into CTSpinoPelvic1K v5 fix the rib numbering errors, and can grouping vertebrae by rib-bearing retire the naming argument and agree with a radiologist?
- Summary: On 802 cases and 11,579 ribs, v5 = v4 plus 169 rib and 107 thoracic spine reviews, merged anatomy-pure by id range. Scoring each rib against the vertebra its number claims (within 10 mm), numbering errors fell 170 to 109 (down 36%) and the targeted -1 shift 129 to 71, while the +1 arm barely moved, the signature of a real correction; lumbar ribs rose 21 to 25, so anomalies were preserved. Two QC bugs of the author's own inflated rib-count asymmetry: 230 of 801 cases was field-of-view truncation (228 of 236 unmatched ribs touch the top slice), and excluding truncated ribs singly still gave 112; excluding by rib number on both sides gives 15 (1.9%), and 57% of ribs are clipped. Counting non-rib-bearing vertebrae between the last rib and the sacrum returns 5 in 93.3% of cases but agrees with the radiologist on only 2 of 22, because the labels assign L1 to L5 plus a sacrum by convention, so the count recovers the convention, not the patient. Two mask-based sacral tests (ilium contact with L5, sacroiliac height against the S1 endplate) are proposed as a second anchor; v5 is a local tree on the grid and nothing is pushed to HuggingFace.
- Key claims:
  - (measured) The review reduced rib numbering errors from 170 to 109, a 36% drop.
  - (measured) The corrections are directional: the -1 arm fell 45% while +1 barely moved, which a noise reshuffle would not do.
  - (measured) The review preserved anomalies rather than smoothing them: lumbar ribs rose 21 to 25.
  - (measured) The raw rib-count asymmetry of 230 cases was field-of-view truncation, not anatomy: in 228 of 236 unmatched ribs the present rib touches the top slice.
  - (inferred) Truncated ribs must be excluded by rib number on both sides, since excluding them one at a time manufactures asymmetry.
  - (measured) 57% of ribs in the dataset are clipped by the field of view, so any rib-count phenotype must use the paired uncut subset.
  - (measured) The non-rib-bearing count returns 5 in 93.3% of cases but agrees with the radiologist on only 2 of 22 overlapping cases.
  - (inferred) The count fails because the label volume assigns L1 to L5 plus a sacrum regardless of true anatomy, so it recovers the labelling convention.
  - (inferred) The 93.3 / 3.3 / 3.3 distribution should not be quoted as a phenotype prevalence.
  - (measured) A 10 mm proximity criterion marked L1 rib-bearing in 61 cases where strict incidence found only 14 lumbar ribs; nearest-vertebra assignment reconciled them.
  - (measured) The merge is anatomy-pure: each cohort writes only its own id range (ribs 34-57, thoracic 8-19).
  - (measured) The spine ledger under the personal namespace is on label scheme v2, and overlaying it on a v4 base would have silently relabelled vertebrae.
- Numbers (48 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 802 cases: cases in the audit cohort
  - 11,579 ribs, n = 802 cases: ribs in the cohort
  - 169 rib review cases: rib corrections merged into v5
  - 107 spine review cases, n = 113: thoracic spine additions merged into v5
  - 0 rejects: review cases rejected
  - 34-57 label ids: id range the rib cohort writes
  - 8-19 label ids: id range the thoracic cohort writes
  - 29 cases, n = 802: cases with both rib and spine corrections
  - 555 cases, n = 802: cases unchanged from v4
  - 170 -> 109 numbering errors: ribs on a different thoracic level than claimed, v4 to v5
- Datasets: CTSpinoPelvic1K (anonymous-mlhc/CTSpinoPelvic1K@v4), CTSpinoPelvic1K v5 (local tree data/v5_final on the grid), Lumbosac.csv (radiologist's non-rib-bearing vertebra count), the spine ledger under the personal namespace (source_revision v2); tools: qc_rib_vertebra_incidence.py, qc_vertebra_grouping.py, analyze_rib_incidence.py, analyze_vertebra_grouping.py, slurm/build_v5.sh, HuggingFace (dataset branch, not yet pushed), Slurm; models: none named.
- Decisions:
  - Truncated ribs are excluded from counts by rib number on both sides, never individually., the author: Excluding truncated ribs one at a time manufactures the asymmetry it is meant to remove (112 asymmetric cases remained).
  - A vertebra is rib-bearing only when it is that rib's single nearest vertebra, not merely within 10 mm., the author: Proximity is not articulation: a 12th rib head is within 10 mm of both T12 and L1, marking L1 rib-bearing in 61 cases against 14 lumbar ribs found by strict...
  - Indeterminate cases (20) are excluded rather than undercounted., the author: A missing level would fabricate a sacralization.
  - The 93.3 / 3.3 / 3.3 count distribution is not to be quoted as a phenotype prevalence., the author: It is largely the label convention: labels assign L1 to L5 plus a sacrum regardless of true anatomy.
  - A vertebra anchors a rib only at 6,000 voxels or more; rib incidence is within 10 mm; a misnumbered case is 3 or more ribs at one consistent delta., the author: An FOV-edge sliver cannot claim a rib whose true vertebra is out of view; 10 mm separates the right vertebra from an off-by-one neighbour; one stray offset i...
  - The paper's framing: record evidence and disagreement rather than a name, since a lumbosacral field of view cannot resolve vertebral identity in principle., the author: There is no imaging gold standard for vertebral identity on a lumbosacral field; counting from C2 on whole-spine imaging is the only definitive method.
  - Next: two mask-based sacral tests (ilium contact with the L5 transverse process; sacroiliac articulation height against the labelled S1 endplate), calibrated where rib and sacral anchors agree., the author: Both fail for different reasons than rib counting, so where they agree numbering is settled and where they disagree the transitional cohort triages itself.
- Open questions: Whether the 15 remaining asymmetric cases (1.9%) are genuine asymmetry; they are flagged for a human look. / Whether the 10 mm incidence threshold cuts through a mode or between two (tested in panel C). / Whether the two proposed sacral-boundary tests work; neither is computed yet. / When v5 is pushed to a HuggingFace branch; it is only a local tree on the grid.

### 39. Registration-Free Level ID Feasibility

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/registration-free-level-id-feasibility-uramo1`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: URAmo1Fa5rGj9zssVgmZGP; page, feasibility; date: 2026-09-15 (Subtitle line 'Deep research . 102 agents . adversarially verified . 15 September 2026'.); confidentiality: schwingnet-other.
- Question: Is a registration-free single-frame vertebral level classifier feasible on real radiographs or fluoroscopy, given the published evidence?
- Summary: A deep-research page (102 agents, each claim voted on by three verifiers, two refutations killing a claim) that answers whether a registration-free vertebral level classifier is feasible. Its verdict is narrow: no surviving claim gives a per-level identification accuracy for a registration-free single-frame system on real radiographs or fluoroscopy; the only registration-free labeller in scope (Huang et al. 2022, trained on images synthesised from 297 CTs and tested on 50 AP + 50 lateral cadaveric Long-Film images from 13 specimens) survived only as a protocol description, its 88.3% and 3.3 mm figures refuted. What is established is a ceiling on the harder sub-task: a single lateral lumbar film can flag LSTV presence (ResNet-50 on 3,116 films: accuracy 76.4%, sensitivity 85.1%, specificity 61.9%, AUC 0.84, below a specialist at 83.9%) but cannot assign a Castellvi type (radiograph readers 32% sensitivity, 42% accuracy; 35.2% of AP subtypes disagree with CT), and clinicians miscount levels in 54% (38/71) of LSTV patients on limited-field imaging. The comparator LevelCheck needs a preoperative CT and 3D-2D registration, placed labels within the correct level on all 398 radiographs after excluding 21, was judged helpful in 42.2% of cases (thoracic 77.7%, lumbar 33.5%) and has never shown a reduction in wrong-level error. Eight findings survived; the realistic promise is a binary LSTV/TLTV/atypical-count flag with abstention when the sacrum, odontoid or twelfth rib is out of field. Four open questions and a caveats paragraph on coverage gaps close the page.
- Key claims:
  - (inferred) No verified evidence establishes per-level vertebra-identification accuracy for a registration-free single-frame system on real radiographs or fluoroscopy.
  - (reported) Level determination without a whole-spine reference fails systematically in transitional anatomy.
  - (reported) Clinicians nominate the wrong level in about half of LSTV patients on limited-field imaging.
  - (reported) Limited field of view is a named failure mode for any single-frame method; the remedy is counting from C2 and comparing with preoperative CT/MRI.
  - (reported) A single lateral lumbar radiograph supports only binary LSTV detection at modest accuracy.
  - (reported) Castellvi typing from a single plain radiograph is unreliable even for trained readers.
  - (reported) Radiograph readers were consistently rather than randomly wrong about Castellvi type, mostly under-calling fusion.
  - (reported) No single landmark visible on one frame names a vertebra reliably, and numerical variants defeat local counting.
  - (reported) LevelCheck is registration-based, requiring the patient's preoperative CT or MRI, and placed labels within the correct level in all evaluated cases after exclusions.
  - (reported) LevelCheck's clinical evidence is confidence support, not a measured reduction in wrong-level surgery.
  - (reported) Surgeons found automated labelling least useful in the lumbar spine, where LSTV lives.
  - (inferred) Several of LevelCheck's published per-region fractions do not reproduce their percentages.
- Numbers (68 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 102 agents: size of the deep-research run
  - 8 findings: claims that survived verification
  - 3 verifiers per claim: verification rule; two refutations kill a claim
  - 297 CTs: CT scans Huang et al. 2022 synthesised training images from
  - 50 AP + 50 lateral Long-Film images, n = 13 cadaveric specimens: Huang et al. 2022 test set
  - 88.3 % labelling accuracy: Huang 2022 figure that did NOT survive verification
  - 3.3 mm: Huang 2022 localisation figure that did not survive verification
  - > 96 % F1: Huang 2022 F1, refuted
  - 11,805 simulated Long-Film images, n = 370 CTs: SPIE 2022 precursor training set, tested on 50 images of 14 cadavers
  - 3,116 standing lateral radiographs: Kwak 2025 whole dataset, single Korean centre
- Datasets: Kwak 2025 single-centre Korean set of 3,116 standing lateral lumbar radiographs (1,941 LSTV / 1,175 normal; labels from MRI report text), Huang 2022 training set synthesised from 297 CTs; cadaveric Long-Film test set (50 AP + 50 lateral, 13 specimens), SPIE 2022 precursor: 11,805 simulated Long-Film images from 370 CTs, 50 images of 14 cadavers, LevelCheck 2016 evaluation set: 398 intraoperative radiographs from 178 patients, Hanhivaara 2024 cohort: 852 screened, 100 LSTV, Hou 2020 cohort: 91 CT-confirmed Castellvi II-IV, Nakagawa 2017 cohort: 550 consecutive lumbar-surgery patients, No public or licensable intraoperative fluoroscopy dataset with level labels was found; tools: LevelCheck (3D-2D registration, normalised-gradient-information metric, multi-start CMA-ES), PubMed search, O-arm Long-Film slot-scan geometry; models: MVMS R-CNN (Huang et al. 2022, multi-perspective region-based CNN), ResNet-50 (Kwak, Ro, Kang 2025), DINOv2 and CLIP (compared by Kwak 2025 on the test set), LevelCheck (Otake 2013, De Silva 2016/2017, Manbachi 2018).
- Decisions:
  - Huang et al. 2022's accuracy figures (88.3% labelling, F1 > 96%, 3.3 mm, lateral 35 points worse) are refuted (0-3) and not asserted on the page. (2026-09-15), the verifiers: associated accuracy claims 0-3 refuted; full text paywalled
  - The 'registration-based tools report near-perfect accuracy' summary (Otake 99.99%, Lo 100%/99.3%, Vaishnav 100%) is voted down. (2026-09-15), the verifiers
  - The claim that radiograph kappa 0.16-0.32 makes plain-film labels unusable as ground truth is voted down 1-2. (2026-09-15), the verifiers: the kappa values themselves are in the abstract
  - Refuted claims are absent from the page; numbers are as reported by the cited papers. (2026-09-15): two refutations kill a claim
- Open questions: What per-level accuracy does a registration-free CNN reach on real (not cadaveric, not Long-Film) C-arm fluoroscopy and plain films, by region, and how much... / Can local morphology on a single AP or lateral frame distinguish L6 from L5, T13 from L1, or a lumbarised S1 from L5 when the sacrum or twelfth rib is in the... / Which public or licensable datasets contain real intraoperative spine fluoroscopy or radiographs with whole-spine-verified level labels and transitional-anat... / Does a level-confirmation alert change surgeon behaviour or wrong-level rates in a prospective trial? None has been reported. / The sim-to-real gap for real C-arm frames from DRR training remains unquantified in this evidence set.

### 40. FluoroCap Overnight Report

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/fluorocap-overnight-report-xnhgdo`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: XNhGDo7nZCyswabMVcydAL; page, report; date: 2026-09-15 (Header line: "Overnight report . read at 06:25, updated 08:30 on 15 September 2026, grid time"; the before row of the...); confidentiality: mixed.
- Question: What did the FluoroCap pipeline finish, lose, fix and leave running overnight into 15 September 2026, and which corpus and level-model numbers changed?
- Summary: An overnight status page for the FluoroCap pipeline, read at 06:25 and updated 08:30 grid time on 15 September 2026, reporting what finished, what still runs, what broke and the numbers that changed, all read off result files. Round one of the publisher refetch replaced 1,489 of 2,901 figures with larger copies (median 6.3x the pixels), raising the median panel short side from 231 to 320 px and the share at or above 512 px from 7.9% to 23.4%; round two was still running after a WSL disk outage, and Wolters Kluwer, Ovid and Thieme remained blocked pending proxy sessions. The all-spine v1 level model learned the thoracic levels the 14 Sep audit found missing (T10 from 0.0% to 95.8% on CT renders), but its 92.7% all-level render score is called a training-set score; the v2 model, retrained after a link step destroyed its first weights, scores 64.2% on held-out renders, and on six-lumbar spines the models still shift every lumbar name down by one, which the v2 bank's rare-anatomy oversampling and a decoder that inserts T13 or L6 are built to fix. Synthetic frame generation by SDXL img2img was dropped after it erased the vertebrae, the CUT translator's first run was declared invalid for training toward contaminated panels, and a content-addressed model registry replaced ln -sf linking. The page ends with two requests to the owner about proxy cookie exports.
- Key claims:
  - (measured) Round one of the publisher refetch replaced 1,489 of 2,901 figures with larger copies at a median 6.3x the pixels.
  - (measured) After round one the median panel short side rose from 231 to 320 px and panels at or above 512 px went from 7.9% to 23.4%.
  - (unverified) Whether the splitter now over-splits the larger figures is unchecked, and the new panels have not yet been decoded.
  - (measured) The campus proxy session made SAGE, Wiley and JNS reachable; Wolters Kluwer, Ovid and Thieme still bounce to the proxy login page.
  - (measured) The all-spine v1 model fixed the audit's failure: T2 to T11 had zero training crops and are now learned; T10 went from 0.0% to 95.8% on CT renders.
  - (measured) The v1 all-level render score of 92.7% was measured on renders of training cases and should be read as a training-set score; the honest held-out render number for v2 is 64.2%.
  - (measured) The v2 model's first weights were destroyed two minutes after being written by a link step that ran ln -sf on a file whose name was already the link's name.
  - (measured) On six-lumbar spines the all-spine v1 model shifts every lumbar name down by one, reading the column as five lumbar bodies above the sacrum.
  - (measured) With v2 held out, L6 single-crop reaches 63.0% but the caption-free decoder still calls only 39.8% of them L6, so the rare-column penalty is set too high.
  - (measured) Nothing has yet taught the model a thirteenth thoracic vertebra: T13 scored 0 of 16, and the corpus has no real panel labelled T13 or L6.
  - (measured) SDXL image-to-image has no usable strength: at 0.2 it leaves the render unchanged and at 0.35 it already smears end-plates and pedicles, so img2img is dropped.
  - (measured) The CUT translator's first run is not a test of translation because its target panels were contaminated: the modality classifier calls only 2,991 of 18,119 panels spine radiographs.

### 41. FluoroCap Dataset Card

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/fluorocap-dataset-card-xwd4wx`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: XWD4wxc9ufnki6FCB8pEGQ; page, dataset-card; date: 2026-09-14 (Script data: "generated": "2026-09-14 09:43"; the page says it is "generated from the manifest, not typed" and "Numbe...); confidentiality: mixed.
- Question: What is the FluoroCap collection of published intra-operative spine fluoroscopy and radiograph panels with level-naming captions, how was it assembled and licensed, and where does its reference standard stand?
- Summary: FluoroCap is a prospectively defined, reproducible collection of published intra-operative spine fluoroscopy and radiograph figure panels whose captions name the vertebral level, built under a written search-and-inclusion protocol (query strings in eleven languages) for validating vertebral-level identification; one manifest (10,281 rows, manifest version 1.1, dataset version 0.2) yields Dataset A (all open-access tiers, research use) and Dataset B (CC BY, CC0 and public domain only). Each source figure is fetched, hashed, split into panels at the gutters, re-encoded from pixel data with no EXIF or DICOM metadata, hashed again and written as one manifest row carrying source, licence tier, fetch record, modality, levels, devices, population sentences, geometry, inclusion or exclusion reason and subgroup flags; four inclusion criteria (I1 to I4) hold, E1 (no level named) and E5 (classifier below 0.3) exclude, and cadaver/phantom (E4) and licence (E8) are flags only at this version. The reference standard is pending: the level attached to each panel is the author's caption plus a constraint decoder combining caption, named hardware and contiguity with the model's proposal, a proposal for the readers, not ground truth; the protocol calls for two blind readers, third-reader adjudication and locking before any performance claim. Figure mates agree on region 88% of the time with the decoder against 52% for the model alone. v0.1 was frozen; the working manifest will be frozen as v1 with reader labels; version 0.3 (video frames, archive scans, teaching-case sites) is in progress.
- Key claims:
  - (measured) Two releases come from one manifest: Dataset A (all open-access licence tiers, research use) and Dataset B (CC BY, CC0 and public-domain panels only).
  - (measured) The working manifest holds 10,281 rows at manifest version 1.1 and dataset version 0.2, generated 2026-09-14 09:43.
  - (measured) Every panel is re-encoded from pixel data with no EXIF or DICOM metadata, and no patient record, DICOM header or institutional image was accessed.
  - (measured) Inclusion needs all four of: open-access source, caption states fluoroscopy/C-arm/intra-operative or plain radiography, caption names the spine, a vertebral level named.
  - (measured) E1 (no level named) and E5 (classifier below 0.3) are applied exclusions; E4 (cadaver/phantom) and E8 (licence) are flags, not exclusions, at this version.
  - (measured) The detector runs at 640 px and level crops are scaled to a 96 px target height, so working resolution is set by the model, not the file.
  - (measured) No panel with an unresolved licence (tier E, review queue) enters Dataset B.
  - (measured) Reader labels are pending for every panel; the attached level is a caption-plus-decoder proposal, not ground truth.
  - (reported) The protocol specifies two readers blind to each other and the proposal, third-reader adjudication, and locking of dataset, model and analysis plan before any performance claim.
  - (measured) Figure mates agree on region 88% with the decoder, 52% for the model alone.
  - (measured) An unbroken hash chain runs from original figure SHA-256 to panel SHA-256 to the recorded input transform to the weights' SHA-256.
  - (measured) The manifest is regenerated by one command and is byte-identical for identical inputs; releases are frozen with SHA256SUMS and a lock record.
- Numbers (17 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 10281 manifest rows: size of the working manifest the card was generated from
  - 1.1 manifest version: manifest schema version
  - 0.2 dataset version: working dataset version the card reads
  - 2026-09-14 09:43 generation stamp: when the card was generated from the manifest
  - 640 px: resolution the detector runs at
  - 96 px target height: level crop scaling
  - 0.3 classifier score threshold: E5 exclusion: panel classifier below this for 'spine radiograph'
  - four inclusion criteria: all four must hold (I1 to I4)
  - eleven languages of query strings: search protocol
  - two + one readers (two blind, a third adjudicates): reference-standard protocol
- Datasets: FluoroCap Dataset A (research, all open-access tiers), FluoroCap Dataset B (clean: CC BY, CC0, public domain), PMC open-access subset (source aggregator), manifest.csv; tools: tools/fluorocap_report_stats.py, docs/FLUOROCAP_PROTOCOL.md, SHA-256 hashing, SHA256SUMS, lock record, model registry, the panel classifier, the constraint decoder, the implant (hardware) detector; models: the vertebra detector (runs at 640 px), the panel classifier ('spine radiograph' score), the constraint decoder (caption, hardware, contiguity with the model's proposal), the implant detector.
- Decisions:
  - Dataset B holds only licence tiers A and B; no panel with an unresolved licence enters it., the dataset's authors: anything not clearly Creative Commons is tier E and waits for a person to read the licence statement
  - E4 (cadaver/phantom) and E8 (licence) are flags, not exclusions, at this version; species and cadaver/phantom words are review-queue items, not silent exclusions., the dataset's authors
  - The dataset, the model and the analysis plan are locked before any performance claim; two blind readers and a third adjudicator form the reference standard., the protocol: the current caption-plus-decoder level is a proposal, not ground truth
  - Reader labels replace the detector counts in the subgroup table when the reference standard is complete., the dataset's authors

### 42. Level Context Ladder

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/level-context-ladder-xd7eltc`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: Xd7ELTcKW1yXaC7KtKma9z; page, report; date: 2026-09-13 (Header 'Runs of 12-13 September 2026 . updated 13 Sep 11:30'.); confidentiality: schwingnet-method.
- Question: How much of the spine does a model need to see to name a vertebra's exact level, on held-out patients, on real lateral films and on CT projections?
- Summary: A run report on classifiers over 27 levels (C1-L6, T13, sacrum) that scale the target vertebra to a fixed pixel height and shrink the visible context one rung at a time down to a single vertebra, with 30% of patients from every annotated source held out. On real films one vertebra gives 96.7% exact and 99.7% within one level over 8,422 crops; cervical reaches 97.7% (7,900 crops) and lumbar 80.7 to 85.4% (410 crops), rising to 91.9% at three vertebrae. The jump from 84% to 97% came entirely from adding CSXA's 4,963 cervical patients and BUU's 7,200 lumbar films; architecture, absolute size in millimetres, learning rate, epochs and masking moved nothing by more than a few points, and CT-only models transfer 7% to real film. On CT projections the ladder runs from 63.5% at one vertebra to 91.8% with the whole scan, where 80% of the remaining errors are whole-case shifts, so within-one-level is the honest primary metric. An outline of 128 silhouette points matches the image arm on synthetic data. The page says none of this is clinical validation and lists what runs next: the +BUU rungs, VinDr pseudo-labelling as a train-only lumbar source, dual-view fusion and contrastive pretraining.
- Key claims:
  - (measured) One vertebra on real films, held-out patients: 96.7% exact level and 99.7% within one level over 8,422 crops.
  - (measured) Cervical single-vertebra accuracy is 97.7% on 7,900 crops; lumbar is 80.7 to 85.4% on 410 crops and 91.9% at three vertebrae.
  - (measured) The headline moved from 84% to 97% in one night and every point came from data (CSXA and BUU entering training), not from architecture or hyperparameters.
  - (inferred) Lumbar is lower only because about 380 annotated lumbar films exist in training against about 5,000 cervical; the training count, not the model, bounds it.
  - (measured) CT-only models transfer nothing to real film: 7.4% exact at one vertebra on NHANES, with region accuracy below chance.
  - (measured) Raising target resolution from 48 to 96 px lifted lumbar one-vertebra accuracy from 80.7% to 85.4%, the same lever as on CT (73.7% to 82.8%).
  - (measured) BUU's counting-inferred labels, used train-only and never scored, added 2.0 points at one vertebra.
  - (measured) Absolute size in millimetres cost 1.7 points and has never helped in three tries; the +/-1 confusions are not a magnification problem.
  - (measured) Architecture barely matters: ResNet-50 and ConvNeXt-tiny within two points of ResNet-18 on CT; ViT-B/16 47 points worse.
  - (measured) On CT projections the ladder runs from 63.5% (VerSe alone, one vertebra) to 91.8% (full merge, whole scan); with the collimated field at 96 px, 82.8% at one vertebra.
  - (measured) With the whole scan visible, 80% of the remaining errors are whole-case shifts, so the ceiling at wide context is enumeration and within-one-level is the honest primary metric.
  - (measured) A vertebra's outline alone (128 silhouette points) reaches 72.5% exact and 95.0% within one on synthetic cases, equal to the image arm at one vertebra: the identity of a single vertebra lives in its outline.
- Numbers (49 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 96.7 % exact, n = 8,422 crops: one vertebra visible, real films, all regions, held-out patients
  - 99.7 % within one level, n = 8,422 crops: one vertebra, real films, all regions
  - 97.7 % exact, n = 7,900 crops: cervical, one vertebra, CSXA + NHANES
  - 80.7-85.4 % exact, n = 410 crops: lumbar, one vertebra, NHANES (91.9% at three)
  - 7 % exact: CT-only models scored on real film
  - 84->97 % exact: the headline number's move in one night from data
  - 4,963 patients: CSXA cervical patients (C3-C7) entering training
  - 7,200 films: BUU lumbar films entering training (7,203 films, AP + lateral; laterals used)
  - ~380 films: lumbar films with annotated levels in training, against ~5,000 cervical
  - 30 % of patients: held-out share from every annotated source
- Datasets: VerSe, CTSpinoPelvic1K (801 readable cases), NHANES II (543 films, 364 patients), CSXA (4,963 patients, cervical), BUU-LSPINE (7,203 films, train only), VinDr-SpineXR (5,136 lumbar films, pipeline running), AASCE (481, not used), Mendeley (591, not used); tools: tools/collect_context_curve.py, ~/tmp/ladder_summary.json, spinesurg-ct-nnunet/tools/make_drr_dataset.py, make_context_crops.py, train_level_classifier.py, train_view_classifier.py, pseudolabel_films.py, make_contours.py, train_contour_pointnet.py, pandas, the grid (Slurm jobs 40215523, 40215525), corner detector, view classifier; models: ResNet-18, ResNet-50, ConvNeXt-tiny, ViT-B/16, contour PointNet (128 silhouette points), three-vertebra film model (99.1%), view classifier, Multi-View (MICCAI 2023) with Sequence Loss (prior work, left out), SpineCLUE (prior work).
- Decisions:
  - BUU's counting-inferred labels are admitted as a train-only source, never scored; the test set is annotated levels only. (2026-09-13): Excluded from the first report on principle; admitted once the test set was annotated-only, and it helped (+2.0 points).
  - The Sequence Loss of the closest prior work is left out of every arm. (2026-09-13): It re-injects the counting prior.
  - Every real-film arm trains on films. (2026-09-13): CT alone does not transfer: 7.4% exact on NHANES, and soft-tissue projection still reaches only a third of what real films in training give.
  - Within-one-level is the honest primary metric for a level-confirmation tool. (2026-09-13): At wide context the ceiling is enumeration (whole-case shifts), not local morphology.
  - VinDr laterals are to be pseudo-labelled and admitted as a second train-only lumbar source. (2026-09-13): The next lumbar step is more lumbar films with levels; the training count bounds the lumbar number.
  - The view classifier's r.view bug (pandas Series.view) was fixed to r["view"] and the run relaunched at 11:05. (2026-09-13): Every training film had been labelled AP, so the first run labelled zero films lateral.
- Open questions: Whether the three- and five-vertebra rungs of the +BUU arm (relaunched, job 40215523) hold the one-vertebra gain. / Whether VinDr pseudo-labelled laterals raise the lumbar single-vertebra bar. / Whether dual-view fusion on BUU's paired AP + lateral films adds an untested information source. / Whether supervised-contrastive pretraining of the encoder on the crop bank buys a few points.

### 43. REFORMS compliance checklist

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/reforms-compliance-checklist-bpzdoi`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: BPzdoiEZ14Vc6Yn7fTTz72; docs, checklist; date: 2026-10-03 (Header line under the title: "Oct 3, 2026 . @[the owner]"); confidentiality: symphony-beethoven.
- Question: Which of the 32 REFORMS reporting items can be enforced as an automated gate in the hpcworker and Beethoven pipeline, where each gate fires, and which repository owns it?
- Summary: The checklist maps all 32 REFORMS items (Kapoor et al.) to a gate, finding 20 verifiable by code and 12 only enforceable as a declared non-empty field in study.yaml. Each gate fires before dispatch, after the run, before publish, or as a declared field, and none exists yet: both repositories are the Remote Control launcher (hpcworker framework 11, extension 12.0.0). Ownership splits three ways because the framework is held to bash 4.2 and Python 3.6 stdlib on the CentOS 7 login node while Hydra, MONAI, W&B and CodeCarbon can only live in the container image: hpcworker takes the run manifest, Run ID, clean-tree refusal and publish lock; a proposed beethoven-research repository takes the schema, splitter, leakage checks, bootstrap CIs and card builders; the extension takes a read-only checklist view and the human Publish approval, through a per-run contract file reforms.json. Four conflicts with CLAUDE.md are listed with fixes (Run ID must not hash Slurm variables, publishing waits for a human click, tokens in a mode-0600 file, LiteLLM key rotation is out), three questions are left for the owner, and a seven-step build order starts with the two gates that need no ML libraries.
- Key claims:
  - (inferred) All 32 REFORMS items can be tied to a gate, but only 20 are verifiable by code; 12 can only be required as a non-empty written field.
  - (inferred) None of the gates exists yet; both repositories are the Remote Control launcher with no Hydra, run hashing, splitting, CI, CodeCarbon, LaTeX or archiving code.
  - (unverified) The gates split three ways because the framework is held to bash 4.2 and Python 3.6 stdlib while most gates need libraries only the container can carry.
  - (reported) The item list comes from the REFORMS paper and its module names were confirmed on the project site.
  - (inferred) Hashing volatile Slurm variables into the Run ID makes every rerun a new ID and defeats the reproduction script item.
  - (inferred) Zenodo and Hugging Face publishing are permanent, so the publish action must wait for the student's click, never the agent.
  - (reported) Energy reporting is not a REFORMS item and rides on item 2c as an addition.
  - (unverified) A workflow engine such as Metaflow cannot run on the login node, so its scheduler would have to run inside a job.
  - (inferred) The seam between the gate runner and the panel is one contract file per run, reforms.json, with item id, state and evidence path.
- Numbers (14 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 32 REFORMS items: REFORMS items that can each be tied to a gate
  - 20 items, n = 32: items whose gate can be verified by code
  - 12 items, n = 32: items enforceable only as a declared non-empty field
  - 11 framework version: hpcworker framework version at the time of writing
  - 12.0.0 extension version: beethoven-extension version at the time of writing
  - 4 firing points: points at which a gate can fire (before dispatch, after the run, before publish, declared)
  - 4.2 bash version: bash version the framework is held to on the login node
  - 3.6 Python version: Python stdlib version the framework is held to
  - 3 owners: ways the gate ownership splits
  - 0600 file mode: mode of the file holding Zenodo and Hugging Face tokens
- Datasets: none named; tools: hpcworker (framework/, jobs/build_image.sbatch), beethoven-extension, beethoven-research (proposed), Hydra, MONAI, Weights and Biases (W&B), CodeCarbon, LaTeX, Zenodo, Hugging Face, Metaflow, Slurm (sbatch, sacct), git, Remote Control, claude -p, LiteLLM, MetaTensor, DICOM; models: none named.
- Decisions:
  - Run ID hashes config, commit SHA, image digest, lockfile and split file; Slurm variables are recorded beside the ID, never in it (proposed, and assumed by the checklist).: Hashing SLURM_JOB_ID or node names makes every rerun a new ID, which defeats the reproduction script item (2e).
  - Zenodo and Hugging Face gates only prepare a draft deposition and card; the publish action waits for the student's click in the panel, never the agent.: Publishing is permanent and outward; a DOI cannot be withdrawn.
  - Zenodo and Hugging Face tokens live in a mode-0600 file the job reads, never in sbatch --export, logs, the manifest or the extension.: The no-secrets-on-argv rule.
  - LiteLLM key rotation is not part of the checklist.: Rotating several accounts' keys breaks 'one person, one account' and the no-credentials rule.
  - The extension panel only reads files the job writes and never holds a Zenodo or Hugging Face token.: The panel's contract: it reads files the job writes.
  - Build order starts with the hpcworker run manifest, Run ID and clean-tree refusal, stdlib only, with tests.: Every later gate writes into the manifest those two gates create.
- Open questions: New repository beethoven-research, or a research/ folder in hpcworker? / Metaflow, or the framework's own job chaining, given that a workflow engine cannot run on the login node? / Does the interactive Remote Control worker launch gated runs, or only headless claude -p jobs?
- Stale markers: None of these gates exists yet / both repositories today are the Remote Control launcher (hpcworker framework 11, extension 12.0.0) / proposed beethoven-research repository / three unticked open-question checkboxes for the owner

### 45. OSC Outreach Funding

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/osc-outreach-funding-kak2gk`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: KaK2gKBYF5vcPoJuSxtoyf; page, playbook; date: 2026-09 (Closing line: "Amounts, deadlines and contacts here were read off each funder's own pages in September 2026". Later d...); confidentiality: personal-outreach.
- Question: Where can a currently enrolled medical student, with the resident as mentor, ask for money to run OpenSpineConsortium outreach, ordered by how fast it can arrive, and what will each source pay for?
- Summary: A briefing for a medical student in the OSC research year listing twelve funding sources ordered by speed of arrival: local small awards (WSUSOM Student Senate $250 to $400 with a $450 ceiling and no committee cycle; the Student Activities Funding Board for on-campus free public events; the Office of Medical Student Research Scholarly Activity Award up to $500 with a 3-day post-acceptance fast path; SOM Alumni Association funds via Student Affairs), months-long applications (WSU GME Seed Grant up to $2,500, which the resident must apply for and which alone covers materials; the AOA Service Leadership grant $9,000 over two years, deadline 16 Feb 2027; the Community Foundation for Southeast Michigan, with a $70,000 precedent to a WSU student group), and the radiology societies whose cycles open in October 2026 with deadlines around 1 February (RSNA Research Medical Student Grant $3,000 plus a $3,000 departmental match; RSNA Education Project Award $10,000 to $20,000; the Radiology Education Research Development Grant up to $10,000; SIR student awards; the RSNA Education Scholar Grant up to $75,000 as a later destination). Two structural facts shape it: the resident cannot be the applicant on student awards, and OSC is not a Registered Student Organization, so an existing registered organization should host events with OSC as the program. Every application should cite OSC's record: 802 CT records annotated and openly released, 13 medical students trained as annotators, a peer-reviewed Cureus paper on the education model and two permanent archives. The immediate steps are to confirm enrolment, ask Student Affairs about alumni funds (which makes the Senate request admissible), and settle RSNA membership and the departmental match before October.
- Key claims:
  - (inferred) The list is ordered by how fast money can arrive, not by amount; the first seven are local and small and the radiology societies' window opens next month.
  - (inferred) Nearly every award requires a currently enrolled medical student; a research or gap year usually keeps enrolment but must be verified with the Registrar.
  - (unverified) The resident cannot be the applicant on any student award, and OSC is not a Registered Student Organization.
  - (reported) The Student Senate funding request is $250 to $400 with a $450 ceiling, no posted deadline and no committee, but positions itself as the last resort after alumni and mentor funds.
  - (reported) SAFB funds registered organizations' on-campus events but requires the event to be on campus and free and open to the public for its whole duration.
  - (reported) The Scholarly Activity Award (up to $500) has a 60-day lead time and a fast path of three days after an abstract acceptance; it funds travel to present, continental US and Canada only.
  - (reported) The WSU GME Seed Grant (up to $2,500) is the one source that pays for materials, software and printing; it prohibits travel, registration and publication fees and requires IRB documentation, a faculty mentor's approval and a mentoring plan.
  - (reported) The AOA Medical Student Service Leadership Project Grant is $9,000 over two years ($5,000 then $4,000), deadline 16 Feb 2027, one proposal per institution every three years, needing the Dean's endorsement and matching funds.
  - (reported) A WSU School of Medicine student group won $70,000 over two years from the Community Foundation for Southeast Michigan for STEM outreach, the precedent for OSC.
  - (reported) RSNA's 2026-27 cycles are closed; the next open in October 2026 with medical student deadlines around 1 February.
  - (reported) The RSNA Research Medical Student Grant is $3,000 matched by the sponsoring department to $6,000, with up to ten additional grants for students underrepresented in medicine.
  - (reported) The RSNA Education Project Award ($10,000 to $20,000, one year) is the closest fit, but needs an RSNA member applicant, realistically the resident or a faculty sponsor.
- Numbers (28 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - $250-$400 dollars: WSUSOM Student Senate funding request, typical amount
  - $450 dollars ceiling: Student Senate ceiling
  - up to $500 dollars: Office of Medical Student Research Scholarly Activity Award, per student
  - $3,500 dollars: group rate for organization travel under the Scholarly Activity Award
  - 60 days lead time: Scholarly Activity Award standard lead time
  - 3 days post-acceptance: Scholarly Activity Award fast path when an abstract is accepted fewer than 60 days before the event
  - up to $2,500 dollars: WSU GME Seed Grant, applicant the resident
  - $9,000 dollars over 2 years: AOA Medical Student Service Leadership Project Grant
  - $5,000 then $4,000 dollars by year: AOA grant structure
  - 16 Feb 2027 deadline: AOA grant deadline
- Datasets: OSC's released CT annotation dataset (802 CT records; Zenodo DOI and a public dataset mirror); tools: none named; models: none named.
- Decisions:
  - The student, not the resident, applies for every student award; the resident applies only for the GME Seed Grant and, realistically, the RSNA Education Project Award., the briefing's author: the resident cannot be the applicant on any student award
  - Have an existing Registered Student Organization host the event with OSC as the program rather than registering OSC., the briefing's author: OSC is not a Registered Student Organization, which several sources require, and this is the fastest way around it
  - If the outreach cannot be staged as an open on-campus workshop, skip SAFB rather than reshape the program., the briefing's author: SAFB requires the event to be on campus and free and open to the public for its whole duration
  - The RSNA Education Scholar Grant is not a target this year; it is listed as a destination., the briefing's author: it supports individuals with an established record in radiologic education
  - Apply to SIR only if a real line to interventional radiology can be drawn; prefer one application done properly over several done thinly., the briefing's author: OSC is spine imaging and morphometry, not interventional radiology

### 48. Level Verification Bench

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/level-verification-bench-mkjxsy`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: MKJXSYgzFBa5ndLK8TVsKo; page, bench; date: undated (Neither index.html nor the data file verify_data.js carries a date, run stamp or 'as of' line; the data rows hold no...); confidentiality: schwingnet-method.
- Question: Is the vertebral level name the model gave each numbered box on a random sample of decoded panels right, wrong or undecidable when a person looks at the image?
- Summary: An interactive bench that shows a random sample of decoded fluoroscopy panels, 179 panels holding 865 numbered vertebra boxes, and asks a person to mark each model-given level name as right, wrong or can't tell, typing the correct level where known. The page says the sample is random within each route and drawn with a fixed seed so the result is an unbiased estimate of accuracy, and that a set chosen by the model's own uncertainty could not serve that way; it calls the sample the measurement, not training data. Each panel card shows the route, whether the decode was caption-constrained or caption-free, what decided the names (model, caption, mate, hardware, vlm or anchor) and whether the panel was read bottom-up; a key marks a whole panel right at once, and verdicts save to the page's document store as verdicts/&lt;id>. The export carries the page and its 4.2 MB data file but no verdicts: the counters stand at 0 / 0 and the accuracy reads as a dash.
- Key claims:
  - (measured) The sample is 179 decoded panels with 865 vertebra boxes and is the measurement, not training data.
  - (inferred) Because the sample is random within each route with a fixed seed, the verified accuracy is an unbiased estimate; a set chosen by the model's own uncertainty could not be used this way.
  - (unverified) A whole panel being correct is expected to be the common case, so one key marks every box on a panel right.
  - (measured) Accuracy is computed as right over (right + wrong), leaving can't-tell marks out of the denominator.
  - (measured) Verdicts persist only where the page has a store; without one they do not persist.
- Numbers (10 recorded; the first 10 here):

  - 865 vertebrae (numbered boxes), n = 179 panels: Boxes to be verified across the sample; the page sums n_boxes over the rows

  - 108 constrained, 71 free panels by caption constraint, n = 179: How many panels' decodes were caption-constrained (hard_caption true) versus caption-free, tallied from the data rows

  - 11 boxes on the first panel, n = 1 panel: FLUO-000001, route commons, caption-constrained, decided by model, labels T1 through T11
  - 0.0 to 0.589 confidence per box, n = 11 boxes: Per-box confidences on the first panel, two of them 0.0

- Decisions:
  - Verify a random sample drawn within each route with a fixed seed, not a set chosen by the model's own uncertainty, so the accuracy is an unbiased estimate.: a set chosen by the model's own uncertainty could not be used this way
  - The sample is the measurement and is not training data.
  - Can't-tell marks are left out of the accuracy denominator.

### 49. SchwingNet Figures

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/schwingnet-figures-jshksq`; account: gschwing; link id: JSHksQ6Tgad2w8cwJmaDZT; figures, figures; date: 2026-09-22 (Header line 'spinesurg-ct-nnunet . paper/rsnai/figures . 22 September 2026, 20:12'; the composite was stacked at 19:3...); confidentiality: schwingnet-method.
- Question: Which SchwingNet manuscript figures exist on disk on the evening of 22 September 2026, which rows are current after the algebraic rule was removed, and what does each row and audit figure show?
- Summary: A gallery of every figure on disk at 20:12 on 22 September 2026 in spinesurg-ct-nnunet's paper/rsnai/figures on branch L6_Class: the tall composite fig1_story_tall.png (stacked at 19:31 by tools/fig_story_compose.py, not yet restacked), ten current row images (a to j) and three audit figures. Two rows (anatomy, fluoroscopic localisation) were re-rendered at 20:12 to remove the algebraic rule annotations. The captions describe the method: the off-by-one ambiguity of a lumbarised S1; the chain expose, detect, link, chain, count, confirm with only the shaded boxes learned; the count walking up two real VerSe columns (verse108 with 23 bodies, verse547 with 24 and a thirteenth thoracic); a four-exposure sweep stitched and counted from the sacrum; consecutive-window matching whose millimetre values are under review because verse_drr spacing is suspected about 15% short (checklist item A8); confirmation on a render with a 0.18 margin over one body off; fluoroscopic localisation readout; confirmation on Cios Fusion-styled frames finding a sixth lumbar vertebra at a weak 0.01 margin; the training corpus pictograms; the detector (1024 px, CSP backbone, PAN over three scales, decoupled head) and localiser (96 px crop plus target channel, ResNet-18, BiGRU over two to nine bodies, 27-level posterior); and a region-occlusion ablation at L3. The audit figures cover which training data contributes (the DeepFluoro question, text in SchwingNet/results/DATA_CONTRIBUTION.md), per-device C-arm card sampling with its saturation spikes, and the share of the learned joint distribution a frame reached (1.7-5.1% at the clip width in code, 99% with the typicality gate).
- Key claims:
  - (measured) Two rows (anatomy, fluoroscopic localisation) were re-rendered at 20:12 to remove the algebraic rule; the composite stacked at 19:31 is not yet restacked.
  - (inferred) The same three-body window holds L4.L3.L2 in an ordinary spine and L5.L4.L3 in one with a lumbarised S1, the off-by-one that becomes wrong-level surgery.
  - (measured) Row c counts up two real VerSe columns: verse108 with 23 bodies and verse547 with 24 including a thirteenth thoracic, with the extra body now marked directly instead of by a rule.
  - (inferred) The millimetre values in the overlap row come from verse_drr, whose spacing is suspected about 15% short by the row-spacing defect of 19 September, so none is quotable yet.
  - (measured) Confirmation on a render locates the body with a correlation margin of 0.18 over one body off.
  - (measured) On frames styled by the Cios Fusion card a sixth lumbar vertebra is counted from the sacrum with a margin of 0.01, which the page calls weak.
  - (measured) The two networks are a detector (1024 px, CSP backbone, PAN over three scales, decoupled head) and a localiser (96 px crop plus target channel, ResNet-18, BiGRU over two to nine bodies, 27-level posterior).
  - (measured) In the region-occlusion ablation at L3 the target body and the neighbour above matter most and end-plates barely.
