### 51. Symphony and Beethoven Taxonomy Spec

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/symphony-and-beethoven-taxonomy-spec-lcbqro`; account: gschwing; link id: Lcbqrojz4uLvqPfvv1DZym; docs, spec; date: 2026-10-15 (Header line under the title: "Oct 15, 2026 . @f"; the as-of verification dates throughout are also 2026-10-15 ("Verif...); confidentiality: symphony-beethoven.
- Question: What kind of software Symphony and Beethoven are, where they sit among their neighbors on a twelve-axis taxonomy, what they add that is unique, what GitHub Actions' free public minutes mean for them, and what is still unproven.
- Summary: The spec places Symphony (a claim protocol, board and workers for Claude Code agents kept in a GitHub repository and run on GitHub Actions, with no server) and Beethoven (protocols, gates and agents for research on Wayne State's Slurm cluster) and the stack they are designed to form, which today meets nowhere in code. It proposes a twelve-axis taxonomy of agent systems, adding the one axis it says no published taxonomy has, cost model and payer, and places both systems and about twenty neighbors on it. It gathers the evidence of need (CooperBench's coordination gap, agent-driven research failures, metered CI costs), words six narrow uniqueness claims U1 to U6 after an adversarial search of 25 candidates, and sets out what GitHub's free unlimited standard runners on public repositories do and do not cover. It closes with fourteen risks, among them a push-guard directory bug found on 2026-10-15 and the pusher-written idle clock (finding F8), and ten research questions R1 to R10 with acceptance criteria. Every claim carries one of the artifact's own grades: Verified, Inferred, Reported or Open.
- Key claims:
  - (reported) Symphony makes one GitHub repository the shared state for any number of Claude Code workers and has no server of its own; the rules are a dependency-free Python library vendored into each repository.
  - (reported) Claude runs in only three workflows on one subscription token; everything else is deterministic code on GITHUB_TOKEN, so a worker with nothing to do starts no Claude session.
  - (measured) The validation harness searched 16.7 million states at depth 8 and 18.8 million with two issues at depth 7 with no violation in the run of 2026-10-14, and 93 mutants prove the checks bite.
  - (measured) Building the harness and reviewing its fixes found 19 defects: six fixed in 0.4.1, eleven on main, one settled by changing the spec, one (F8) waiting on the owner.
  - (reported) Beethoven is mostly unbuilt beyond beethoven-qa; of the research gates only splits.py exists, and Metaflow on Slurm, the fenced lease, the registry and the bus are not built.
  - (reported) Symphony and Beethoven meet nowhere in code today; the stack is the owner's design and its composition is inferred.
  - (inferred) No published taxonomy of agents has an axis for where the compute runs and who pays for it.
  - (reported) CooperBench found one agent passed far more often than two agents that could only message each other: GPT-5 315 against 183 of 652, Claude Sonnet 4.5 307 against 168.
  - (reported) What fails in two-agent runs is duplicated or incompatible work, not skill; a first-turn plan nearly halved the conflict rate.
  - (measured) The owner's own coordbench pilot of 8 pairs shows no significant difference between Symphony's arm and one agent.
  - (measured) The coordbench pilot's token and dollar figures are inflated by a prompt of about 78,000 to 82,000 tokens that every agent inherits.
  - (reported) gh-aw's work queue is Symphony's closest neighbor but grants claims only to Actions runs and refuses the subscription token.
- Numbers (82 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 1,379 tests: symphony-protocol library tests passed on 2026-10-15
  - 581 tests: symphony-template tests passed on 2026-10-15
  - 16.7 million states: validation harness search at depth 8, no violation
  - 18.8 million states: validation harness search with two issues at depth 7
  - 93 mutants: mutants proving the harness's checks bite
  - 19 defects: defects found by building the harness and reviewing fixes
  - 6 defects: defects fixed in release 0.4.1
  - 11 defects: defects fixed on main ahead of the next release
  - 11 workflow files: workflow files beside the library in a whole-template copy
  - 3 workflow files: at most this many workflow files in a protocol-only adoption

### 52. How the Beethoven repositories are encapsulated

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/how-the-beethoven-repositories-are-encapsulated-6h4zq9`; account: [the owner's login]; link id: 6h4ZQ9Tgwu5zqmsLUgkTeT; docs, report; date: 2026-10-07 (Header line "Oct 7, 2026 . @[the owner]"; the body dates the merges, the v2.0.0 tag and the owner's access-service ch...); confidentiality: symphony-beethoven.
- Question: How are Beethoven's seven code repositories sealed from one another, which repository pins what from which, and what still crosses a boundary?
- Summary: The report states that all seven Beethoven code repositories (hpcworker, beethoven-sites, beethoven-extension, beethoven-desktop, beethoven-qa, beethoven-agents, beethoven-research) are now sealed under a five-rule standard merged in the hub as INTERFACES.md: each builds from its own tree, declares its exports and imports in interface/INTERFACE.json, takes another repository's work only as a pinned copy checked against an ORIGIN record, tests only against the published surface, and follows a fixed direction (qa, then agents, then research, never back). A table gives who offers what to whom, each repository's section says how it proves its export is the truth about its code (contract vectors, conformance and boundary tests), and a list names the three entries that still reach another checkout and why. Read-only neighbours (Beethoven for VS Code, the website, the two studies, spinesurg-ct-nnunet) were audited but not changed, with the high-weight crossings listed. The report leaves the owner two decisions: whether the VS Code edition gives up its own grid scripts for hpcworker's, and whether qa's vendor command should fetch through gh. It closes with the pull requests merged and what their gates showed, including fourteen client readers that disagreed with hpcworker's real output and two faults found in CrossHair 0.0.111.
- Key claims:
  - (measured) All seven code repositories the work could change are sealed, each declaring its exports and imports and taking others' work only as pinned, checked copies.
  - (measured) The launcher side and the engine side share data only, never code.
  - (measured) Fourteen client readers disagreed with what the cluster side really writes, found by running hpcworker's vectors through the clients' real readers, all fixed.
  - (measured) qa's CI found two faults in CrossHair 0.0.111 that the local run missed; the patterns were respelled and no contract was loosened.
  - (measured) Three entries in two repositories still reach another checkout, each on a known list in that repository's boundary test so the list can only shrink; none runs in CI or where a student goes.
  - (inferred) The VS Code edition's grid install is a second cluster side next to hpcworker and the largest piece left; everything that runs on the grid should be hpcworker's.
  - (measured) The website renders an outdated version of the VS Code terms because it reads sibling working trees.
  - (measured) hpcworker counts a sign-in with an empty apiProvider as a subscription, a gap in the one fundamental rule.
  - (measured) spinesurg-ct-nnunet's worker container binds the owner's whole lab folder beside the study's data, a high-weight crossing.
  - (measured) A session cannot create tags, so the owner tagged beethoven-qa v2.0.0 by hand.
- Numbers (33 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 7 repositories: Code repositories sealed under the standard
  - 5 rules: Rules of the sealed-repository standard, each checked by every repository on itself
  - 8 export kinds: Kinds an export may have (contract, schema, data, python, cli, files, program, text)
  - 1 version: Version of every export offered today
  - 2 decisions: Decisions left to the owner
  - 55 tests: beethoven-extension's conformance test reading every hpcworker vector
  - 7 readers: Extension readers found disagreeing with the framework, fixed in the same pull request
  - 24 tests: beethoven-desktop's contract test
  - 7 differences: Desktop differences from the contract found and fixed
  - 14 readers: Total client readers that disagreed with the cluster side (7 extension + 7 desktop)
- Datasets: none named; tools: hpcworker (contract, profiles, module-schema, testbed, framework exports; grid_access.sh; framework/tests/vectors.py), beethoven-sites, beethoven-extension, beethoven-desktop, beethoven-qa (python -m beethoven_qa boundary; vendor; vendor-check; the kit; standards.yml), beethoven-agents (bee; accounts.json; usage files), beethoven-research, Beethoven for VS Code (beethoven-vscode; admin.mjs; deploy.sh; wizard.ts), the website (build_site.py; services.json), beethoven-access (the access service on Cloudflare), OpenSpineToolkit, gh, CrossHair 0.0.111, icontract, pull.sh with ORIGIN records, conda environment and container cache (the two studies); models: none named.
- Decisions:
  - The sealed-repository standard with five rules (own tree, one declaration, pinned checked copies, published surface only, direction) is merged in the hub as INTERFACES.md. (2026-10-07), the owner: So another repository reaches a sealed unit only through what it publishes, at a version, through a copy pinned to a merged commit, never its source tree.

### 53. The Lab Recipe

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/the-lab-recipe-1t2gvp`; account: [the owner's login]; link id: 1T2gVPMkKhzU2J4TryUDRG; docs, runbook; date: 2026-09-27 (Header line 'Sep 27, 2026 . @[the owner]'; the closing gap table is headed 'What is not yet true (2026-09-27)'.); confidentiality: mixed.
- Question: How does a lab paper run as a chain of files from a frozen pre-registration through grid jobs and a numbers manifest to the manuscript, and which data rules, grid rules and checklists hold that chain together?
- Summary: A Docs page (kept as Markdown) that sets out how a lab project is staffed (lead, co-lead, members; senior scientist co-PI screens, senior clinical PI confirms clinical reads, the PI signs every freeze) and how a paper is produced so that a number enters the manuscript only through a recorded chain: PREREG.md frozen with its sha256, results files carrying a _run block, numbers_manifest.json, \RESULT{key} macros in the tex, and a reproduce target that fails on MISSING or MOVED. It fixes data rules that never bend (the five-criterion IRB umbrella HPR 2026-269, CTSpinoPelvic1K licences, a private-forever list including level-identification code, FluoroCap, weights, spinesurg-ct-nnunet and XRSpinoPelvic1K, credentials, personal data, label maps, volume orientation, zero-padded ids, two tracks), each with the incident that caused it. Fourteen grid rules with their incidents, a Slurm job template, Nextflow guidance for three-or-more-stage CPU pipelines, a numbers-checking procedure, rules for working with Claude (checkpoint block, no squeue polling beyond ten minutes, status computed by pipeline_state.py), handover and authorship rules, and three checklists follow. It ends with a table of ten gaps that are not yet true as of 2026-09-27, among them 167 bare numerals in the SchwingNet tables and 0 of 79 manifest keys carrying a Slurm job id.
- Key claims:
  - (inferred) A stop-on-bad-input habit would have prevented most of the lab's tallied incidents.
  - (measured) The SchwingNet manuscript's tables still carry bare result numerals because the macro check scanned only the prose.
  - (measured) No manifest key yet begins with a Slurm job id.
  - (measured) The website shipped model weights publicly until mid-September 2026.
  - (measured) A staff list with e-mail addresses sat in a public folder for a long run of commits.
  - (measured) Internal consistency checks do not catch a shared bad landmark.
  - (measured) Canal depth medians looked normal while a fifth of L3 canals were implausibly small.
  - (measured) The public mirror of the CT release still serves v10 label identifiers.
  - (measured) Pre-stated rules make a negative result reportable; the U251 primary endpoint failed its pre-stated bar.
  - (measured) Polling the queue from an agent burns session limits.
  - (measured) Pelvic incidence was computed for a whole release against the wrong label map without any error raised.
  - (unverified) None of the imaging repositories runs Nextflow, contrary to the public tutorial.
- Numbers (45 recorded; the first 10 here):

  - 15 minutes: length of the weekly check-in a lead owes
  - 3 weeks: silence after which a member is re-staffed

  - 5 criteria: conditions all of which must hold for a dataset to sit under IRB umbrella HPR 2026-269
  - 121 MB: model weights the website shipped until 2026-09-14
  - 171 commits: how long a staff list with e-mails sat in a public folder
  - 29 label id: the v10 carved S1 identifier retired in v11; public mirror still v10

- Datasets: CTSpinoPelvic1K (labels CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, CT CC BY 3.0, code Apache-2.0; v10 on the public Hugging Face mirror, v11 retiring id 29), XRSpinoPelvic1K (private forever), FluoroCap (private forever); tools: Slurm (sbatch, squeue, scancel, sacct, --dependency=afterany, --parsable), Nextflow with nf-core pipelines (rnaseq 3.22.2, differentialabundance 1.5.0, fetchngs 1.12.0, methylarray), singularity / conda container, pre-unpacked sandbox, CephFS lab volume, pan_quota, git, sha256sum, flock, latexmk, Hugging Face read token, WSL (wsl -e bash -lc) on Windows, tools/render_board.py, tools/pipeline_state.py, results_macros.py (--check), compose_figures.py (--check), check_limits.py, check_claim_checklist.py, run_record() (RECIPE.md section 4), make env-lock, verify_numbers.py, _roster5.py (scratch verifiers), nibabel (as_closest_canonical, nib.save), torch, CUDA, Claude (sessions, checkpoint blocks); models: SchwingNet (manuscript compiled through the chain; weights private forever), spinesurg-ct-nnunet (level-identification code, private forever).
- Decisions:
  - A dataset sits under IRB umbrella HPR 2026-269 only if all five criteria hold (public and de-identified, downloadable without application or DUA, not restricted-use, no re-identification key, no linkage attempt); anything else needs its own filing before use.: one dataset was used before the separate filing the umbrella promised
  - Level-identification code, FluoroCap, model weights, spinesurg-ct-nnunet and XRSpinoPelvic1K are private forever: never in a public repository, never on the website.: the website shipped 121 MB of weights until 2026-09-14
  - Credentials are read from files, never echoed, never in sbatch --export, never committed; students use their own Hugging Face read token.: a token on a command line is in the history and the job log
  - Survey CSVs, contact spreadsheets and staff lists are never in a repository.: a staff list with emails sat in a public folder for 171 commits
  - Label identifiers are read from the release's dataset_labels.json through labels_for(volume); never import a fixed map.: pelvic incidence was computed for the whole release against the legacy map
  - Canonicalise volumes to analyse; never reorient to write; volumes are stored P,I,R.: as_closest_canonical + nib.save transposed a label away from its CT
  - Students, the grid and non-commercial data are the research track; nothing moves from it to a commercial track.: written policy
  - Pre-register in PREREG.md, freeze it with its hash, never edit it again; departures go in DEVIATIONS.md.: Pre-stated rules make a negative result reportable
  - Job scripts use set -uo pipefail, not set -e, with a message and exit code per check.: with -e a job dies without saying which check failed
  - Chains use --dependency=afterany, never afterok; the child reads a file to decide whether the parent succeeded.: an 8 h limit made a whole chain DependencyNeverSatisfied (2026-09-09)

### 54. Secure Hybrid RDP Access: Spec and Setup Runbook

- Folder: `SchwingNet/artifacts/secure-hybrid-rdp-access-spec-and-setup-runbook-exbt1w`; account: [the owner's login]; link id: ExbT1w2MAF9wysQBWmuj3n; docs, runbook; date: 2026-09-27 (Header line 'Sep 27, 2026 . @[the owner]'.); confidentiality: schwingnet-other.
- Question: How is Remote Desktop to the P50 workstation set up so that it is reachable only over Tailscale, with Duo MFA on every device except the owner's own laptop, and without any way to lock the owner out?
- Summary: A spec and step-by-step runbook for Remote Desktop to the owner's P50 workstation: the P50 accepts RDP only over Tailscale, the owner's laptop signs in with the Windows password alone, every other device must also pass Duo MFA, and nothing on the P50 is reachable from the open internet. A table lists what changed from the original spec (no port forwarding instead of internet-exposed RDP guarded by Duo; a /32 bypass for the laptop instead of the whole 100.64.0.0/10 range; firewall and Tailscale access rules added; key expiry disabled only on the P50; Duo prompting only on RDP with bypass codes on paper). Five design rules are followed by prerequisites (Windows Pro or above, a Duo paid plan since Duo Free cannot do the bypass, a password logon, BitLocker key saved offline), a Windows 10 warning (6th-generation Intel CPU, Windows 10 extended support ending October 13, 2026), and five phases: prep, Tailscale on both machines, RDP with the firewall scoped to the tailnet, Duo Admin Panel policy, Duo install with RDP-only prompting, and a Tailscale grant restricting access to the owner's own devices. Six verification tests, a troubleshooting table and lockout recovery close it.
- Key claims:
  - (unverified) The P50 accepts Remote Desktop only over Tailscale; port 3389 never opens on the router.
  - (inferred) The Duo bypass works only if Duo sees the RDP client's Tailscale address; Phase 4 checks this in the Authentication Log.
  - (reported) Duo Free cannot do the laptop bypass because it only controls the new-user policy.
  - (unverified) Windows Home cannot host Remote Desktop.
  - (reported) The P50's 6th-generation Intel CPU is not on Microsoft's supported list for Windows 11, and Windows 10 extended support ends October 13, 2026, with Duo ending Windows 10 support the same month.
  - (inferred) Duo prompting only on RDP means the P50's own keyboard never needs Duo, which is the lockout protection.
  - (unverified) Without 'Run unattended', the P50 drops off the tailnet after a reboot until someone signs in at its keyboard.
  - (reported) Duo's registry workaround for Microsoft accounts lets Microsoft accounts skip Duo entirely and must not be used.
  - (reported) The Tailscale grant 'autogroup:member to autogroup:self' blocks other users and anything shared into the tailnet.
- Numbers (11 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 3389 TCP port: RDP port that is never forwarded
  - 100.64.0.0/10 IP range: Tailscale address range the firewall rules accept; the original spec's MFA bypass range
  - /32 prefix length: MFA bypass narrowed to the laptop's single Tailscale IP
  - 48 digits: BitLocker recovery password to write on paper
  - 2026-10-13 date: end of Windows 10 extended support
  - 6th Intel CPU generation: the P50's CPU, not on Microsoft's Windows 11 supported list
  - 5 design rules: rules every step implements
  - 6 verification tests: tests that must all pass
  - 10 / 10 / 10 / 15 / 15 / 5 minutes per phase (0 to 5): estimated time of each phase
  - 192.0.2.1/32 test IP: placeholder address to prove the bypass is laptop-only
- Datasets: none named; tools: Tailscale (tailscale ip -4, tailscale ping, admin console, grants), Duo Authentication for Windows Logon and RDP (Duo Mobile, Admin Panel, Policy Calculator, Authentication Log, bypass codes), Windows Remote Desktop (mstsc, Network Level Authentication), PowerShell (Set-NetFirewallRule, Get-NetFirewallAddressFilter), BitLocker (manage-bde), Microsoft Windows App (phone RDP client), winver; models: none named.
- Decisions:
  - No port forwarding: RDP is never reachable from the public internet; other devices join the tailnet and pass Duo (revised from the original spec's internet-exposed RDP guarded by Duo). (2026-09-27), the owner: Nothing on the P50 is reachable from the open internet; the only way in is Tailscale.
  - The MFA bypass is the laptop's single Tailscale IP (/32), not the whole 100.64.0.0/10 range. (2026-09-27), the owner: Every other device gets a Duo prompt.
  - Windows firewall RDP rules accept only tailnet addresses. (2026-09-27): Firewall scoped to the tailnet.
  - The tailnet policy lets only devices signed in as the owner reach the P50 (autogroup:member to autogroup:self). (2026-09-27): Blocks other users and anything shared into the tailnet.
  - Key expiry is disabled on the P50 only, left on for the laptop. (2026-09-27): So the P50 never drops off, while a lost laptop eventually loses access.
  - Duo prompts only on RDP; bypass codes are kept on paper with the BitLocker key. (2026-09-27): No self-lockout: the P50's own keyboard always works; bypass codes cover remote emergencies.
  - Devices that cannot run Tailscale are out of scope; put Tailscale on them rather than opening a port. (2026-09-27): Keeps rule one (no port forwarding).
  - FailOpen stays unchecked.: If Duo's cloud is unreachable, remote logins wait rather than skip MFA.
  - Never add the home public IP to Duo's authorized networks.: The bypass must match only the laptop's Tailscale address.
  - Do not use Duo's registry workaround for Microsoft accounts; switch the account to a local account instead.: The workaround lets Microsoft accounts skip Duo entirely.
- Open questions: Whether the P50 is still on Windows 10, and whether to upgrade to Windows 11 by the unsupported-hardware route or accept an unpatched machine reached remotely. / Whether the Duo plan is Essentials, Advantage or Premier (Free cannot do the bypass). / Whether the P50 account is local or a Microsoft account, which changes the RDP username and may need extra Duo setup. / Whether Duo sees the laptop's Tailscale address as the access device IP (Phase 4, step 7).
- Stale markers: Windows 10 extended support ends October 13, 2026 (already past as of 2026-10-22). / Duo ends Windows 10 support the same month. / Option labels differ between Duo's new and legacy policy editors. / Duo installer link duo-win-login-latest.exe. / Revised from an 'original spec' whose six rows are tabled.

The text names the owner's Windows account name and a Windows domain name; neither is reproduced here. Steps are instruct...

### 55. Grid and cloud Claude workers: review

- Folder: `symphony-template/artifacts/grid-and-cloud-claude-workers-review`; account: first account; link id: L7nL3xkYzoitmWf4ab8ARN; docs, audit; date: 2026-10-21 (Header line 'Oct 21, 2026'; the state was 'read at 12:03 UTC, 21 Oct'.); confidentiality: symphony-beethoven.
- Question: How do the grid and cloud Claude workers compare with industry practice, and which ten fixes are worth making, in what order?
- Summary: A review of how the control panel starts, watches, stops and opens Claude workers on the Wayne State grid (a press runs grid-accounts.yml, one fixed ssh word to a forced-command gate, hpcworker submits one sbatch running Claude Code under Remote Control) and in GitHub Actions (workers.yml on the token pool). On 21 October the grid answered with framework 22, one worker online on node hao1 and account 1 signed in; no second worker has been started since that spends allocation. Against Runner Controller, Slurm, Kubernetes operators, hosted coding agents and Temporal, the design is on par for cancel, idempotency, coordination and credential scope, and behind on intake (a burst of presses loses one), tracking (state known only when asked), observability, error causes and key lifetime. Ten ranked fixes follow, the first five being durable request files, starting N workers in one word, pushing claims every 30 minutes and before TERM, a scheduled list every 15 minutes, and showing the shell's error. What was verified and what is not yet measured are listed.
- Key claims:
  - (measured) On 21 October the grid answers with framework 22, one worker online on hao1 and account 1 (a Max subscription) signed in.
  - (reported) GitHub keeps at most one waiting run per concurrency group, so a third quick press of Start replaces the second, which never runs.
  - (measured) The gate's floor is 60 seconds for start, login and usage and 3 seconds for the other accounts words, one call at a time under a lock.
  - (measured) A start by a name already running submits nothing.
  - (measured) Start, Stop and Cancel are covered by the panel's tests G9, G10 and R1 with mutants, and by the template's tests with a fake ssh and git.
  - (inferred) A grid worker busy for a day without pushing loses its claim to the board's 24-hour release.
  - (inferred) No port is open on the grid, no Claude credential leaves it, and git is the durable record of who works on what.
  - (inferred) The design falls short in three places: lost or slowed requests, state known only when asked, and failures without causes.
  - (measured) The Beethoven Connect failure showed only 'the grid stopped answering' while the real error sat in the debug log.
  - (inferred) The gate's ssh key never expires; industry uses short-lived credentials.
  - (unverified) On the grid a successor job continues the same Remote Control session for up to seven days; in the cloud nothing survives a run except what it pushed.
  - (inferred) The industry columns describe each tool's documented design, not a test run here.
- Numbers (18 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 22 framework version: the grid's answer on 21 Oct
  - 12:03 UTC, 21 Oct time: when the state was read (request verify22)
  - 1 worker online: worker named worker on node hao1; account 1 signed in
  - 10 ranked fixes: weak points ranked by help against cost; five worth making first
  - 24 hours: idle time after which the board releases a claim
  - 60 seconds: gate floor between start, login and usage calls
  - 3 seconds: gate floor for the other accounts words, so a scheduled list costs nothing
  - 1 running + 1 waiting runs per concurrency group: why a burst of presses loses one
  - about N minutes for N workers minutes: start latency from the 60-second floor; five workers take five minutes or more
  - 15 minutes: proposed scheduled list interval
- Datasets: none named; tools: the control panel (Grid tab, Runs tab), grid-accounts.yml and the environment grid, workers.yml and the token pool (pool.py), hpcworker and its gate script (forced-command ssh key), Slurm (sbatch, squeue, sacct, scancel, --array, --requeue, afterany, --signal), Claude Code Remote Control, GitHub Actions (workflow_dispatch, concurrency groups, cancel API), the branch grid, Beethoven (Connect, debug log), Actions Runner Controller, Kubernetes operators and Lease, Temporal, Copilot coding agent, Devin, OpenHands (comparison only), OIDC and ssh certificates (comparison only), H200 GPUs; models: none named.
- Decisions:
  - No second grid worker is started until the owner gives the word and a number. (2026-10-21), the owner: that spends allocation
  - The cause of the Beethoven Connect failure is parked until the debug log from the next failed press. (2026-10-21), the author (the session): the real error was only in the debug log
- Open questions: Two or more grid workers running at once on real nodes / How long a start takes from press to online, and queue wait per partition / Whether a successor job's --continue keeps a long session whole across several job ends / The cause of the Beethoven Connect failure / Which of the ten ranked fixes the owner takes

### 56. Several grid workers, one per project

- Folder: `symphony-template/artifacts/several-grid-workers-one-per-project`; account: first account; link id: UjuFBMxLEL8CnifRofU3Vv; docs, plan; date: 2026-10-21 (Header line 'Oct 21, 2026' and the request line '[the owner], 2026-10-21 20:06 UTC'.); confidentiality: symphony-beethoven.
- Question: How can the Beethoven extension switch between and update several Claude workers on the grid, one per claude.ai project, when the laptop cannot keep several Claude desktops signed in at once?
- Summary: A plan answering the owner's request of 2026-10-21 to switch between and update projects through several Claude workers on the grid, driven from the browser through the Beethoven extension. It says most of the grid side exists: since hpcworker framework 22 the grid runs several named workers at once, each a separate Slurm job with its own Remote Control link and status, started with accounts start N NAME on a Claude account slot, and the control panel's Grid tab lists them; what is missing is on the Beethoven side, which knows one worker name and one project address. The design gives Beethoven a projects list in Settings (name, project address, grid worker, account slot), a chip switcher on the home screen with one tab per project, start-on-press through the portal shell, an Update press that fast-forwards the worker's workspace to main, and the same pairing stored on the grid in accounts.json so the control panel reads one record. The build order is four pull requests: hpcworker's project field and accounts list, Beethoven's list and switcher (a store release), the control panel's project column, then the workspace Update. Two open questions are not yet measured: whether two workers on one Claude account can each stay a separate environment, and whether a project's Work locally pairing survives a wall-time restart with --continue.
- Key claims:
  - (unverified) Most of the grid side already exists: since framework 22 the grid runs several named workers at once, each a Remote Control environment; what is missing is on the Beethoven side.
  - (unverified) A claude.ai project reaches a worker only through Work locally with the folder allowed on the card, so each project must be paired with its own worker by hand once.
  - (unverified) Beethoven 23.x holds one worker_name and one project_url, so Open my project and the home screen follow a single worker.
  - (inferred) Storing the project pairing on the grid in accounts.json lets Beethoven and the control panel read one record, so a session can start or stop a project's worker without the owner.
  - (inferred) If two workers cannot share one Claude account as separate environments, the number of projects running at once equals the number of Claude accounts signed in on the grid, today one.
- Numbers (6 recorded; the first 6 here):
  - 22 hpcworker framework version: Since this framework the grid runs several named workers at once; the current range is 22-23
  - 23.x Beethoven version: The extension version that knows one worker name and one project address
  - 20:06 UTC, 2026-10-21 time of request: When the owner asked for the feature
  - 4 pull requests: Build order, one pull request each: hpcworker, Beethoven, control panel, Update press
  - 1 (today), 2 with the gmail account Claude accounts signed in on the grid: Bound on projects running at once if workers cannot share an account
  - 1 contract version step: hpcworker's contract export moves up one version with the project field
- Datasets: none named; tools: hpcworker (accounts start N NAME, accounts stop NAME, worker status --name NAME --json, accounts list, accounts.json, status.json, connection.json), Beethoven extension (worker_name, project_url, Open my project, Settings projects list, switcher), Control panel Grid tab (Start, Stop, Log, Sign in another) and Workers tab (Running now), Slurm jobs and Remote Control environments, the portal shell (one-sbatch press), the gate's update (fast-forward only), claude.ai Work locally pairing, --continue on a successor job; models: none named.
- Decisions:
  - Give Beethoven a projects list in Settings (name, project address, grid worker, account slot), the existing single address becoming the first row so nothing breaks. (2026-10-21), the session that wrote the plan: Beethoven today knows one worker name and one project address
  - Store the project-to-worker pairing on the grid in accounts.json so Beethoven and the control panel read one record. (2026-10-21), the session that wrote the plan: so a session can start or stop a project's worker through the gate without the owner
  - Update pulls the worker's workspace to its repository's main by fast-forward only, asking first if the worker is busy. (2026-10-21), the session that wrote the plan: the same rule as the gate's update
  - Start on press never starts a second worker of the same name. (2026-10-21), the session that wrote the plan
  - Build in four pull requests: hpcworker first, then Beethoven (a store release), then the control panel, then the workspace Update on both sides. (2026-10-21), the session that wrote the plan

- Question: In what phases, each with a measured gate, should the Beethoven research engine be built on top of hpcworker and beethoven-extension, and what must the owner decide first?
- Summary: A six-phase roadmap for turning the two Beethoven repositories, hpcworker (framework 11) and beethoven-extension (12.0.0), from a transport layer into a research engine. Phase 0 is one probe job that decides whether the inner loop can be headless claude -p on the student's own login from a batch job; phases 1 to 5 add one headless run, a chained-Slurm-job outer loop with a run ledger, REFORMS gates enforced by code, paper and archive steps with a student-pressed Publish, and scale to two concurrent projects and a second site. A table maps each REFORMS control to its phase and enforcement, with module numbers not yet checked against Kapoor et al. 2024. The repositories' standing rules (no credential handling, nothing on the login node beyond one sbatch, 60 s polling, node-local temp, bash 4.2 and Python 3.6) shape the design. Six decisions are left to the owner with a recommendation each, and the notebook's proposals that the plan rejects are listed.

### 60. The Dollar-per-Feature Ladder

- Folder: `symphony-template/artifacts/the-dollar-per-feature-ladder-9ymg61`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: 9Ymg61SPUZTqaNBCbzGHMx; docs, comparison; date: 2026-10-09 (Header line 'Oct 9, 2026 . @[the owner]'; body says 'the ladder below was verified against GitHub's and Anthropic's o...); confidentiality: symphony-beethoven.
- Question: What does running the sdlc-template (the shared-repository, agent-driven software project) cost at each GitHub and Claude price rung, and what named feature does each dollar above the bottom rung buy?
- Summary: The page argues that the template runs whole for $17 a month (one Claude Pro seat billed annually plus a public repository on GitHub Free), that the GitHub bill at that rung is $0 by construction because an account with no stored payment method is halted at the quota rather than billed, and that GitHub Pro and Team both include the same 3,000 private-repo CI minutes, so money above the bottom rung buys governance and usage rather than compute until Enterprise's 50,000 minutes. It lays out a five-rung ladder (Free+Pro, +GitHub Pro, Team+Claude Team, premium seats or Max, Enterprise) with what each genuinely adds, describes three pull requests (#3, #5, #7) that turned the protocol's prose into mechanism, and explains the zero-inference core (native settings beat workflows, workflows beat inference). It refuses to price the Enterprise tier because Claude Enterprise's pricing could not be verified. A verification section reports a 103-agent research run on 2026-10-09 with 21 sources, 95 claims, the top 25 verified by three votes each, 23 confirmed and 2 refuted, and a 'Turning it on' checklist with the first item done.
- Key claims:
  - (reported) The whole template runs for $17 a month on one Claude Pro seat and a public GitHub Free repository.
  - (reported) The GitHub bill at the bottom rung is guaranteed $0 because GitHub halts usage at the quota when no payment method is stored and a public repository never draws on the quota.
  - (reported) GitHub Pro and GitHub Team include the same 3,000 private-repo CI minutes.
  - (inferred) Above the bottom rung money buys governance and usage, never compute, until Enterprise.
  - (inferred) Cutting the board's cron fallback from hourly to six-hourly saves a private repository about 630 billed minutes a month.
  - (reported) Claude Enterprise pricing is custom and could not be verified, and a circulating $20-per-seat figure was refuted.
  - (reported) Server-side push restrictions and bypass lists belong to organizations and cannot be bought on a personal account at any price; the template's hook and protocol check stand in client-side.
  - (reported) Organization-wide rulesets moved down from Enterprise to Team in June 2025.
  - (inferred) Claude Max adds usage only, no feature, so it is the right spend only when one person's weekly limit is the constraint.
  - (measured) The three pull requests #3, #5 and #7 pass CI, the protocol check validating itself against its own pull request.
  - (inferred) The repository as workspace approximates Claude Enterprise's shared workspace on any paid plan, at the cost of git-fetch synchronization latency.
  - (measured) The research run verified 23 of 25 top claims and refuted 2, both refutations changing the design.
- Numbers (37 recorded; the first 10 here):

  - 100-200 USD per month, n = 1 person: Claude Max solo

### 61. Symphony and Beethoven Taxonomy Manual

- Folder: `symphony-template/artifacts/symphony-and-beethoven-taxonomy-manual-re4jtx`; account: gschwing; link id: RE4jtXqsFFgiZxhQLamUDR; docs, manual; date: 2026-10-15 (Header line under the title: "Oct 15, 2026 . @f"; the text also says sources were "Already checked against primary so...); confidentiality: symphony-beethoven.
- Question: How does one use the Symphony and Beethoven taxonomy spec: run the deep research that checks it, test a claim, place any other tool in the taxonomy, explain the approach to grant reviewers, engineers and lab leads, and keep the spec current?
- Summary: A companion to the taxonomy spec (linked as a claude.ai artifact) that holds procedures rather than claims: what to read first, which claims were already checked against primary sources on 2026-10-15, and a three-pass deep-research procedure whose first pass is a long prompt to paste into claude.ai Research because the project's cloud sessions cannot reach docs.github.com, arxiv.org and other hosts. It gives a six-step method for testing a claim (write it with its scope, write the refutation first, search by mechanism, run it on a scratch repository, record a null search, narrow or drop), a ranked table of which sources give which grade (Verified, Inferred, Reported, Open), and a twelve-axis worksheet plus five mechanisms (a) to (e) for placing any tool, worked through for unsorry (agenticsnz/unsorry at 029d328b), which scores one yes and three partials and so does not refute uniqueness claim U1. Section 6 gives the argument in order (need, mechanism, economics, evidence, limits) with tailored paragraphs for grant reviewers, engineers and lab leads, a table of what to say and not say about free GitHub Actions minutes, and honest answers to engineers' questions (only a claim's creation is enforced on GitHub; idleness is read from the pusher's committer date; F8 is written but not merged). Section 7 lists seven recipes for combining Symphony and Beethoven with other software (security gates, beethoven-qa as a qa lane, the Claude-free core in a Beethoven repository, other agents under the claim, merge queue, workflow managers, Actions as control plane), each marked works today, planned, chosen or not tried. Section 8 says what to re-check when prices, terms, releases or neighbors change, and section 9 is a glossary.
- Key claims:
  - (reported) In CooperBench one GPT-5 agent passed 315 of 652 feature pairs where two agents, each in its own copy and messaging only, passed 183; the failures were duplicated and contradictory work.
  - (reported) Symphony's claim is atomic because git lets exactly one of two racing branch creations win, so no lock server is needed.
  - (measured) The atomic claim is not unique: gh-aw's work queue, unsorry and agentic-setup each have one, so claim U1 names only the combination of mechanisms.
  - (reported) The shipped protocol code was checked over 16.7 million states against an oracle written from the spec, with mutants, and found no violation; building the harness found 19 defects.
  - (measured) On GitHub only a claim's creation is enforced; a fast-forward or forced push onto an existing claim is accepted because claude/** is left out of every ruleset so a release can rename the branch.
  - (measured) The push guard judged a lease by the session's own directory rather than the clone git -C names, so a lease onto another worker's claim passed from a directory whose clone had neither branch.
  - (measured) Idleness is read from the committer date, which the pusher writes; a fix reading GitHub's own record of the push is written but not merged (F8).
  - (reported) Standard GitHub-hosted runners are free and unlimited on public repositories, but inference is paid by a Claude plan and one plan's usage window is shared.
  - (measured) unsorry scores one yes and three partials on the five mechanisms and so does not refute U1, but is why U1 and U2 are worded narrowly.
  - (reported) No full-scale benchmark of Symphony exists yet.
  - (reported) No Beethoven repository runs Symphony's code today; the stack is a design.
  - (reported) gh-aw's work queue is also a compare-and-swap on a git ref, but a trusted scheduler grants claims to Actions runs only, claims never expire for idleness, and its Claude engine refuses the subscription token.
- Numbers (34 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 315 of 652 feature pairs passed, n = 652 pairs: One GPT-5 agent on CooperBench
  - 183 feature pairs passed, n = 652 pairs: Two GPT-5 agents, each in its own copy, messaging only, on CooperBench
  - 4 vCPU, 16 GB runner size: Standard GitHub-hosted Linux runner on a public repository, twice a private one's
  - 20 / 40 / 60 / 500 concurrent jobs: GitHub Actions concurrency on Free, Pro, Team and Enterprise plans
  - 6 hours: Maximum GitHub Actions job length
  - 1,000 API requests per hour: GITHUB_TOKEN rate limit per repository
  - 2,000 / 3,000 / 50,000 included minutes a month: Private repositories by plan
  - 0.006 USD per minute: Linux minutes on private repositories past the quota, since 2026-01-01
  - 16.7 million states: Interleavings checked against the oracle for the shipped protocol code
  - 19 defects: Found by building the validation harness; six fixed in 0.4.1, eleven on main unreleased, one settled in the spec, one (F8) open

### 62. SDLC Build Plan

- Folder: `symphony-template/artifacts/sdlc-build-plan-bjzikn`; account: gschwing; link id: BjZikNkPVNU2G4KGaCxawZ; docs, plan; date: 2026-10-08 (Header line under the title: "Oct 8, 2026 . @f". The body also records later events: "sized on 2026-10-09" and "On 20...); confidentiality: symphony-beethoven.
- Question: Should the shared-plane SDLC be built by expanding Claude Swap Cloud (Route A) or as a focused utility on top of what already exists (Route B), and in what phases?
- Summary: The plan recommends Route B: build a small standard-library materializer that turns a cswap Project export into repository files (CLAUDE.md, docs/memory/, docs/history/, docs/project.md), leave the Claude Swap Cloud extension and bridge unchanged, and run the unattended export through a routine inside the Project rather than any browser scheduler, which the Consumer Terms forbid. It sets each account's claude.ai Project as a thin pointer to the repository and leans on GitHub Issues, branch protection, a board-regenerating Action, Anthropic-run routines and cloud threads instead of bespoke services. A table maps each architectural need to an existing tool. Six phases (0 to 5, an hour to two days each) each end with a measurement on the real claude-swap Project; on 2026-10-09 the owner chose GitHub Issues and asked for the repository side first as a template repository with no export or import in its first layer. Risks listed include the terms, multi-account use of one repository, routine caps, the lossy prompt route, one-way memory flow, instruction drift, branch protection on a private repository, keys in Actions and unpublished routes.
- Key claims:
  - (inferred) Route B (a focused utility) is recommended and Claude Swap Cloud is left as it is.
  - (inferred) What is missing from the architecture is about 300 lines of code plus two short GitHub Actions.
  - (measured) The extension and bridge are 14,907 lines of Chrome-only plumbing for 6,864 lines of script.
  - (reported) The one thing the architecture would need from the extension, an export on a schedule, is what the terms forbid a browser script to do.
  - (reported) The read side of a Project is proven; the write side only for a new project; nothing in cswap should drive claude.ai unattended.
  - (inferred) The materializer is sized at 300 to 480 lines plus 300 to 500 lines of tests.
  - (inferred) The materializer writes by content so an unchanged Project gives an empty diff, and needs no request, credential or key.
  - (reported) Routine caps are thirty runs an hour per routine and a hundred an hour per account; a weekly export and daily board are far under them.
  - (measured) A live export read a Project's 19 memory entries on 2026-10-08; the memory write was never exercised.
  - (inferred) Claiming work by a branch named after the issue needs no lock service because git accepts only the first push of a new branch.
  - (measured) The prompt export route is lossy and varies run to run.
  - (unverified) Branch protection on a private repository needs a paid GitHub plan or a public repository; claude-swap is private today.
- Numbers (20 recorded; the first 10 here):

### 63. SDLC Primitives Spec

- Folder: `symphony-template/artifacts/sdlc-primitives-spec-ldx4zd`; account: gschwing; link id: LDx4ZDE5CUexASCnEtHZVL; docs, spec; date: 2026-10-08 (Header line "Oct 8, 2026 . @f"; body says "Every claim below cites a file and line in the claude-swap repository as o...); confidentiality: symphony-beethoven.
- Question: What can cswap and Claude Swap Cloud already export and import of a claude.ai Project, and what gaps remain before the multi-account SDLC can be built on them?
- Summary: The spec inventories every primitive in the claude-swap repository that moves or holds a claude.ai Project's knowledge (the export and import browser scripts, cswap project-move in its folder, branch-carrier and auto forms, the Chrome extension and its bridge, cswap teleport, the handoff note, the checkpoint and the read-only account registry) and maps each Project field (name, instructions, topic, memory, files, repositories, settings, members, threads, sessions) to the route it is read from, the file it lands in, the request that writes it back and whether the round trip is whole. It then measures these against what the SDLC architecture (documents 22, 23 and 25 and the plan of 2026-10-08) needs: CLAUDE.md from the instructions, docs/memory/ from the memory, docs/history/ from the conversations, a deterministic handoff brief and an unattended export. It finds nothing writes instructions into CLAUDE.md or memory under docs/memory/, the memory write precondition has never been seen live, exports are non-deterministic with no checksums, and no unattended export is possible under Anthropic's terms except a lossy routine-run prompt route. It closes with the binding constraints: no scripting of claude.ai on a schedule, Projects belong to one account, every account signs in itself, trust boundaries and store rules stay, no new runtime dependencies, deterministic checkpoints, and platform limits on routine fires and file sizes.
- Key claims:
  - (inferred) The repository is the only plane shared across accounts, since no first-party feature lets one account's Project reach another's.
  - (measured) Project memory can be read live but writing it back has never been confirmed.
  - (measured) An export is non-deterministic and unchecksummed, so an unchanged Project does not give an empty diff.
  - (measured) Nothing in cswap writes the Project's instructions into CLAUDE.md or its memory under docs/memory/.
  - (measured) The two browser scripts do all the reading and writing of a Project; the extension and bridge are plumbing at 2.2 lines per script line.
  - (inferred) An export nobody has to click cannot be built in cswap, because the terms allow claude.ai to be scripted only by a routine or a cloud session.
  - (measured) No CI workflow or script in the repository reads or writes CLAUDE.md, .claude/, memory, a handoff note or an export.json, and none runs on a schedule.
  - (inferred) The checkpoint's inference beyond tagged lines is regex-based, so a plausible wrong line can reach CLAUDE.md.
  - (measured) The hand-written checkpoint and the CLAUDE.md marker section disagree and nothing checks them against each other.
  - (inferred) A webhook receiver and a scheduler of browser sessions are outside cswap's remit and should not be added.
- Numbers (38 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 4,149 lines: Size of the export script project-export.js (wc -l on main at PR #45)
  - 2,715 lines: Size of the import script project-import.js
  - about 2,520 lines: cswap project-move with a folder, including its CLI parts
  - 18 / about 90 lines: The branch-carrier route: lines of prompt and lines of polling code
  - about 2,000 lines: cswap project-move auto (DevTools-driven Chrome automation)
  - 14,907 lines: The extension and its bridge together (listed parts: extension/src 9,758; bridge.py 4,661; file_dialogs.py 287)
  - about 610 lines: cswap teleport (teleport.py 462 plus the pre-dispatch in cli.py)
  - 610 lines: handoff.py, the handoff note renderer
  - 1,084 lines: checkpoint.py
  - about 10,050 lines: The account core, of which the read-only registry surfaces are a few hundred

- Decisions:
  - The import always creates a new private project and never changes an existing one; no sync, diff or merge.: Design of project-import.js (lines 36-39); for a thin Project pointing at the repository this stops mattering.
  - The environment and browser-access config keys are left out of the import by design.
  - The two browser scripts are not changed for the import side and the extension's copies stay byte-equal; browser/*, project_move.py and handoff.py are reused read-only.: Recorded in the repository's CLAUDE.md Constraints.

  - No new runtime dependencies: Python standard library only, the extension plain ES modules with no build step.: CLAUDE.md, Constraints.
  - A failed step never loops and a person can always step in; a challenged, blocked or unauthenticated request is never re-sent by code and Retry-After is honoured in full., the owner: The owner's rule, recorded under CLAUDE.md Decisions.
  - A webhook receiver or edge proxy and a scheduler of browser sessions are not to be added to cswap. (2026-10-08), the author of the spec: The first is GitHub's and Linear's own machinery; the second runs against the terms and the extension's own decisions (nothing retried on its own, the person...
  - The plan should never need the repository-to-Project memory write direction. (2026-10-08), the author of the spec: It rests on a write precondition never seen live.

  - Any change to data handling bumps PRIVACY.md in both repositories, byte-equal; scripts ship minified but never obfuscated.: The Chrome Web Store's rules (PR #44).

### 64. Symphony versus competitors

- Folder: `symphony-template/artifacts/symphony-versus-competitors-lynwzk`; account: [the owner's login]; link id: LynwZkPadyo2SQ7G4mJfHc; docs, comparison; date: 2026-10-16 (Header line under the title: "Oct 16, 2026 . @[the owner]"; sources read on 2026-10-15 and 2026-10-16.); confidentiality: symphony-beethoven.
- Question: Where does Symphony stand, by design, against every other way of coordinating coding agents, and what may honestly be claimed for it before any outcome is measured?
- Summary: The Doc sets Symphony, a GitHub repository acting as the coordinator of Claude Code agents with no server of its own, beside four kinds of system: work-queue coordinators (gh-aw, unsorry, openai/symphony, CCPM, Beads and Gas Town, paperclip, agentic-setup, Orbi, aeon), vendor-hosted agents, single-session orchestration and agent frameworks. Two tables apply the manual's two questions (what stops a second agent taking the same work, what frees an unfinished claim) and record where each runs, who pays and what was read at which commit on 2026-10-15. It argues Symphony is better by design in three respects, graded Inferred: one claim rule for sessions, routines and Actions agents on a flat-rate subscription, an idle claim released with its work kept, and a shipped library checked over every interleaving against an oracle with mutants. It then lists where Symphony is not better or not yet (no outcome evidence, pre-release 0.4.1, the claim enforced only at creation, the idle clock read from the committer date, scale unmeasured), what not to claim, one paragraph per audience and a full sources section.
- Key claims:
  - (inferred) Symphony is better by design in three respects, with better meaning better designed since no outcome is measured.
  - (measured) An adversarial search of 25 candidate systems found none with four of the five named mechanisms.
  - (inferred) Each of the three uniqueness claims is graded Inferred because a search that finds nothing cannot verify.
  - (reported) gh-aw's Claude engine does not support the Claude subscription token; the secret is ignored.
  - (reported) Copilot's agents bill AI credits at $0.01 each, and Actions minutes where those are billed.
  - (reported) Standard GitHub-hosted runners are free and unlimited on public repositories.
  - (measured) The protocol library's validation harness found no violation over 16.7 million states at depth 8 and 18.8 million at depth 7 with two issues.
  - (measured) 93 mutants were each caught, 109 on main after #35; building the harness found 19 defects.
  - (reported) gh-aw checks TLA+ models of its queue design, not the shipped code.
  - (reported) CooperBench: one GPT-5 agent passed 315 of 652 feature pairs, two agents 183; 33.2% of failed runs were overlap; a first-turn plan nearly halved conflicts (29.4% vs 51.5%).
  - (reported) gh-aw never expires a Claim by time; unsorry deletes a claim two hours after it was made and keeps nothing.
  - (measured) GitHub enforces a claim only at its creation; the local push guard was found to read the hook's working directory rather than the clone a git -C push names.
- Numbers (52 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 25 candidate systems: Size of the adversarial uniqueness search of 2026-10-15
  - 14 web queries: Extent of the search; two leads unread
  - 5 mechanisms: Mechanisms the spec names for Symphony
  - 3 claims: Claims of being better by design, all graded Inferred
  - 0.4.1 version: Last release of the protocol library, 2026-10-13
  - 12 fixes: Fixes on main and unreleased since 0.4.1; a thirteenth finding F16 settled in the spec
  - 24 hours: Idle time after which a claim is released
  - 3 attempts: gh-aw's retry cap after a Claim ends
  - 15 minutes: Period of unsorry's reaper in Actions
  - 2 hours: unsorry claim TTL (TTL_SECONDS = 7200)
- Datasets: CooperBench (benchmark; the paper's PDF in cooperbench/website, arXiv 2601.13295, repository at 63b9d44); tools: Symphony (symphony-protocol library, symphony-template, SymphonyBoard App), GitHub Actions, git refs and branches as claims, anthropics/claude-code-action, Claude Code (sessions, agent teams, subagents, Projects, hooks, push guard), gh-aw (GitHub Agentic Workflows work queue), unsorry, openai/symphony, CCPM, Beads and Gas Town (Dolt), paperclip, agentic-setup, Orbi, aeon, Copilot cloud agent and automations, Codex cloud, Devin, Cursor cloud agents, Google Jules, OpenHands Cloud and OpenHands SDK, Kiro, claude-squad, Conductor, vibe-kanban, LangGraph, Temporal, CrewAI, AutoGen and Microsoft Agent Framework, AG2, OpenAI Agents SDK, Claim Plane, TLA+ (gh-aw's model checks), coordbench, terraform-backend-git, DND-IT/action-lock, validation harness (symphony-protocol validation/); models: GPT-5 (CooperBench), Claude (subscription plans, claude-code-action), Codex, Gemini (unsorry volunteers' runs).
- Decisions:
  - The three uniqueness claims are worded narrowly and graded Inferred, since unsorry has the board in CI and looser forms of the other mechanisms. (2026-10-15), the document's author (the research session): A search that finds nothing cannot verify; the spec names what would refute each claim.
  - coordbench's numbers stay out of the grant until the owner lifts the hold. (2026-10-15), the owner: The harness audit found 11 of 40 runs spoiled; figures are not reportable until reruns and the pre-registered full run complete.
  - Orbi and aeon are left out of the head-to-head tables; Claude Code Projects and Claim Plane are named for completeness only., the document's author: They have no claim between workers (Orbi, aeon), no shared claim across accounts (Projects) or serialize almost every execution (Claim Plane).
  - Do not claim an atomic claim on the forge, serverless coordination in Actions, free coordination on a public repository, the subscription token in Actions, one issue one branch one PR, free or unlimited agents, 'nothing else does this', Anthropic's approval, a benchmark win, or the Symphony and Beethoven stack., the spec (section 8) and the manual: Each has prior art or is untrue as a headline.
  - Better means better designed, not measured better, until an outcome is shown., the document's author: No peer-reviewed study or benchmark compares the protocol with one agent at a matched budget.

### 65. Symphony in brief

- Folder: `symphony-template/artifacts/symphony-in-brief-9cdciz`; account: [the owner's login]; link id: 9CDCiZqYfbrobDk6rRrMho; docs, report; date: 2026-10-14 (Header line under the title: "Oct 14, 2026" with the author chip; the body says "On symphony-protocol's main today (b...); confidentiality: symphony-beethoven.
- Question: What is Symphony, how does it coordinate many Claude Code workers on one GitHub repository, which of its 17 guarantees are checked, what does it not promise, and how does it stand against CooperBench and other agent coding work as of 2026-10-14?
- Summary: Symphony lets many Claude Code workers share one GitHub repository using only GitHub's own objects: issues are the queue, the first push of a branch claude/N is the lock, and a pull request is the only way work lands. A board on the branch board, a coordinator step in the same run (releases claims idle 24 hours, tells overlapping work its landing order) and fences (a push guard hook, branch protection, CI's protocol check) run around six steps: queue, pick, claim, work in the open, finish, release. Of its 17 bookkeeping guarantees, 12 hold on symphony-protocol main b5f098d, 2 hold only if commit dates are honest, 1 is known broken (G7, repeat overlap notices), 1 is partly checked and 1 not checked, by bounded search of small worlds, 59 caught mutants and 20,970 generated push commands, which is checking and not proof. It does not promise correct code, progress, speed from parallel work, semantic conflict detection, fair idle times or protection from anyone with write access. Against CooperBench (Sonnet 4.5 fell from 47.1% solo to 25.8% as a pair; the authors' later ablation got 56% with a shared git remote and 62% with a team) Symphony is the opposite, shared and continuously merged design, but coordbench's pre-registered 50-pair run has not happened, so no outcome is measured yet. A table sets it beside SWE-bench, MetaGPT, ChatDev, Anthropic's C compiler experiment, Claude Code agent teams, Anthropic's multi-agent research system and Cursor's long-running agents.
- Key claims:
  - (reported) One worker holds an issue at a time because GitHub refuses a second push of the branch claude/N.
  - (measured) On symphony-protocol main b5f098d, 12 of 17 guarantees hold, 2 hold only if commit dates are honest, 1 is known broken, 1 partly checked, 1 not checked.
  - (measured) Breadth-first search of every interleaving of small worlds (two workers depth 5, about 29,500 states; three workers depth 4; every fault kind) found no violation.
  - (measured) 59 mutants of the rules are each caught, showing the checks bite.
  - (measured) The push guard matches git on 16,542 of 20,970 generated push commands, refuses 1,584 harmless ones by shape, and cannot read 2,844 inside bash -c.
  - (measured) G7 (overlapping work told once) is broken: a claim is told again when it opens its pull request (F2 open).
  - (measured) The checking is bounded search of small worlds, not a proof; the deep lane (symphony-protocol#21) is unmerged.
  - (reported) Nothing after release 0.4.1 is released; the template already runs F4, F7, F12 and F14 ahead of release; F9, F13 and F15 come with the next release; F16 needs no code there.
  - (reported) There is no API key anywhere; Claude in Actions runs on one account's subscription token and an idle worker costs no Claude usage.
  - (inferred) Symphony guarantees how work is shared, not that the work is good, finished or faster.
  - (reported) Overlaps are found by file name only, so two changes to different files can still break each other.
  - (inferred) Idleness is read from the commit date, which whoever pushes sets, so a worker can keep a claim alive or lapse early by its clock (F8 open).
- Numbers (52 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 17 guarantees: count of Symphony's bookkeeping guarantees
  - 12 guarantees, n = of 17: guarantees that hold on symphony-protocol main b5f098d on 2026-10-14
  - 2 guarantees, n = of 17: guarantees (G2, G3) that hold only if commit dates are honest, F8 open
  - 1 guarantee, n = of 17: known broken: G7, repeat overlap notices
  - 1 guarantee, n = of 17: partly checked: G13 merge gate, in memory only
  - 1 guarantee, n = of 17: not checked: G11b, branch protection behind pushes the guard cannot read
  - 24 hours: idle time after which an unfinished claim is released
  - 6 hours: fallback interval of the board's rebuild
  - about 29,500 states, n = two workers on one issue, depth 5: breadth-first search of interleavings; no violation found
  - 4 search depth, n = three workers: depth of the three-worker search
- Datasets: CooperBench (652 tasks from 12 repositories in 4 languages), coordbench survey-1 pairs (50 pairs from 20 tasks), SWE-bench, SWE-bench Lite, BrowseComp, MAST dataset of over a thousand annotated traces; tools: symphony-protocol (Python library, vendored copies), symphony-template, coordbench, Claude Code, GitHub Actions (board.yml, coordinator.yml), push guard hook, CI protocol check, merge gate, exact McNemar test, SWE-agent, Agentless, MetaGPT, ChatDev, Claude Code agent teams, Cursor long-running agents, git; models: Claude Sonnet 4.5, GPT-5, Claude Sonnet 5.5.
- Decisions:
  - coordbench's run is pre-registered: 50 pairs from 20 tasks, five arms (sdlc, solo, coop, coop-git, team), Claude Sonnet 5.5 for every agent, 120 minutes per agent, one seed.
  - The primary test is sdlc against coop on CooperBench's own pass verdict, by an exact McNemar test.
  - A second seed on the pairs where arms disagree is the planned follow-up.: One seed at 50 pairs separates only large gaps.
  - coordbench scores with CooperBench's own scorer at commit 63b9d44, which on a conflict tests the first agent's patch alone, unlike the rule behind the paper's numbers.
  - The paper's own numbers sit beside coordbench's for orientation only.: They came from other models and harnesses.
  - F16 needs no code in the template, only new wording in its docs at the sync. (2026-10-14): 0.4.1 already behaves as the spec now says.
  - Still open before 0.5.0, in order: F17 (fix written and in its checks), then a two-workers clerk finding, F2 (G7), F8 (idleness by GitHub's push time) and a claim stuck by a lost reply. (2026-10-14)
- Open questions: Whether Symphony beats CooperBench's coop arm, or the shared-git and team arms: coordbench's pre-registered run has not happened, a real dispatch waits on th... / Whether parallel work under Symphony is faster or more successful than one agent (unmeasured). / Whether the leases and epic planning answer Cursor's locks held too long, the compiler's one giant task and agent teams' same-file overwrites (untested). / G11b: whether GitHub's branch protection stops pushes the guard cannot read; only the canary's check C7 measures it and the canary is not adopted. / G13: the merge gate on real git waits on the deep lane, symphony-protocol#21, unmerged. / F8: idleness judged by GitHub's push time rather than the pusher's clock. / F2: the repeat overlap notice when a claim opens its pull request (G7 broken). / F17: a fork's pull request named like a claim holds that claim forever (fix written).

### 66. Shared multi-account Claude project: plan

- Folder: `symphony-template/artifacts/shared-multi-account-claude-project-plan-rqzwh9`; account: [the owner's login]; link id: Rqzwh9ncY9aj6kZa5BQp7m; docs, plan; date: 2026-10-08 (Byline 'Oct 8, 2026' and the sources line 'All opened on 2026-10-08.'); confidentiality: symphony-beethoven.
- Question: How can several Claude accounts work on one project as a coordinated whole when no Claude product shares a Project, an agent or a memory across accounts?
- Summary: The plan says to build around the repository, not around a shared Project, because a Project belongs to one account and no Claude product lets one account's agent talk live to another's. The repository holds code, rules and shared memory; Linear holds the queue; a task is claimed by pushing a branch named after its issue, which git accepts only once; the open PR is the progress and a merged PR plus a Done issue is the record. A coordinator routine on the owner's main account wakes on PR events, on pushes to main through a GitHub Action, and hourly, releasing claims idle for 24 hours, flagging overlapping PRs and rewriting a STATE.md board. Shared memory is one fact per file under docs/memory/ with an index, changed in the same PR as the work. A nine-step setup and a failure-mode table, drawn from CooperBench and other sources, close it, with a warning that running several subscription accounts as one swarm is a grey zone after reported bans in February 2026.
- Key claims:
  - (reported) A redesigned Project belongs to one account and cannot be shared, so cross-account coordination must go through GitHub and Linear.
  - (inferred) The branch push is the claim lock: git accepts only the first push of a new branch, so two accounts cannot both claim one issue.
  - (inferred) Nothing first-party stays running and reachable by other accounts, so the coordinator must be event-driven with its continuity in repo files.
  - (unverified) GitHub sends no event when main advances and a PR starts to conflict, so a GitHub Action must comment on out-of-date PRs.
  - (reported) Protected branches on a private repository need a paid GitHub plan; on GitHub Free the repository has to be public.
  - (reported) Running several subscription accounts as one swarm is a grey zone: bans were reported in February 2026, unconfirmed by Anthropic.
  - (reported) In CooperBench two agents building separate features in one repo succeeded about 30% less often than one agent doing both.
  - (reported) A plan sent first nearly halved merge conflicts in CooperBench, which is why the claim comment lists files before any edit.
  - (reported) Locks alone left 64% duplicate work; locks plus a shared record of finished work brought it to 0% (synthetic agents).
  - (reported) Routines belong to one account; API fires are capped at 30 an hour per routine and 100 an hour per account, and events above the cap are dropped.
  - (unverified) Because the repo is the Project's only resource, its threads load the repo's hooks, so a SessionStart hook can run the fetch; with several repos the hooks are not loaded.
- Numbers (12 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 30 % less often: CooperBench: two agents on separate features succeed less often than one agent doing both
  - 29.4 vs 51.5 % conflicts: CooperBench merge conflicts with a plan sent first versus without
  - 64 % duplicates: duplicate work with locks alone in a synthetic-agent study; 0% with locks plus a shared record
  - 30 fires per hour per routine: routine API trigger cap (100 an hour per account)
  - 32 sessions: Remote Control server mode, each in its own worktree
  - 24 hours: idle time after which the coordinator releases a claim
  - 200 lines or 25 KB size limit: MEMORY.md index size loaded at start
  - 2 accounts: starting number of accounts; a third only when Linear has more independent issues than two can take
  - 3 accounts: the architecture figure's shared space
  - hourly schedule: routines run at most hourly; the coordinator's hourly run catches missed events within the hour
- Datasets: CooperBench (benchmark, cited); tools: cswap (Project export), Linear and the Linear connector, GitHub Actions, Claude GitHub App, routines, Remote Control, Agent teams, Claude Tag in Slack, Claude Code cloud threads and sessions, redesigned Projects, GitHub branch protection, CLAUDE.md and .claude/rules/, SessionStart hook in .claude/settings.json, .mcp.json, MCP Agent Mail (cited); models: none named.
- Decisions:
  - Build around the repository, not a shared Project; each account gets its own Project pointed at the same repo. (2026-10-08), the owner (author): A Project belongs to one account and cannot be shared, and no Claude product lets one account's agent talk live to another's.
  - Claim work by pushing a branch named after the issue, with a claim file in the first commit. (2026-10-08), the owner (author): Git accepts only the first push of a new branch, so two accounts cannot both claim one issue.
  - The coordinator is a routine on the main account, event-driven (PR events, pushes to main via an Action, hourly), with its picture kept in docs/agents/STATE.md. (2026-10-08), the owner (author): Nothing first-party stays running and reachable by other accounts.
  - Shared memory is docs/memory/ in the repo, one fact per file, changed in the same PR as the work; Project memory and auto memory stay private. (2026-10-08), the owner (author): The only memory every account sees is what is committed to the repo.
  - Every account signs in itself, only in Anthropic's own apps, at ordinary use; nothing scripts claude.ai; start with two accounts. (2026-10-08), the owner (author): Consumer Terms forbid sharing a login and automated access, and bans of several accounts on one device were reported.
  - Protect main: require a PR, a green CI check and branches up to date before merging. (2026-10-08), the owner (author): Refuses stale merges; needs a paid GitHub plan on a private repo.
- Open questions: Whether a comment posted by a bot wakes a cloud thread's auto-fix is not yet tested. / Whether several accounts on one device or IP set off the ban filter; the third-party guess is not confirmed by Anthropic. / Whether several Max accounts are within the terms rests on a quoted team member, not an official statement.
- Stale markers: Pro and Max beta only, not on Team or Enterprise yet / Whether a comment posted by a bot wakes auto-fix is not yet tested / not confirmed by Anthropic (February 2026 bans) / All opened on 2026-10-08 / Linear as the work queue (the batch's readme summary says this plan became Symphony) / a bug report says the autoMemoryDirectory setting was not respected

### 67. Moving Beethoven for VS Code onto hpcworker

- Folder: `symphony-template/artifacts/moving-beethoven-for-vs-code-onto-hpcworker-apbd6t`; account: [the owner's login]; link id: ApBd6TpHYnsWVKvP1NjvyX; docs, plan; date: 2026-10-07 (Header line "Oct 7, 2026 . @[the owner]" and the status note "On 2026-10-07 at 22:33Z [the owner] deferred the move";...); confidentiality: symphony-beethoven.
- Question: What would it take for Beethoven for VS Code to run its grid workers through the lab's shared hpcworker instead of its own grid scripts, and what must the owner decide before any code is written?
- Summary: A plan, deferred by the owner on 2026-10-07, for Beethoven for VS Code to stop shipping its own ten grid scripts and become a client of the lab's shared hpcworker on the Wayne State grid, as the browser and desktop editions already are. It reads both code bases at pinned commits (beethoven-vscode 631226e, hpcworker e77863c) and finds that starting, stopping, status, the log, the Claude sign-in folder and the Remote Control consent carry over, while hpcworker lacks four things VS Code relies on: the Ask request channel, per-worker sizes and GPUs, the Remote-SSH sysroot lines, and GitHub and Hugging Face tokens. It flags four things to know now, among them a credential path in VS Code 5.0.0 by which an unchecked result id can return the Claude credential file to the laptop's Claude, and a lab-access gap by which a VS Code student not on the access service's roster loses the lab folder at every sync. It sets out a side-by-side table of every grid feature, an eleven-step order of work across hpcworker, beethoven-sites, beethoven-vscode and the hub (framework 18 and 19, VS Code 6.0.0 and 7.0.0), what already set-up students keep, the risks, and fourteen decisions for the owner with a recommended answer each. Nothing on the grid is deleted at any step, and many behaviours are marked not yet measured.
- Key claims:
  - (inferred) The move is feasible but not a quick swap: hpcworker covers start, stop, status and log but lacks four things VS Code students rely on (Ask, worker sizes, Remote-SSH lines, tokens).
  - (reported) The owner deferred the move on 2026-10-07 because he is the only user of the VS Code edition and nothing else depends on it; VS Code keeps its grid scripts for now.
  - (inferred) VS Code 5.0.0 has a credential path: result and its relay check neither worker name nor request id, so the id ../../../claude/.credentials returns the student's Claude credential file to the laptop's Claude; found by reading, not run.
  - (measured) VS Code 5.0.0 lets the laptop's Claude ask for bypassPermissions, which the grid runs as claude -p --dangerously-skip-permissions; hpcworker's contract refuses that mode.
  - (measured) VS Code's grid/bootstrap.sbatch is the only tool that has ever built the image hpcworker runs at Wayne State, so someone must own rebuilding it before grid/ goes.
  - (measured) A VS Code student approved only through VS Code's own lab-access approval, and not on the access service's roster, loses the lab folder at every sync of tools/grid_access.sh, whether or not the move happens.
  - (measured) VS Code's login-node calls break hpcworker's etiquette: status --json makes 2N+1 or more squeue calls, wait, ask and relay are long-lived, and login_relay.sh's 2 s squeue loop is the only scheduler polling under 60 s found.
  - (measured) hpcworker has no GPU support of any kind: no --gpus, no --gres line, and HPCW_NV is read but never set.
  - (measured) hpcworker's resume window is 4 h (14400 s) in code, replacing seven days on purpose after Anthropic's statement; the seven days in its CLAUDE.md and DIAGNOSTICS.md is stale text.
  - (inferred) Which hpcworker framework version is live on the grid is not established: documents say 10 and 13, commits record 15 and 16, the desktop stand-in answers 16, and framework/VERSION is 17.
  - (measured) The VS Code worker job's cleanup trap never runs because the script ends in exec, so /dev/shm scratch is left behind unless the node removes it.
  - (measured) The Claude sign-in carries over without a new login because both editions use the same sign-in folder ~/.beethoven/claude and the same consent markers.
- Numbers (81 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 10 scripts: Grid scripts Beethoven for VS Code copies into each student's ~/.beethoven/bin
  - 9 grid-scripts version: Current VS Code grid-scripts version; missing reads as 0
  - 4 file checks per second: Ask's polling loop on the login node
  - 2 / 8 / 8 CPUs / GB / hours: hpcworker Wayne State default worker a moved student gets
  - 4 / 16 / 24 CPUs / GB / hours: VS Code's default worker today
  - 3 lines: Remote-SSH sysroot lines in ~/.bashrc for CentOS 7 compute nodes
  - 14 questions: Decisions for the owner, each with a recommended answer
  - 18 framework version: hpcworker release carrying the small additions
  - 19 framework version: hpcworker release carrying the Ask request channel
  - 6.0.0 VS Code release: Switch-over release with new terms

- Question: How does the owner upload Claude Swap Cloud to the Chrome Web Store for the first time, fill its listing, privacy, distribution and test tabs, register the bridge, set the five CWS_ secrets and the CWS_ON_PUSH switch, after moving the two repositories to OpenSpineConsortium?
- Summary: A step-by-step runbook for the owner, refreshed on 2026-10-08 for release 0.2.0.773 of the Claude Swap Cloud Chrome extension. Step 1, moving claude-swap and claude-swap-privacy to OpenSpineConsortium, was done on 2026-10-06 and the repository renamed claude-swap-cloud on 2026-10-07, so the owner starts at Step 2: create the store item from CI's zip (124,457 bytes), fill the twelve listing fields, the privacy tab (single purpose, per-permission justifications, remote code No, four data-usage boxes, PRIVACY.md version 15), distribution (Free, Unlisted, all regions) and test instructions (403 of 500 characters), then submit. Before submitting, cswap must be published on PyPI (pending publisher, tag v0.27.0b1) because the reviewer installs it with uv tool install. Step 6 registers the bridge with the store's item id on each computer, Step 7 creates the five CWS_ secrets (publisher id, item id, OAuth client id and secret, refresh token through the OAuth Playground), and Step 8 sets the variable CWS_ON_PUSH to upload, then publish, so each merge to main releases to the store. A closing table maps each refusal message to what to do.
