### 69. Claude Chat vs. Claude Code: Architectural Analysis of Interface-Exclusive Capabilities

- Folder: `symphony-template/artifacts/claude-chat-vs-claude-code-architectural-analysis-snuzfd`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: SnuzFDHCC9kM3GgYziMApb; docs, analysis; date: 2026-10-03 (Header line 'Oct 3, 2026 . @[the owner]'; closing section: 'Every product claim above was checked against Anthropic's...); confidentiality: symphony-beethoven.
- Question: What can Claude Chat do that Claude Code cannot, and the reverse, and how should the two surfaces be combined in one working procedure?
- Summary: A Docs-type analysis dated 3 October 2026 comparing Claude Chat (claude.ai/chat and the consumer apps) with Claude Code (one harness shipped through the CLI, the IDE extensions, cloud sessions at claude.ai/code, the GitHub Action and the Agent SDK). It first corrects the prompt's taxonomy: claude.ai/code is not the CLI, and three of the six features the prompt called exclusive (artifacts, vision, MCP) are shared, differing in ingestion path, scale and control plane rather than by a hard boundary. Chat's exclusives are the in-conversation preview runtime, server-side Project knowledge with retrieval mode and the hosted AI-powered artifact runtime billed to each viewer; Claude Code's are read/write/execute on the user's own machine, the terminal error loop against the real toolchain, hooks and permission rules, headless use and multi-session orchestration. It documents the agent loop, the tool set with its output limits, MCP transports, scopes and security, the data flow and retention by plan, and a set of workflow scenarios graded trivial, possible or impossible on each surface. It closes with a hybrid standard operating procedure (design and communicate in Chat; plan, build and verify in Claude Code; hand off through a committed spec, a pull request and a published page, with headless glue for recurring steps) and a sources table, flagging three statements as inferences rather than documented facts.
- Key claims:
  - (inferred) The two surfaces run the same models; the difference is the harness, the tools and where the execution and trust boundary sits.
  - (reported) claude.ai/code is not the CLI: Claude Code is one harness shipped through several front-ends, and claude.ai/code is the web surface for cloud sessions, Remote Control, Projects and Routines.
  - (inferred) Three of the six features the prompt labelled exclusive (artifacts, vision, MCP) are shared between the surfaces.
  - (reported) Artifacts are two systems: the chat-only in-conversation preview runtime and the shared published page, which Claude Code can write to since June 2026.
  - (reported) An artifact can reach only Anthropic's API, proxied by claude.ai and billed to the viewer's subscription; third-party endpoints are blocked by the CSP and external data comes through connectors.
  - (reported) Claude Code's Artifact tool arrived in v2.1.183 with stricter conditions than Chat: a paid plan, a /login session, the Anthropic API as provider and no CMEK, HIPAA or Zero Data Retention policy.
  - (reported) A Chat Project is server-side grounding that switches to retrieval mode near the context window; Claude Code's equivalent is the CLAUDE.md hierarchy plus agentic search over the repository.
  - (reported) Claude Code Projects at claude.ai/code are a work organizer of parallel task threads, unrelated to Chat Projects' knowledge base.
  - (reported) Vision is a property of the model, not of the chat UI; the surfaces differ in how pixels enter the request and at what scale, and bulk image work belongs in a script with Read as the spot-check.
  - (reported) On macOS, Linux and WSL the dedicated Glob and Grep tools are left out of the default set; find and grep through Bash run embedded bfs and ugrep binaries.
  - (reported) Chat's code-execution container is isolated, egress-limited and reset between tasks: a calculator, not a workstation.
  - (unverified) The write-run-read-fix loop fails when the model stops learning from the oracle; six named patterns (oscillation, test tampering, phantom API, wrong file, stale context, permission thrash).
- Numbers (53 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 20 files per chat: Chat's upload cap per conversation
  - 500 MB per file: Chat's upload size cap
  - 8000 x 8000 px per image: Chat's image dimension cap
  - 30 MB per file: Chat Project knowledge file cap, unlimited count
  - about 10x capacity multiplier: Gain from a Project's retrieval mode over fitting the corpus in context
  - about 1,568 px long edge: Downscale bound of the vision encoder
  - roughly 1.15 megapixels: Vision encoder resolution bound
  - (width x height) / 750 tokens per image: Approximate image token cost formula
  - about 1,500 tokens per screenshot: Cost of a full-size screenshot (marked an inference derived from the ratio)
  - 1000 x 1000 px: Recommended minimum image size
- Datasets: none named; tools: Claude Code CLI, VS Code and JetBrains extensions, Claude desktop app (Code tab), Claude Code cloud sessions and Remote Control, GitHub Action and GitHub App (@claude), Agent SDK, Routines (/schedule), MCP (stdio, Streamable HTTP, SSE), Connectors (Google Drive, Gmail, Calendar, Slack, Linear, Notion), claude_desktop_config.json and .mcpb bundles, Artifact tool and /design canvas, ToolSearch, cdnjs, jsDelivr, Tailwind play CDN, jQuery CDN, Google Fonts, React, lucide-react, recharts, d3, three (r128), plotly, chart.js, mathjs, lodash, papaparse, SheetJS, shadcn/ui, tone, mammoth, tensorflow, Mermaid, ripgrep, bfs, ugrep, uv, pytest, ruff, mypy, jq, gh, @bytebase/dbhub, Playwright MCP, ImageMagick, OpenCV, PIL, Seatbelt, bubblewrap, socat, Bedrock, Vertex, Foundry, OpenTelemetry, Compliance API and audit log; models: claude-sonnet-4-6 (in the artifact API code example).

### 70. Claude Code on a Slurm + Apptainer Cluster with a Consumer Subscription: Implementation Playbook

- Folder: `symphony-template/artifacts/claude-code-on-a-slurm-apptainer-cluster-7dbjwc`; account: gregoryamdg; link id: 7dBJwcSiDjUFrAKSJfFhEF; docs, playbook; date: 2026-10-03 (Header line under the title reads "Oct 3, 2026" followed by the author's byline; the closing section says "Facts abou...); confidentiality: symphony-beethoven.
- Question: How can one person run Claude Code on a shared Slurm cluster inside an Apptainer image, authenticated by their own consumer subscription, within Anthropic's terms, with safe credentials, hard guardrails and batch runs that resume across job windows?
- Summary: A dated implementation playbook for running Claude Code on a shared Slurm cluster inside a read-only Apptainer image, authenticated by one person's consumer (Pro or Max) subscription. It fixes four compliance conditions (one user one account, unmodified binary, no intermediation, no limit evasion), keeps regulated data off a consumer plan, and gives complete scripts: site discovery into site.env, a container definition that pins the Claude Code binary, relocation of all Claude Code state to scratch, a Slurm client passthrough with a submit-shim fallback, a launcher (claude-hpc), and two credentials (a one-year setup token for batch, a /login credential for interactive and Remote Control sessions). Guardrails are three layers: managed settings baked into the image (deny rules, timeouts, a 16G tool memory cap, sandbox off), a user settings allow list, and a PreToolUse guard hook; a CLAUDE.md blueprint, a slurm-operator subagent and a slurm-submit skill enforce cluster etiquette (job caps, measured resource requests, no sbatch --wrap). Three execution patterns are scripted: an interactive srun allocation, a persistent tmux workspace running claude remote-control, and a resumable claude -p pipeline that sizes itself to the Slurm window, resumes by session id and turns a usage-limit hit into a delayed resubmission. It closes with a version table of gated features, a source table of Anthropic documentation, and names two inferences to validate on site.
- Key claims:
  - (reported) The deployment is permitted under Anthropic's terms as long as four conditions hold: one person's subscription authenticates only that person's sessions, the binary runs unmodified, no one else's work is routed through the account, and nothing evades usage limits.
  - (reported) Anthropic's documentation describes the claude setup-token credential as intended for CI and non-interactive environments.
  - (unverified) Most compute nodes cannot reach the internet directly, and Claude Code needs api.anthropic.com on every turn, so jobs must run on a partition with egress or through the site proxy.
  - (reported) CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN in the environment takes precedence over .credentials.json, which lets the launcher choose the credential by injecting or omitting the token file.
  - (reported) A headless -p run started after the /login access token has expired has been reported not to refresh it, which is why batch runs use the setup token.
  - (reported) The setup token is valid for one year and can only make model requests; it cannot open Remote Control or use claude.ai connectors.
  - (reported) Subscription limits are per account, so parallel sessions on many nodes draw down one budget proportionally faster; the structure is a rolling five-hour window plus weekly caps.
  - (reported) Rotating accounts or pooling tokens to widen the usage window is prohibited by Anthropic's terms; a workload that needs more than one subscription's window belongs on an API key.
  - (reported) Deny rules in managed settings are evaluated before ask and allow rules and cannot be re-enabled by user or project files; a Read deny also blocks Edit and Write on the same path.
  - (inferred) Claude Code's bubblewrap sandbox cannot normally nest inside an unprivileged Apptainer, so the sandbox is off and the container is the isolation layer.
  - (unverified) Remote Control started with only the setup token fails; it needs the /login credential.
  - (inferred) The Slurm client passthrough and the nested-sandbox option are inferences to validate on each cluster, not documented behaviour.
- Numbers (75 recorded; the first 10 here):
  - 4 conditions: compliance conditions that keep the deployment permitted
  - 1 year: lifetime of the claude setup-token credential
  - 0600 file mode: permission on the user's token file on scratch
  - 64 MiB: session tmpfs Apptainer gives when /tmp is not bound
  - hundreds MB: expected growth of transcripts after a month of batch runs
  - 2.1.271 Claude Code version: example pinned version in the build command
  - 22 Node major version: Node version installed in the image
  - 1.2+ Apptainer version: minimum Apptainer for %arguments in the definition file
  - 9 Rocky Linux release: base image of the container
  - 24.04 Ubuntu release: alternative base for Ubuntu hosts
- Datasets: none named; tools: Claude Code (claude, claude setup-token, /login, /usage, /agents, claude -p, claude remote-control, --session-id, --resume, --max-turns, --permission-mode dontAsk, --output-format stream-json), Slurm (sbatch, squeue, sacct, scontrol, scancel, sinfo, srun, salloc, sstat, sshare, sprio, seff, sacctmgr), Apptainer / Singularity (SIF, --containall, --cleanenv, --no-home, --nv, --fakeroot, %arguments), munge (socket bound into the container), tmux and screen, jq, curl, git, Node.js, npm, npx (nodesource setup), uv, pip, ripgrep, rsync, GNU timeout, uuidgen, cron, bubblewrap sandbox (disabled), MCP servers, Remote Control, claude.ai/code and the Claude mobile app, pytest, ruff, mypy, black, make, cmake, ninja (allow-listed), snakemake, nextflow (examples of project-level allow rules), jsonschema (example task), Rocky Linux 9 / Ubuntu 24.04 base images, dnf, managed-settings.json, settings.json, PreToolUse hooks, CLAUDE.md, subagents, skills; models: sonnet (default model in user settings, the slurm-operator subagent and batch runs), Opus-class models (reserved for plan mode).
- Decisions:
  - Batch and non-interactive runs authenticate with the one-year setup token; interactive and Remote Control sessions use a full /login credential., the author: a headless -p run started after the access token has expired has been reported not to refresh it (issue #50743), and /login is the only credential that can o...
  - The Claude Code bubblewrap sandbox is switched off in managed settings; the Apptainer container is the isolation layer., the author: the bubblewrap sandbox cannot normally nest inside an unprivileged Apptainer
  - One account per user, no account rotation, no pooled tokens; a workload needing more than one subscription's window goes to an API key., the author: rotating accounts or pooling tokens to widen the window is the behaviour Anthropic's terms prohibit
  - Regulated data (PHI, export-controlled, GDPR special categories) stays off a consumer plan; use an API key, a Team or Enterprise seat, or a cloud-provider route for that work., the author: on Free, Pro and Max plans the account's Help improve Claude setting decides whether coding sessions are used for training
  - The image contains no credentials; each user mints their own token on their laptop and stores it 0600 on their own scratch, never in .bashrc, a job script, an sbatch --export line or a module file., the author: the token is a bearer credential for the whole subscription and one user, one account must stay mechanically true on a shared cluster
  - Default to --model sonnet and medium effort for implementation runs; reserve Opus-class models for plan mode., the author: in plan mode turns are few and reasoning matters; subscription limits are drawn per token per turn
  - Only the slurm-operator subagent submits, monitors or cancels jobs; other subagents never call sbatch, srun, salloc or scancel., the author: the guard hook, the permission rules and the instructions then apply at a single point, and an implementer subagent cannot queue jobs as a side effect of tes...
  - Non-interactive runs add --permission-mode dontAsk., the author: it turns every would-be prompt into a denial the model can see and route around instead of a hang
  - Host Slurm binaries are bound into /opt/slurm-bin rather than over /usr/bin., the author: so the container's own tools are never shadowed
  - The image base is rockylinux:9., the author: it matches the RHEL family most university clusters run, so host Slurm binaries bound in load against a compatible glibc
- Open questions: Whether the Slurm client passthrough works on a given site depends on its library layout and the host-to-image glibc relationship; claude-hpc --selftest exer... / Whether enableWeakerNestedSandbox behaves under Apptainer at all, since it is documented for Docker. / How a leaked setup token is revoked, since Anthropic's documentation describes generating one but not revoking it. / Whether the site's security policy allows binding the munge socket into containers, which decides passthrough versus the submit shim. / Whether the partition-name heuristics in site-discover.sh match the site's partition names. / Whether the site's Apptainer permits unprivileged user namespaces, which would allow a second sandbox layer. / Whether the pinned Claude Code build carries every version-gated feature the playbook uses.
